Theory of weights for log convergent cohomologies II: the case of a proper SNCL scheme in characteristic p > 0 p>0
Yukiyoshi Nakkajima

Abstract




{\rm ABSTRACT}

For a flat
p
-adic formal family
S
of log points
over a complete discrete valuation ring

{\cal V}
with perfect residue field of mixed characteristics
(0,p)
and
for a simple normal crossing log scheme
X
over an exact closed
log subscheme of
S
defined
by a nonzero element
\pi
of the maximal ideal of
{\cal V}
,
we construct fundamental filtered complexes

(A_{\rm conv}(X/S),P)
and
(A^{N}_{\rm conv}(X/S),P)

in the convergent topos
(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}

of
\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}

(not in the log convergent topos
(X/S)_{\rm conv}
of
X/S
)
by using
(X/S)_{\rm conv}
.
We prove that
there exists a canonical isomorphism
(A^{N}_{\rm conv}(X/S),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(A_{\rm conv}(X/S),P)
.
Because the complex
A_{\rm conv}(X/S)
is shown to calculate
the log convergent cohomology sheaf of

X/S
, the filtered complex
(A_{\rm conv}(X/S),P)
produces
the weight filtration on the log convergent cohomology sheaf
if
\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}
is proper.
The filtered complex
(A_{\rm conv}(X/S),P)

is a
p
-adic analogue of a generalization of Steenbrink-Fujisawa-Nakayama’s
\infty
-adic filtered complex.
The filtered complex
(A^{N}_{\rm conv}(X/S),P)

is a
p
-adic analogue of a generalization of Rapoport-Zink-Nakayama’s
l
-adic filtered complex
and Steenbrink-Zucker-Fujisawa-Nakayama’s
\infty
-adic filtered complex.
We give a comparison theorem between
the projection of
(A_{\rm conv}(X/S),P)

in the Zariski topos
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\rm zar}
of
\overset{\circ}{X}

and the isozariskian weight-filtered complex

(A_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

of
X/(S,p{\cal O}_{S},[~])
constructed in
[
Nakk4
]
,
where
[~]
is the canonical PD-structure on
p{\cal O}_{S}
.

-filtered convergent complexes and

Introduction

This paper is a continuation of
[
Nakk4
]
and
a simple normal crossing log version of
[
NS2
]
.

Inspired by the Weil conjecture and theory of Hodge-Deligne, A. Grothendieck
has proposed the motivic theory of weights which realizes
the theories of weights on various cohomologies of algebraic varieties.
Supported by his philosophy, in this paper
we construct the
p
-adic weight spectral sequence of
a proper SNCL(=simple normal crossing log) scheme over a
p
-adic family of log points
in a way which is philosophically similar (but technically non-similar)
to Rapoport-Zink-Nakyama’s construction
of the
l
-adic weight spectral sequence of
a proper SNCL variety over a log point in
[
RZ
]
and
[
Nak
]
.
In Rapoport-Zink’s construction, the canonical filtration on a certain complex
has an important role; in our construction,
the canonical filtration on another complex has an important role.
Our construction uses the theory of (log) convergent topoi in

[
O2
]
,
[
O3
]
,
[
Sh1
]
,
[
Sh2
]
,
[
Sh3
]
and
[
NS2
]
.
The use of the theory of the (log) convergent topoi is definitive because the construction
using the theory of (log) crystalline topoi in
[
B1
]

and
[
Ka
]
does not enable us to obtain the
p
-adic version of Rapoport-Zink’s construction
because the
p
-adic purity for the vanishing cycle sheaf obtained
by the use of the morphism forgetting the log structure of a smooth scheme with
an SNCD(=simple normal crossing divisor)
does
not
hold in the theory of the log crystalline topoi (
[
NS1
]
). On the other hand,
we have proved the
p
-adic purity for the vanishing cycle sheaf of the log scheme
in the log convergent topoi (
[
NS2
]
).
(We shall review these in this Introduction.)

In order to explain our construction in more details,
we recall results in
[
Nakk4
]
and
[
NS2
]
. First we recall results in
[
Nakk4
]
,
which are ideal generalizations of results in
[
Mo
]
(see also
[
Nakk1
]
).

For a ringed topos
({\cal T},{\cal A})
, let

{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal A})
and
D^{+}({\cal A})
be the derived category of
bounded below filtered complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules and the derived category of
bounded below complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules, respectively.

For a log (formal) scheme
Y
, let
\overset{\circ}{Y}
be the
underlying (formal) scheme of
Y
.
Let
S
be a
p
-adic formal family of log points defined in
[
Nakk4
]
;
locally on
S
,
S
is isomorphic to a log
p
-adic formal scheme

(\overset{\circ}{S},{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{S})
,
where the morphism
{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{S}

is defined by the morphism

(n,a)\longmapsto 0^{n}a

(n\in{\mathbb{N}},a\in{\cal O}_{S}^{*})
, where
0^{n}=0\in{\cal O}_{S}

for
n\not=0
and
0^{0}:=1\in{\cal O}_{S}
.
Assume that the
p
-adic formal scheme

\overset{\circ}{S}
is flat over
{\rm Spf}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
.
Let
(S,{\cal I},\gamma)
be a
p
-adic formal PD-family of log points
(
S
is a
p
-adic formal family of log points and

{\cal I}
is a quasi-coherent
p
-adic PD-ideal sheaf of
{\cal O}_{S}

with PD-structure
\gamma
).
Let
S_{0}
be an exact closed log subscheme of
S
defined by
{\cal I}
.
Let
X/S_{0}
be a (not necessarily proper)
SNCL scheme with structural morphism

f\colon X\longrightarrow S_{0}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S
.
Let
\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
be
the set of smooth components of
X/S_{0}
defined in
[
Nakk4
]
.
(When
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
is the spectrum of a field of characteristic
p>0
,

\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
can be taken as
the set of the irreducible components of
\overset{\circ}{X}
.)
For a nonnegative integer
k
, let

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}:=\coprod_{\{\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{k}\}~|\lambda_{i}\in\Lambda,\lambda_{i}\not=\lambda_{j}(i\not=j)\}}\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{0}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{k}}

be a scheme over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
well-defined in
[
Nakk4
]
.
Let
a^{(k)}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}
be the natural morphism.
Let
\overset{\circ}{f}{}^{(k)}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
be the structural morphism.
Let
\epsilon_{X/S}\colon X\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}
be a morphism forgetting the log structure of
X

over a morphism
S\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
forgetting the log structure of
S
.
Let
\epsilon^{\rm crys}_{X/S}\colon(X/S)_{\rm crys}\longrightarrow(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm crys}

be a morphism of (log) crystalline topoi induced by
\epsilon_{X/S}
.
For fine log schemes
Z
over
S_{0}
and
W
over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
,
let
u^{\rm crys}_{Z/S}\colon(Z/S)_{\rm crys}\longrightarrow Z_{\rm zar}
and

u^{\rm crys}_{W/\overset{\circ}{S}}\colon(W/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm crys}\longrightarrow W_{\rm zar}
be the canonical projections, respectively.
Let
E
be a flat quasi-coherent crystal of
{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
-modules.
In
[
Nakk4
]
we have constructed a filtered complex

(A_{\rm zar}(X/S,E),P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))

such that there exist the following canonical isomorphisms

(1.0.1)

\displaystyle\theta\colon Ru^{\rm crys}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm crys}*}_{X/S}(E))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}A_{\rm zar}(X/S,E)

and

(1.0.2)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}A_{\rm zar}(X/S,E)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}

\displaystyle a^{(2j+k)}_{*}(Ru^{\rm crys}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(a^{(2j+k)*}_{{\rm crys}}(E))

\displaystyle\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))[-2j-k]

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
.
Here
\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(m)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})

(m\in{\mathbb{N}})

is the crystalline orientation sheaf associated to
the set
\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
. That is,

\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(m)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})
is
the extension to
(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(m)}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm crys}

of the direct sum of

\overset{m}{\bigwedge}{\mathbb{Z}}^{E}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{0}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{m}}}

in the Zariski topos
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(m)}_{\rm zar}
of
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(m)}

for the subsets
E=\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{0}},\ldots,\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{m}}\}
’s of

\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
.
We call the filtered complex
(A_{\rm zar}(X/S,E),P)
the

zariskian Steenbrink complex
of
E
.
When
E
is trivial, we call

(A_{\rm zar}(X/S,E),P)
the
zariskian Steenbrink complex
of
X/S
.
If
E
is an
F
-crystal on the crystalline site
{\rm Crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})

of
\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}
,
then the isomorphism (
1.0.2
) becomes the following isomorphism

(1.0.3)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}A_{\rm zar}(X/S,E)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}

\displaystyle a^{(2j+k)}_{*}(Ru^{\rm crys}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(a^{(2j+k)*}_{{\rm crys}}(E))

\displaystyle\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))(-j-k)[-2j-k].

One may think that
(-j-k)
means the usual Tate twist
and that there arises no new problem. However this is not so.
Let
F_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}\colon\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}

be the absolute Frobenius endomorphism
of
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
.
First we have to consider the relative Frobenius morphism

F_{S_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}\colon S_{0}\longrightarrow S_{0}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0},F_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}}\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}=:S^{[p]}_{0}

of
S_{0}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
as in
[
O3
]
.
Then we have to consider a new fine log formal scheme

S_{0}^{[p]}(S)
, which has been considered
in
[
Nakk4
]
as a special case.
The log formal scheme
S_{0}^{[p]}(S)
is, by definition, as follows:
the underlying formal scheme of

S_{0}^{[p]}(S)
is
\overset{\circ}{S}
and the log structure of

S_{0}^{[p]}(S)
is the sub-log structure of
S

such that the isomorphism

M_{S}/{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}M_{S_{0}}/{\cal O}_{S_{0}}^{*}
induces
the following isomorphism

(1.0.4)

\displaystyle M_{S_{0}^{[p]}(S)}/{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm Im}(F_{S_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}^{*}\colon F_{S_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}^{*}(M_{S_{0}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0},F_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}}\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}})\longrightarrow M_{S_{0}})/{\cal O}_{S_{0}}^{*}.

Then we have an obvious morphism
(S,{\cal I},\gamma)\longrightarrow(S_{0}^{[p]}(S),{\cal I},\gamma)
of log PD-formal schemes.
Now the Tate-twist
(-j-k)
means the Tate twist
with respect to
the morphism
X\longrightarrow X\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0},F_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}}\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}

induced by the absolute Frobenius endomorphism
F_{X}\colon X\longrightarrow X
of
X

over
(S,{\cal I},\gamma)\longrightarrow(S_{0}^{[p]}(S),{\cal I},\gamma)
.
Note also that the actions of the Frobenius endomorphisms
on
p
-adic Steenbrink complexes and the
p
-adic weight spectral sequences
of proper SNCL schemes over the log point of
a perfect field of characteristic
p>0
in some contexts
are mistaken in published papers
except
[
Nakk1
]
,
[
Ma
]
and
[
Nakk4
]
(as far as I know)
because the actions of the Frobenii on the non-zero local section “
d\log t
”
are not considered in the
p
-adic Steenbrink double complexes in almost all papers,
where
t
is a “local log parameter” of the log point.
As a consequence of (
1.0.1
) and (
1.0.3
),
we obtain the following spectral sequence:

(1.0.5)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}R^{q-2j-k}f^{\rm crys}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(a^{(2j+k)*}_{{\rm crys}}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})))(-j-k)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow R^{q}f^{\rm crys}_{X/S*}(E)

if
E
is an
F
-crystal on
{\rm Crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})
,
where
f^{\rm crys}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}:=\overset{\circ}{f}{}^{(2j+k)}\circ u^{\rm crys}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

and
f^{\rm crys}_{X/S}:=f\circ u^{\rm crys}_{X/S}
.
In
[
Nakk4
]
we have called this spectral sequence
the
p
-adic weight spectral sequence of
X/S

in the case where
\overset{\circ}{f}
is proper and
E
is trivial.

Next we recall main results in
[
NS2
]
. This motivates us to
gives a nice technical framework in this paper.

Let
{\cal V}
be a complete discrete valuation ring
of mixed characteristics
(0,p)

whose residue field
\kappa
is perfect.
Let
\pi
be a nonzero element of the maximal ideal of
{\cal V}
.
Let
S
be a
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme in the sense of
[
O1
]
,
that is, a noetherian formal scheme over
{\rm Spf}({\cal V})

with the
p
-adic topology which is topologically of finite type
over
{\rm Spf}({\cal V})
.
Let
S_{1}
be a closed subscheme of
S
defined by
the ideal sheaf
\pi{\cal O}_{S}
of
{\cal O}_{S}
.
Let
Y
be a smooth scheme over
S_{1}
and
let
D
be a relative SNCD(=simple normal crossing divisor)
on
Y/S_{1}
.
Stimulated by
[
Ka
]
and
[
Fa
]
,
we have constructed an
fs(=fine and saturated) log structure

M(D)
on
Y_{\rm zar}

with morphism
M(D)\longrightarrow({\cal O}_{Y},*)
in
[
NS1
]
.
If there exists the following cartesian diagram

\begin{CD}D\cap V@>{\subset}>{}>V\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\underline{\rm Spec}_{S_{1}}({\cal O}_{S_{1}}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{d}]/(x_{1}\cdots x_{a}))@>{\subset}>{}>\underline{\rm Spec}_{S_{1}}({\cal O}_{S_{1}}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{d}])\end{CD}

(
a,d\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}

a\leq d
)
for an open subscheme
V
of
Y
,
where the right vertical morphism
in the diagram above is étale,
then

(M(D)|_{V}\longrightarrow({\cal O}_{V},*))=(({\cal O}_{V}^{*}x_{1}^{\mathbb{N}}\cdots x_{a}^{\mathbb{N}},*)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}({\cal O}_{V},*)).

We denote the log scheme
(Y,M(D))
simply by
(Y,D)
.

Let
g\colon Y\longrightarrow S_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S
be the structural morphism.
Let
({Y/S})_{\rm conv}
be the convergent topos of
Y/S
defined in
[
NS2
]
,
which is the relative version of the convergent topos in
[
O2
]
.
Let
{\cal K}_{Y/S}
be the isostructure sheaf in
({Y/S})_{\rm conv}
.
Let
({(Y,D)/S})_{\rm conv}(=({(Y,D)/S})_{{\rm conv},{\rm zar}})
be the log convergent topos
of
(Y,D)/S
which is the relative version of
the log convergent topos in
[
Sh2
]
.
Let

\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(Y,D)/S}\colon(({(Y,D)/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(Y,D)/S})\longrightarrow(({Y/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S})

be a morphism of ringed topoi forgetting the log structure
M(D)
.
Set
{\cal K}_{S}:={\cal O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Let

u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}\colon(({Y/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S})\longrightarrow({Y}_{\rm zar},g^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))

be the canonical projection.
In
[
NS2
]
we have constructed
the following filtered complex

(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(Y,D)/S}),P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y/S}).

If there exists an immersion
(Y,D)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}

into a log smooth scheme over
S
,
then

(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(Y,D)/S}),P)=(L^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),\{L^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/S}(P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}})\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y/S}),

where
{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
is the exactification of the immersion

(Y,D)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}
and

\{P_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
is the usual filtration on
\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S}

obtained by counting the numbers of log poles of local sections of

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S}

and
L^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/S}
is the convergent linearization functor for
coherent
{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
-modules with respect to
the immersion
Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}
over
S
.
We have proved that the natural morphism

L^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/S}(P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\longrightarrow L^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})

is indeed injective in
[
NS2
]
, while we have proved that
the integral log crystalline analogue of this morphism is
not
injective in
[
NS1
]
.
Hence we cannot consider the integral log crystalline analogue
“
(C_{\rm crys}({\cal O}_{(Y,D)/S}),P)
” of

(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(Y,D)/S}),P)
.

In
[
NS2
]
we have proved the following three theorems:

Theorem 1.1

(
p
-adic purity).

Let
\{D_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
be the set of the smooth components of
D/S_{1}
defined in

[
NS1
]
.
For a nonnegative integer
k
, set

D^{(k)}:=\coprod_{\{\{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{k}\}~|\lambda_{i}\in\Lambda,\lambda_{i}\not=\lambda_{j}(i\not=j)\}}D_{\lambda_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap D_{\lambda_{k}}

be a scheme over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
well-defined in
[
NS1
]
.
Let
b^{(k)}\colon D^{(k)}\longrightarrow Y
be the natural morphism.
Then there exists a canonical isomorphism

(1.1.1)

R^{k}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(Y,D)/S*}({\cal K}_{(Y,D)/S})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}b^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{D^{(k)}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(D/S))\quad(k\in{\mathbb{N}}),

where
\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(D/S)
is the convergent
orientation sheaf associated to the set
\{D_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
.

On the other hand, in
[
NS1
]
we have proved that

R^{k}\epsilon^{\rm crys}_{(Y,D)/S*}({\cal O}_{(Y,D)/S})\not=b^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal O}_{D^{(k)}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm crys}(D/S))\quad(k\in{\mathbb{N}})

in general in the case where
\pi{\cal O}_{S}
has a PD-structure.
Here
\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm crys}(D/S)
is the crystalline
orientation sheaf associated to the set
\{D_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
.

Theorem 1.2

(
Comparison theorem between
(C_{\rm conv},P)

and
(C_{\rm conv},\tau)
).

Let
\tau
be the canonical filtration on a complex.
Then the following canonical morphism

(1.2.1)

(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(Y,D)/S}),\tau){\longrightarrow}(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(Y,D)/S}),P)

is an isomorphism.

Set

(C_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}({\cal K}_{(Y,D)/S}),P):=Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}((C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(Y,D)/S}),P))\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(g^{-1}({\cal K}_{S})).

Theorem 1.3

(
Comparison theorem between
(C_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar},P)

and
(C_{\rm zar},P)\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
).

Let
(C_{\rm zar}({\cal O}_{(Y,D)/S}),P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(g^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
be the filtered complex
constructed in
[
NS1
]
.
Then there exists a canonical isomorphism

(1.3.1)

(C_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}({\cal K}_{(Y,D)/S}),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(C_{\rm zar}({\cal O}_{(Y,D)/S}),P)\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}.

Consequently

(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(Y,D)/S}),P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y/S})

is an “object above”

(C_{\rm zar}({\cal O}_{(Y,D)/S}),P)\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(g^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))
.

(
The filtered complex
(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(Y,D)/S}),P)

lives in a “higher world”
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y/S})

than
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(g^{-1}({\cal K}_{S})).)

Now we can state the main results in this paper as follows.

Let
{\cal V}
be as above.
Let
S
be a
p
-adic formal family of log points over
{\cal V}
.
Let
X/S_{1}
be an SNCL scheme
and let
f\colon X\longrightarrow S_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S
be
the structural morphism.
Let
{\cal K}_{X/S}
and
{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

be the isostructure sheaves in
({X/S})_{\rm conv}
and

({\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}})_{\rm conv}
, respectively.
Let
E
be an isocrystal in
(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}
.
(In this paper an isocrystal is always assumed to be coherent as in
[
B2
]
.)
In this paper we construct the following two filtered complexes

(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),

(A^{N}_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}).

(We prove that these filtered complexes are canonically isomorphic.
The notation
N
in the latter complex stands for the notation of
the “logarithm of the local monodromy”.)
We also define the following filtered complex by using the projection

u^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\colon((\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\longrightarrow(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))
:

(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,E),P):=Ru^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}((A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P))\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S})).

We call the filtered complexes
(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
and

(A^{N}_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
the

convergent Steenbrink complex
of
E
and
the

convergent Rapoport-Zink-Steenbrink-Zucker complex
of
E
, respectively.
We call
(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,E),P)

the

isozariskian Steenbrink complex
of
E
.
When
E
is trivial, we call

(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
,
(A^{N}_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)

and
(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,E),P)

the
convergent Steenbrink complex
of
X/S
,
the
convergent Rapoport-Zink-Steenbrink-Zucker complex
of
X/S

and the
isozariskian Steenbrink complex
of
X/S
, respectively.

The filtered complex

(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,E),P)
is a generalization of the filtered complex
in
[
GK
]
by the use of the different language in the case
where
\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
is proper,
though the key notion “the derived category of bounded below filtered complexes” does not appear
in [loc. cit.].
(Consequently the filtered complex nor the the weight spectral sequence in [loc. cit.]
is not well-defined.)
Usually the
p
-adic filtered Steenbrink complexes are defined
as filtered complexes in the Zariski topos

\overset{\circ}{X}_{\rm zar}

(e.g.,
[
Mo
]
,
[
GK
]
,
[
Nakk1
]
,
[
Nakk4
]
).
In this paper we define fundamental filtered complexes
in a “higher” category
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

than the category
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))
as in
[
NS2
]
.
(I have learnt this from A. Shiho.)
This formulation enables us to obtain a kind of the
p
-adic “semi-purity”
(see (
1.5
) below for this).

Let
\epsilon_{X/S}\colon X\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}
be a morphism
forgetting the log structure of
X
over the morphism

S\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
forgetting the log structure of
S
.
Let
\epsilon_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}\colon X\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}

be a morphism forgetting the log structure of
X

over
\overset{\circ}{S}
(not over
S\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
).
Let

\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S}\colon((X/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{X/S})\longrightarrow((\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

and

\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}\colon((X/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}})\longrightarrow((\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

be the morphisms of ringed topoi induced by
\epsilon_{X/S}
and

\epsilon_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}
, respectively.
To construct the filtered complex
(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
,
we construct a key fundamental filtered complex

(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}).

(The underlying complex

\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))

is different from the usual complex

R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))
.)
The filtered complex

(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),P)

is a new complex in our theory
which does not appear in the theory in
[
NS2
]
.
We call this filtered complex the
modified
P
-filtered convergent complex

of
\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)
.
To define the complex

\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))
,
we need a slightly careful argument for defining the differentials of

\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))

because the differential is not directly obtained in a general framework.
We prove that
the complex
\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))

fits into the following triangle:

(1.3.2)

\displaystyle R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E))[-1]\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))\longrightarrow R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E))\overset{+1}{\longrightarrow}

in
D^{+}({\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}})
.
(See §
8
for the precise definition of

(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),P)
.)

In the text we calculate the graded complex of

(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),P)

as follows:

Proposition 1.4
.

Let
k
be a nonnegative integer.
Then

(1.4.1)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a^{(k-1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(k-1)*}_{{\rm conv}*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))[-k]&(k>0),\\
{\rm Ker}(a^{(0)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(0)*}_{{\rm conv}*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\longrightarrow a^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(1)*}_{{\rm conv}*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})))&(k=0)\end{cases}

in
D^{+}({\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}})
,
where
\varpi^{(*)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})
is the convergent
orientation sheaf associated to the set
\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
of
the smooth components of
X/S_{1}
.

As a corollary of (
1.4
),
we prove the following, which we call the
p
-adic
semi-purity
of

(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),P)
:

Theorem 1.5

(
p
-adic semi-purity
)
.

The filtration
P
is equal to the canonical filtration
\tau
on

\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))
.
That is,

(1.5.1)

\displaystyle(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),\tau)=(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),P)

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}})
.
Consequently

\displaystyle{\cal H}^{k}(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)))

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a^{(k-1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(k-1)*}_{{\rm conv}*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})))&(k>0),\\
{\rm Ker}(a^{(0)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(0)*}_{{\rm conv}*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\longrightarrow a^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(1)*}_{{\rm conv}*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})))&(k=0)\end{cases}

for a nonnegative integer
k
.

It is not difficult to prove that

\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))

is contravariantly functorial for any morphism of SNCL schemes
X/S
’s.
As a corollary of (
1.5
), it is immediate to see that

(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),P)

is also contravariantly functorial.

By (
1.3.2
) we obtain the following composite morphism

\theta\colon\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))\longrightarrow R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E))\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))[1]

of complexes.
Set
H:=\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))
.
Then we define the filtered complex
(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
as follows.
First we set

\displaystyle A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E):=s(H/\tau_{0}[1]\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}H/\tau_{1}[1]\{1\}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}H/\tau_{j}[1]\{j\}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots),

where
s
means the single complex of the double complex
and
[1]
means the
1
-shift on the left of the complex with the change of the signs
of the differential morphisms as usual and
\{j\}
means
the
j
-shift on the left of the complex
without
the change of the signs
of the differential morphisms unusually.
Note that our definition of a double complex is
not
a traditional one;
we demand the anti-commutative diagram for the definition of the double complex
instead of the commutative diagram for the definition of the double complex.
(Strictly speaking, to define
A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)
, we have to take a representative of

H
.)
Then we define the filtration
P:=\{P_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
on
A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)
as follows:

\displaystyle P_{k}A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E):=s(\cdots\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\tau_{\max\{2j+k+1,j\}}H/\tau_{j}[1]\{j\}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots)\quad(k\in{\mathbb{Z}},j\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Because we use the convergent topos
(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}

and because we prove the
p
-adic semi-purity (
1.5
),
we can define a
p
-adic analogue of Steenbrink-Fujisawa-Nakayama’s
\infty
-adic filtered complex
in a manner of the definition of Rapoport-Zink-Nakayama’s
l
-adic filtered complex as above.
Because
H=\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))
is
contravariantly functorial as already stated, so is
(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
,
though one has to pay attention to the contravariant action on

d\log t
as already remarked.
In particular, on
(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
,
we can consider the pull-back of the Frobenius morphism

X\longrightarrow X\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{1},F_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}}}\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}

over

S\longrightarrow S_{1}^{[p]}(S)
, where
S_{1}^{[p]}(S)
is a
p
-adic formal log scheme
defined similarly as in (
1.0.4
) when
E
is an
F
-isocrystal
on
{\rm Crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})
.

We prove that the complex
A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)

computes the log convergent complex

R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E))

in the category
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

which is above the category
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))

unlike
[
GK
]
nor
[
Nakk4
]
.
That is, we prove the following:

Proposition 1.6
.

There exists the following isomorphism

(1.6.1)

\displaystyle\theta\colon R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)

in
D^{+}({\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}})
.

We calculate the graded complex of

(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
as follows:

Proposition 1.7
.

There exists a canonical isomorphism

(1.7.1)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}

\displaystyle a^{(2j+k)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(2j+k)*}_{\rm conv}(E)

\displaystyle\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))[-2j-k].

Here
\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(\star)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})

is the convergent orientation sheaf associated to
the set
\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
.
If
E
is an
F
-isocrystal on
{\rm Crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})
, then
there exists a canonical isomorphism

(1.7.2)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}

\displaystyle a^{(2j+k)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(2j+k)*}_{\rm conv}(E)

\displaystyle\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))(-j-k)[-2j-k].

Set
\overset{\circ}{f}{}^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}:=\overset{\circ}{f}{}^{(k)}\circ u^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

and
f^{\rm conv}_{X/S}:=f\circ u^{\rm conv}_{X/S}
.
As a corollary of (
1.6.1
) and (
1.7.2
),
we obtain the following spectral sequence
for an
F
-isocrystal
E
on
{\rm Crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})
:

(1.7.3)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}R^{q-2j-k}f^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(a^{(2j+k)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k)}_{{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))(-j-k)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow R^{q}f^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E)).

If
\overset{\circ}{f}
is proper and if
E={\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
,
then we call
(
1.7.3
)
the
weight spectral sequence
of

R^{q}f^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}({\cal K}_{X/S})
.

We also define another filtered complex

(A^{N}_{\rm conv},P)
by using the “derived Hirsch extension” of
H
by adding a one variable to
the complex
H
(the notion of the derived Hirsch extension has been defined in
[
Nakk5
]
)
and by considering the mapping fiber of the monodromy operator on this new complex
as in the complex analytic case.
(See §
10
for the precise definition of
(A^{N}_{\rm conv},P)
.)
We prove the following:

Theorem 1.8

(
Comparison theorem between
(A^{N}_{\rm conv},P)
and

(A_{\rm conv},P)
).

There exists a canonical isomorphism

(1.8.1)

(A^{N}_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P).

Finally we state a relationship
between
(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar},P)
and
(A_{\rm zar},P)

in
[
Nakk4
]
.
Since
\overset{\circ}{X}
is not smooth over
\overset{\circ}{S}
in general,
the relationship is
not so simple for a general coefficient:

Theorem 1.9

(
A certain comparison theorem between
(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar},P)

and
(A_{\rm zar},P)\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
).

Let
{\rm Isoc}_{\rm conv}(X/S)
and

{\rm Isoc}_{\rm crys}(X/S)

be the category of isocrystals on the log convergent site
{\rm Conv}(X/S)

and that of isocrystals on the log crystalline site
{\rm Crys}(X/S)
, respectively.
Denote by

(1.9.1)

\Xi\colon{\rm Isoc}_{\rm conv}(X/S)\longrightarrow{\rm Isoc}_{\rm crys}(X/S)

the relative version of
the functor defined in
[
Sh1
, Theorem 5.3.1]
which is the log version of

[
O2
, Theorem 7.2]

and denoted by
\Phi
in
[
Sh1
, Theorem 5.3.1]
.
Let
L
be a crystal on
{\rm Crys}(X/S)

such that
K\otimes_{\cal V}L=\Xi(\epsilon^{*{\rm conv}}_{X/S}(E))
.

(
It is a nontrivial fact that
L
indeed exists
(
cf.
[
O2
]
,
[
Sh2
]
).)

Then there exists a certain filtered complex
(A_{{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}}(X/S,\overset{\circ}{L}),P)

and there exists a filtered isomorphism

(1.9.2)

(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,E),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,\overset{\circ}{L}),P)

fitting into the following commutative diagram

(1.9.3)

\begin{CD}A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,E)@>{\sim}>{}>A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,\overset{\circ}{L})\\
@A{\theta\wedge}A{}A@A{}A{\theta\wedge}A\\
Ru^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E))@>{\sim}>{}>Ru^{\rm crys}_{X/S*}(L)\otimes^{L}_{\cal V}K.\end{CD}

(
Though we define
(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,\overset{\circ}{L}),P)
,
we do not define
\overset{\circ}{L}
itself.
If
E={\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
,
then

(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,\overset{\circ}{L}),P)=(A_{\rm zar}(X/S,{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),P)\otimes^{L}_{\cal V}K.)

By using this comparison theorem and
results for
(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,\overset{\circ}{L}),P)

which are obtained as in
[
Nakk4
]
,
we can prove properties of
(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,E),P)
.

The contents of this paper are as follows.

In §
2
we recall the definition of a log convergent topos,
the system of log universal enlargements and basic properties of them.

In §
3

we recall the definition of a log convergent linearization functor
and basic properties of them, especially the compatibility of
the log convergent linearization functor with
a closed immersion of log schemes.

In §
4
we prove the Poincaré lemma of the vanishing cycle sheaf
in a more general situation than that in
[
NS2
]
.

In §
5

we give basic results for log convergent linearization functors
when the base log formal scheme is the underlying formal scheme
of a
p
-adic family of log points.

In §
6
we give basic results for
the log convergent linearization functor of an SNCL scheme.

In §
7
we give the notations of the simplicial log convergent topoi.

In §
8
we show the well-definedness of

(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),P)

by using (
1.3.2
). We also prove (
1.4
) and (
1.5
).

In §
9
we give the definition of
(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)

and we prove (
1.6
) and (
1.7
).

In §
10
we give the definition of
(A^{N}_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
and
we prove (
1.8
).

In §
11
we prove the contravariant functoriality of

(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
and
consequently that of
(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,E),P)
.

In §
12
we give an explicit description of
the edge morphism of the spectral sequence
(
1.7.3
).

In §
13

we give the definitions of monodromy operators and we give formulation
of a variational log convergent monodromy-weight conjecture and a
variational log convergent hard Lefschetz conjecture.

In §
14
we prove (
1.9
).
Set

f^{\rm crys}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/S}:=f^{(k)}\circ u^{\rm crys}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/S}

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})

and

f^{\rm crys}_{X/S}:=f\circ u^{\rm crys}_{X/S}
.
As a corollary of (
1.9
),
(
1.7.3
) for the trivial coefficient
turns out to be canonically isomorphic to
the following weight spectral sequence

(1.9.4)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}R^{q-2j-k}f^{\rm crys}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k)}_{{\rm crys}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\otimes_{\cal V}K(-j-k)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow R^{q}f^{\rm crys}_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{\cal V}K,

which has been constructed in
[
Nakk4
]
.
As a corollary of the comparison of (
1.7.3
)
with (
1.9.4
) for the case of the trivial coefficient,
if
X
is proper over
S_{1}
,
then we obtain the
E_{2}
-degeneration of (
1.7.3
)
in this case
by reducing it to that of (
1.9.4
),
which we have already proved in
[
Nakk4
]
.
In the same section, as another corollary of (
1.9
),
we deduce the filtered base change theorem
of
(Rf^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(A_{\rm conv}(X/S)),P)
from
the filtered base change theorem of

(Rf^{\rm crys}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(A_{\rm crys}(X/S)),P)
.

Notations.

(1) The log structure of a log scheme in this paper is
a sheaf of monoids in the Zariski topos.
For a log (formal) scheme
S
,

\overset{\circ}{S}
denotes
the underlying (formal) scheme of
S
and

{\cal K}_{S}
denotes
{\cal O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.
More generally, for a ringed topos
({\cal T},{\cal O}_{\cal T})
,

{\cal K}_{\cal T}
denotes

{\cal O}_{\cal T}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.
For a morphism
f\colon X\longrightarrow S
of log (formal) schemes,

\overset{\circ}{f}
denotes the underlying morphism

\overset{\circ}{X}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
of (formal) schemes.

(2) SNCD=simple normal crossing divisor, SNCL=simple normal crossing log.

(3) For a morphism
X\longrightarrow S
of log (formal) schemes,
we denote by

\Omega^{i}_{X/S}
(
=\omega^{i}_{X/S}
in

[
Ka
]
) the sheaf of
(formal) relative logarithmic differential forms on
X/S

of degree
i

(i\in{\mathbb{N}})
.

(4) For a commutative monoid
P
with unit element
and for a formal scheme
S

with an ideal of definition of
{\cal I}\subset{\cal O}_{S}
,
we denote by
{\cal O}_{S}\{P\}

the sheaf
\varprojlim_{n}({\cal O}_{S}[P]/{\cal I}^{n}{\cal O}_{S}[P])

in the Zariski topos
{S}_{\rm zar}
of
S
.
When
{\cal I}=0
, we denote

\underline{\rm Spf}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}\{P\})
by

\underline{\rm Spec}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}[P])
.

For a log (formal) scheme
S

with an ideal of definition of
{\cal I}\subset{\cal O}_{S}
,
we denote by
S~{\rm mod}~{\cal I}

the log scheme whose underlying scheme is

\underline{\rm Spec}_{\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal O}_{S}/{\cal I})

and whose log structure
is the inverse image of the log structure of
S

by the immersion

\underline{\rm Spec}_{\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal O}_{S}/{\cal I})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S
.

(5) For the log structure
M_{S}
of a log (formal) scheme
S
,
we denote
M_{S}/{\cal O}_{S}^{*}
by
\overline{M}_{S}
.

(6) We denote the completed tensor
\widehat{\otimes}
of
two topological modules
simply by
\otimes
.

2
Log convergent topoi

In this section we recall basic notions and basic results
about the log convergent topos in
[
O2
]
,
[
O3
]
,
[
Sh2
]
,
[
Sh3
]
and
[
NS2
]
.
See
[
NS2
, §2]
for the proofs of the resutls.

Let
{\cal V}
be a complete discrete valuation ring
of mixed characteristics
(0,p)

with perfect residue field
\kappa
.
Let
S
be a fine log formal scheme
whose underlying formal scheme is a

p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme
in the sense of
[
O1
]
:

\overset{\circ}{S}
is a noetherian formal scheme
over
{\rm Spf}({\cal V})

with the
p
-adic topology
which is topologically of finite type over
{\rm Spf}({\cal V})
.
We always assume that
\overset{\circ}{S}
is flat over
{\rm Spf}({\cal V})
.
We call
S
a fine log
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme.
Let
\pi
be a non-zero element of the maximal ideal
of
{\cal V}
. Set
S_{1}=S~{\rm mod}~\pi{\cal O}_{S}
.
Let
Y
be a fine log scheme over
S_{1}

whose underlying scheme
\overset{\circ}{Y}

is of finite type over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
.
As in
[
O3
, p. 212]
,
[
Sh2
, Definition 2.1.8]
and
[
Sh3
, I (2.1)]
,
we consider a quadruple
(U,T,\iota,u)
,
where
u\colon U\longrightarrow Y
is a strict morphism of
fine log schemes over
S_{1}
and

\overset{\circ}{u}\colon\overset{\circ}{U}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{Y}
is a morphism of schemes
of finite type,

T
is a fine log noetherian formal scheme over
S

whose underlying formal scheme
is topologically of finite type over
\overset{\circ}{S}

and
\iota\colon U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T
is
a closed immersion over
S
.
We say that
T
is a fine log
S
-formal scheme.
As in
[
NS2
]
we have assumed the condition
“the strictness of
u
”,
which has not been assumed
in
[
Sh2
, Definition 2.1.1, 2.1.9]

and
[
Sh3
, I (2.1), (2.2)]
.
We call the
(U,T,\iota,u)

a
prewidening
of
Y/S
.
If
\overset{\circ}{T}
is
p
-adic, then we say that

(U,T,\iota,u)
is a
p
-
adic

prewidening
of
Y/S
.
Set
T_{1}:=T~{\rm mod}~\pi{\cal O}_{T}
and
let
T_{0}
be the log maximal reduced subscheme of
T_{1}
.
Set also
\widetilde{T}:=T~{\rm mod}~(\pi{\textrm{-}}{\rm torsion})
.
By imitating
[
O2
, (1.1)]
,

[
Sh2
, Definition 2.1.1, 2.1.9]
and
[
Sh3
, I (2.2)]
,
we obtain the notion of a widening, an exact (pre)widening
and an enlargement of
Y/S
as follows.

(1) We say that
a (
p
-adic) prewidening

(U,T,\iota,u)
of
Y/S

is a (
p
-
adic
)
widening
of
Y/S
if

\overset{\circ}{\iota}\colon\overset{\circ}{U}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{T}

is defined by an ideal sheaf of definition of
\overset{\circ}{T}
.

(2) We say that a (
p
-adic) (pre)widening

(U,T,\iota,u)
of
Y/S

is
exact
if
\iota
is exact.

(3) We say that an exact widening

(U,T,\iota,u)
of
Y/S
is
an
enlargement
of
Y/S
if
\overset{\circ}{T}
is flat over

{\rm Spf}({\cal V})
and if
T_{0}\subset U
;
the latter condition is equivalent to an inclusion

(2.0.1)

({\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{T}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{U}))^{N}\subset\pi{\cal O}_{T}\quad(\exists N\in{\mathbb{N}}).

We define a
morphism of
(
p
-adic
)

(
exact
)

(
pre
)
widenings and
a morphism of enlargements
in an obvious way.
As usual, we denote
(U,T,\iota,u)
simply by
T
.
For a morphism

f\colon(U,T,\iota,u)\longrightarrow(U^{\prime},T^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},u^{\prime})
of
(
p
-adic) (exact) (pre)widenings
or enlargements of
Y/S
, we denote the morphism

T\longrightarrow T^{\prime}
of fine log (
p
-adic) formal schemes over
S

by
f\colon T\longrightarrow T^{\prime}
as usual.

The objects of the log convergent site
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)

are, by definition, the enlargements of
Y/S
; the morphisms
of
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
are the morphisms of enlargements of

Y/S
; for an object
(U,T,\iota,u)
of

{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
, a family

\{(U_{\lambda},T_{\lambda},\iota_{\lambda},u_{\lambda})\longrightarrow(U,T,\iota,u)\}_{\lambda}

of morphisms in
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
is defined to
be a covering family of
(U,T,\iota,u)
if

T=\bigcup_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}
is a Zariski open covering and if
the natural morphism
U_{\lambda}\longrightarrow T_{\lambda}\times_{T}U
is an isomorphism
(cf.
[
O2
, p. 135]
,
[
Sh2
, Definition (2.1.3)]
,
[
Sh3
, I (2.4)]
).
Let
(Y/S)_{\rm conv}

be the topos associated to the site
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
.
An object
E
in
(Y/S)_{\rm conv}

gives a Zariski sheaf
E_{T}

in the Zariski topos
T_{\rm zar}
of
T

for
T=(U,T,\iota,u)\in{\rm Ob}({\rm Conv}(Y/S))
.
As in
[
O2
]
and
[
Sh2
]
,
for a prewidening
T
of
Y/S
,
we denote simply by
T

the following sheaf

h_{T}\colon{\rm Conv}(Y/S)\owns T^{\prime}\longmapsto\{\text{the morphisms $T^{\prime}\longrightarrow T$ of prewidenings
of $Y/S$}\}\in({\rm Sets})

on
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
.
Let
{\cal K}_{Y/S}
be an isostructure sheaf in

(Y/S)_{\rm conv}
defined by
the following formula

\Gamma((U,T,\iota,u),{\cal K}_{Y/S}):=\Gamma(T,{\cal O}_{T})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

for
(U,T,\iota,u)\in{\rm Ob}({\rm Conv}(Y/S))
.

Let
f\colon Y\longrightarrow S_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S

be the structural morphism.
Let
\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\rm zar}
be the Zariski topos of
\overset{\circ}{Y}
and
let

(2.0.2)

u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}\colon(Y/S)_{\rm conv}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\rm zar}

be a morphism of topoi
characterized by a formula

u^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S}(E)(T)=\Gamma(U,u^{-1}(E))
for a sheaf

E
in
\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\rm zar}
and an enlargement
T=(U,T,\iota,u)
of
Y/S
.
Set
f^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}:=f\circ u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}
and

{\cal K}_{S}:={\cal O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Let

(2.0.3)

u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}\colon((Y/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S})\longrightarrow(Y_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))

be a morphism of ringed topoi induced by the morphism (
2.0.2
).

Let
{\cal V}^{\prime}
be another complete discrete valuation ring
of mixed characteristics
(0,p)
with perfect residue field.
Let
\pi^{\prime}
be a non-zero element of the maximal ideal
of
{\cal V}^{\prime}
. Let
{\cal V}\longrightarrow{\cal V}^{\prime}
be a morphism of
commutative rings with unit elements inducing a morphism

{\cal V}/\pi{\cal V}\longrightarrow{\cal V}^{\prime}/\pi^{\prime}{\cal V}^{\prime}
.
Let
S^{\prime}
be a fine log
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}^{\prime}
-scheme
(
\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}
is assumed to be flat over
{\cal V}^{\prime}
).
Set
S^{\prime}_{1}:=S^{\prime}~{\rm mod}~\pi^{\prime}{\cal O}_{S^{\prime}}
.
Let
Y^{\prime}
be a fine log scheme over
S^{\prime}_{1}
such that
the underlying structural morphism

\overset{\circ}{Y}{}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}_{1}
is of finite type.
For a commutative diagram

(2.0.4)

\begin{CD}Y^{\prime}@>{}>{}>S^{\prime}_{1}@>{\subset}>{}>S^{\prime}@>{}>{}>{\rm Spf}({\cal V}^{\prime})\\
@V{g}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{u}V@V{}V{}V\\
Y@>{}>{}>S_{1}@>{\subset}>{}>S@>{}>{}>{\rm Spf}({\cal V})\end{CD}

of fine log (formal) schemes, we have a natural morphism

g_{\rm conv}\colon((Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})\longrightarrow((Y/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S})

of ringed topoi.

The following is easy to prove:

Proposition 2.1

(
[
NS2
, (2.1)]
).

Assume that
{\cal V}^{\prime}={\cal V}
and that
the morphism
{\cal V}\longrightarrow{\cal V}^{\prime}
is the identity of
{\cal V}

(\pi^{\prime}
may be different from
\pi)
.
Assume also that
S^{\prime}=S
and
Y^{\prime}=Y\times_{S_{1}}S^{\prime}_{1}
.
Denote
g
in
(
2.0.4
)
by
i
.
Let
f^{\prime}\colon Y^{\prime}\longrightarrow S

be the structural morphism.
Then the following hold
:

(1)
The direct image
i_{{\rm conv}*}

from the category of abelian sheaves in

(Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv}
to
the category of abelian sheaves in

(Y/S)_{\rm conv}

is exact.

(2)
For a bounded below filtered complex

(E^{\bullet},P)
of
{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S}
-modules,

(2.1.1)

Rf^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(i_{{\rm conv}*}((E^{\bullet},P)))=Rf^{\prime}{}^{\rm conv}_{Y^{\prime}/S*}((E^{\bullet},P))

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{S})
.

(3)

(2.1.2)

Rf^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}({\cal K}_{Y/S})=Rf^{\prime}{}^{\rm conv}_{Y^{\prime}/S*}({\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S})

in
D^{+}({\cal K}_{S})
.

For a fine log
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme
T
,
let
{\rm Coh}({\cal O}_{T})
be
the category of coherent
{\cal O}_{T}
-modules.
Let
{\rm Coh}({\cal K}_{T})
be the full subcategory
of the category of
{\cal K}_{T}
-modules
whose objects are isomorphic to
K\otimes_{\cal V}{\cal F}

for some
{\cal F}\in{\rm Coh}({\cal O}_{T})

(
[
O1
, (1.1)]
).
In
[
NS2
]
we have said that a
{\cal K}_{Y/S}
-module
E
is
a coherent isocrystal
if
E_{T}\in{\rm Coh}({\cal K}_{T})

for any enlargement
T
of
Y/S
and if

\rho_{f}\colon f^{*}(E_{T^{\prime}})\longrightarrow E_{T}
is an isomorphism
for any morphism
f\colon T\longrightarrow T^{\prime}
of enlargements of
Y/S

as in
[
O1
]
,
[
O2
]
,
[
O3
]
,
[
Sh2
]
.
As stated in the Introduction, we call a coherent isocrystal simply an isocrystal as in
[
B2
]

in this paper.

Next we recall the system of universal enlargements
(cf.
[
O2
, (2.1)]
,
[
Sh2
, Definition 2.1.22]
,
[
Sh3
, I (2.10), (2.11), (2.12)]
).

Let
(U,T,\iota,u)
be
an enlargement of
Y/S
.
We say that
(U,T,\iota,u)
is
an enlargement of
Y/S
with radius
\leq|\pi|

if
{\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{T}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{U})\subset\pi{\cal O}_{T}
.
The following is a log version of
[
O2
, (2.1)]

and an immediate generalization of

[
Sh2
, Definition 2.1.22, Lemma 2.1.23]

for the case
n=1
in [loc. cit.]:

Proposition 2.2

(
[
NS2
, (2.2)]
).

Let
T=(U,T,\iota,u)
be
a
(p
-adic
)

(
pre
)
widening of

Y/S
. Then there exists an enlargement

T_{1}=(U_{1},T_{1},\iota_{1},u_{1})
of
Y/S

with radius
\leq|\pi|

with a morphism
T_{1}\longrightarrow T
of

(p
-adic
)

(
pre
)
widenings of
Y/S

satisfying the following universality
:

any morphism
T^{\prime}:=(U^{\prime},T^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},u^{\prime})\longrightarrow T

of
(p
-adic
)

(
pre
)
widenings of
Y/S

from an enlargement of
Y/S

with radius
\leq|\pi|

factors through
a unique morphism

T^{\prime}\longrightarrow T_{1}
of enlargements of
Y/S
.

By replacing
{\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{T}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{U})

by
({\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{T}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{U}))^{n}

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
in (
2.2
),
we have the inductive system
\{T_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

(
T_{n}=(U_{n},T_{n},\iota_{n},u_{n})
) (cf.
[
O1
]
,
[
O2
]
and
[
Sh2
]
).
We call
\{T_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
the
system of the universal enlargements

of
(U,T,\iota,u)
. We denote the log formal scheme
T_{n}

by
{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T)
.
By abuse of notation, we also denote
the enlargement
(U_{n},T_{n},\iota_{n},u_{n})

by
{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T)
.
For a
(
not necessarily closed
)
immersion

\iota\colon U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T
, we can define

T_{n}:={\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T)
as usual.
For a prewidening
T=(U,T,\iota,u)
of
Y/S
,
we can define the following functor

h_{T}\colon{\rm Conv}(Y/S)\longrightarrow({\rm Sets})

by using the morphisms of prewidening of
Y/S
as usual.
We also obtain the localized category

(Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T}
as usual.
Let
\beta_{n}\colon T_{n}\longrightarrow T

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})

be the natural morphism.
Let
E
be a sheaf in
(Y/S)_{\rm conv}
.
Let
E_{T}
be a sheaf in
T_{\rm zar}
defined by
a formula
E_{T}(T^{\prime})={\rm Hom}_{(Y/S)_{\rm conv}}(h_{T^{\prime}},E)

as in
[
O2
, p. 140]
for a log formal open subscheme
T^{\prime}
of
T
.
We can prove that
E_{T}=\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n*}(E_{T_{n}})
as usual.
Let
\overset{\to}{T}
be the associated topos
to the site defined in
[
Sh2
, Definition 2.1.28]
,
which is the log version of the site in
[
O2
, §3]
.
An object of this site is
a log open formal subscheme
V_{n}
of
T_{n}

for a positive integer
n
; let
V_{m}
be
a log open formal subscheme of
T_{m}
;
for
n>m
,
{\rm Hom}(V_{n},V_{m}):=\emptyset

and, for
n\leq m
,

{\rm Hom}(V_{n},V_{m})

is the set of morphisms
V_{n}\longrightarrow V_{m}
’s of
log formal subschemes over the natural morphism
T_{n}\longrightarrow T_{m}
.
A covering of
V_{n}
is a Zariski open covering of
V_{n}
.
Let
\overset{\to}{T}
be the topos associated to this site.
Let
\phi_{n}\colon T_{n}\longrightarrow T_{n+1}
be the natural morphism
of enlargements of
Y/S
.
Then an object
{\cal F}
in
\overset{\to}{T}

is a family
\{({\cal F}_{n},\psi_{n})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
of pairs,
where
{\cal F}_{n}
is a Zariski sheaf in
(T_{n})_{\rm zar}

and
\psi_{n}\colon\phi^{-1}_{n}({\cal F}_{n+1})\longrightarrow{\cal F}_{n}

is a morphism of Zariski sheaves in
(T_{n})_{\rm zar}
.
Let
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}=\{({\cal K}_{T_{n}},\phi_{n}^{-1})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

be the isostructure sheaf of
\overset{\to}{T}
,
where
\phi_{n}^{-1}
is the natural pull-back morphism

\phi_{n}^{-1}({\cal K}_{T_{n+1}})\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{T_{n}}
.
Following
[
O2
, p. 141]
,
we say that a
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}
-module

{\cal F}
is
coherent

if
{\cal F}_{n}\in{\rm Coh}({\cal K}_{T_{n}})

for all
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

and
we say that
{\cal F}
is
admissible
(resp.
isocrystalline
)
if the natural morphism

{\cal K}_{T_{n}}\otimes_{\phi^{-1}_{n+1}({\cal K}_{T_{n+1}})}\phi^{-1}_{n+1}({\cal F}_{n+1})\longrightarrow{\cal F}_{n}

is surjective (resp. isomorphic).
For an object
E
of

(Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T}
and
for a morphism
T^{\prime}\longrightarrow T
of quasi-prewidenings of
Y/S
,
then we have an associated object

{\cal E}^{\prime}=\{{\cal E}^{\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

({\cal E}^{\prime}_{n}:=E_{T^{\prime}_{n}})
in
\overset{\to}{T^{\prime}}
.
Following
[
O2
, p. 147]
, we say that
a
{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{T}
-module
E
is

admissible
(resp.
isocrystalline
)
if
{\cal E}^{\prime}
is coherent and admissible (resp. isocrystalline) for any morphism
T^{\prime}\longrightarrow T

of quasi-prewidenings.
In this isocrystalline case, we say that
E
is an isocrystal of

{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{T}
-modules.
Let
\beta\colon\overset{\to}{T}\longrightarrow T_{\rm zar}

be the natural morphism of topoi
which is denoted by
\gamma
in
[
Sh2
, p. 55]

(=the log version of
\gamma
in
[
O1
, p. 141]
).

The following is a relative log version of

[
O2
, Lemma 2.4]
(with an additional assumption)
and
[
Sh2
, Lemma 2.1.26]
(see also
[
Sh3
, I (2.16)]
):

Lemma 2.3

(
[
NS2
, (2.9)]
).

Let
(U^{\prime},T^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},u^{\prime})\longrightarrow(U,T,\iota,u)
be a morphism
of quasi-prewidenings of
Y/S
such that
U^{\prime}=U\times_{T}T^{\prime}
.
Then the natural morphism

{\mathfrak{T}}_{U^{\prime},n}(T^{\prime})\longrightarrow\widetilde{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T)\times_{T}T^{\prime}}

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
is an isomorphism.
If
\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}
is flat, then the natural morphism

{\mathfrak{T}}_{U^{\prime},n}(T^{\prime})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T)\times_{T}T^{\prime}

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
is an isomorphism.

3
Log convergent linearization functors

In this section we only recall results in
[
NS2
, §3]
without any proof.
Let
S
and
Y/S_{1}
be as in §
2
.
Let
\iota_{Y}\colon Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}
be an immersion into
a fine log
S
-formal scheme.
As in
[
NS2
, §3]
we do not necessarily assume that
{\cal Y}/S
is formally log smooth
except (
3.5
) and (
3.6
).

Let
{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})
be
the quasi-prewidening

(Y,{\cal Y},\iota_{Y},{\rm id}_{Y})
of
Y/S
.
Let

\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

(
{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y}):=(Y_{n},{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y}),Y_{n}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y}),Y_{n}\longrightarrow Y)
,

Y_{n}:=Y\times_{\cal Y}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})
)
be the system of the universal enlargements of

{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})
.
We call
\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

the
system of the universal enlargements of

\iota_{Y}
for short.
Let

((Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})},{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})})

be the localized ringed topos
of
((Y/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S})

at
{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})

and let

j_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}\colon((Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})},{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})})\longrightarrow((Y/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S})

be the natural morphism of the ringed topoi and let

(3.0.1)

\displaystyle\varphi^{*}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}\colon

\displaystyle\{\text{the category of isocrystals of }{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}\text{-modules}\}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\{\text{the category of isocrystals of }{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}\text{-modules}\}

be a natural functor which is
a relative log version (with
\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
)
of the functor in
[
O2
, p. 147]
:

\varphi^{*}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}(\{{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty})(T)=\Gamma(T,\psi_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n}))
for
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, where

\psi_{n}\colon T\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})
is a morphism such that the composite morphism

T\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})
is
the given morphism
for an object
T
of

{\rm Conv}(Y/S)|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}
.
We also have the following natural functor

(3.0.2)

\varphi_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})*}\colon((Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})},{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})})\longrightarrow({\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}_{\rm zar},{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})})

by setting
\varphi_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})*}(E)(U):=E((U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y}))\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y}))
.
For an isocrystal of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}
-module
{\cal E}
, set

(3.0.3)

L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}):=j_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})*}\varphi^{*}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}({\cal E})

by abuse of notation (cf.
[
O2
, p. 152]
,
[
Sh2
, p. 95]
)
(“UE” is the abbreviation of the universal enlargement).
Strictly speaking, we should denote

L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E})
by

L^{\rm UE}_{(Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y})/S}({\cal E})
since

L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E})
depends on the immersion
Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}

over
S
.

For an object
T:=(U,T,\iota,u)
of
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
,
the value
L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E})_{T}
of

L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E})
at
T
is calculated as follows.
First we have the natural immersion

(\iota,\iota_{Y}\circ u)\colon U\longrightarrow T\times_{S}{\cal Y}
over
S
.
Let

\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

be the system of the universal enlargements
of this immersion.
Then we have the following diagram
of the inductive systems of enlargements of
Y/S
:

(3.0.4)

\begin{CD}T@<{\{p^{\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}<{}<\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}@>{\{p_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}>{}>\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}.\end{CD}

Let
{\cal E}=\{{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

be an isocrystal of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}
-modules.
Then, as in the proof of
[
O2
, (5.4)]
and

[
Sh2
, Theorem 2.3.5]
,
we have the following formula:

(3.0.5)

L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E})_{T}=\varprojlim_{n}p^{\prime}_{n*}p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n}).

Definition 3.1
.

We call the functor

(3.1.1)

L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}\colon\{{\rm isocrystals}~{\rm of}~{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}{\textrm{-}}{\rm modules}\}\longrightarrow\{{\cal K}_{Y/S}\text{-}{\rm modules}\}

the
log convergent linearization functor with respect to

\iota_{Y}
for isocrystals of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}
-modules.

In the following we recall results in
[
NS2
, §3]
.

Lemma 3.2

(
[
NS2
, (3.2)]
).

Let
T=(U,T,\iota,u)
be
an exact quasi-
(
pre
)
widening of

Y/S
and let
\{T_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be
the system of the universal enlargements of
T
.
Let
\beta_{n}\colon T_{n}\longrightarrow T
be the natural morphism.
Then, for each
n
,
the pull-back functor

\beta^{*}_{n}\colon\{{\rm coherent}~{\cal K}_{T}{\textrm{-}}{\rm modules}\}\longrightarrow\{{\rm coherent}~{\cal K}_{T_{n}}{\textrm{-}}{\rm modules}\}

is exact.

Proposition 3.3

(
[
NS2
, (3.5)]
).

If the morphism
\overset{\circ}{\cal Y}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
is affine,
then the functor
L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}
in
(
3.1.1
)

is right exact.

Lemma 3.4

(
[
NS2
, (3.6)]
).

Assume that
\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Y}/S}
is locally free.
Let
{\cal E}:=\{{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be an isocrystal of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}
-modules.
Then there exists a family
\{\nabla^{i}\}_{i=0}^{\infty}

of natural morphisms

\nabla^{i}\colon L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Y}/S})\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal Y}/S})

which makes
\{(L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Y}/S}),\nabla^{i})\}_{i=0}^{\infty}

a complex of
{\cal K}_{Y/S}
-modules.

The following is a relative version of

[
O2
, (5.4)]
and
[
Sh2
, Theorem 2.3.5 (2)]
,
though the formulation of it is rather different from them:

Theorem 3.5

(
Log converegent Poncaré lemma (
[
NS2
, (3.7)]
)).

Assume that
{\cal Y}/S
is log smooth.
Let
E
be an isocrystal of
{\cal K}_{Y/S}
-modules.
Set
{\cal E}_{n}:=E_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})}
and

{\cal E}:=\{{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
.
Then the natural morphism

(3.5.1)

E\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}/S})

is a quasi-isomorphism.

The following has been essentially proved
in
[
O2
, (6.6)]
and
[
Sh2
, Corollary 2.3.8]
:

Proposition 3.6

(
(
[
NS2
, (3.9)]
)).

Let the notations be as in
(
3.5
)
.
Set
Y_{n}:=Y\times_{\cal Y}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})
.
Let

\beta_{n}\colon Y_{n}\longrightarrow Y
be
the projection and identify

{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})_{\rm zar}
with

Y_{n,{\rm zar}}
.
Then

(3.6.1)

Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(E)=\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n*}({\cal E}_{n}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}/S})

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))
.

In the rest of this section we recall results in
[
NS2
, §4]
, in which
we have proved that log convergent linearization functors are
compatible with closed immersions of log (formal) schemes.

Let
{\cal V}
,
\pi
,
S
and
S_{1}

be as in §
2
.

Lemma 3.7

(
[
NS2
, (4.1)]
).

Let
\iota_{Y}\colon Y_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}Y_{2}
be
a closed immersion of
fine log schemes over
S_{1}

whose underlying schemes are of finite type
over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
.
Let
T_{j}:=(U_{j},T_{j},\iota_{j},u_{j})

(j=1,2)
be a
(
pre
)
widening of
Y_{j}/S
.
Let
{\iota}_{U}\colon U_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}U_{2}

and
\iota_{T}\colon T_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T_{2}

be closed immersions
of fine log
S
-formal schemes.
Assume that
{\iota}_{U}
and
\iota_{T}

induce a morphism

(\iota_{U},\iota_{T})\colon(U_{1},T_{1},\iota_{1},u_{1})\longrightarrow(U_{2},T_{2},\iota_{2},u_{2})

of
(
pre
)
widenings of
Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
.
Let

\iota^{\rm loc}_{\rm conv}\colon(Y_{1}/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T_{1}}\longrightarrow(Y_{2}/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T_{2}}

be the induced morphism of topoi by
\iota_{Y}

and
(\iota_{U},\iota_{T})
.
Let
(U,T,\iota,u)
be a
(
pre
)
widening of
Y_{2}/S

over
(U_{2},T_{2},\iota_{2},u_{2})
.
Then
\iota^{{\rm loc}*}_{\rm conv}((U,T,\iota,u))=(U\times_{U_{2}}U_{1},T\times_{T_{2}}T_{1},\iota\times_{\iota_{2}}\iota_{1},u\times_{u_{2}}u_{1})

as sheaves in
(Y_{1}/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T_{1}}
.

Let
\iota_{Y,Z}\colon Z\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}Y

be a closed immersion of fine log
schemes over
S_{1}
.
Assume that there exists the following cartesian diagram

(3.7.1)

\begin{CD}Z@>{\iota_{\cal Z}}>{}>{\cal Z}\\
@V{\iota_{Y,Z}}V{}V@V{}V{\iota_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z}}}V\\
Y@>{\iota_{\cal Y}}>{}>{\cal Y},\end{CD}

where
\iota_{\cal Z}
and
\iota_{\cal Y}

are closed immersions into
fine log
p
-adic
S
-formal schemes and

\iota_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z}}
is a closed immersion of
fine log
p
-adic
S
-formal schemes.
Set
{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z}):=(Z,{\cal Z},\iota_{\cal Z},{\rm id}_{Z})

and

{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y}):=(Y,{\cal Y},\iota_{\cal Y},{\rm id}_{Y})
.
Let

\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

and

\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

be the systems of the universal enlargements
of
\iota_{\cal Z}
and
\iota_{\cal Y}

with natural morphisms

g_{{\cal Y},n}\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\longrightarrow{\cal Y}

and

g_{{\cal Z},n}\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})\longrightarrow{\cal Z}
,
respectively.
Because the diagram (
3.7.1
) is cartesian,
we have the following equality by (
2.3
):

(3.7.2)

{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\times_{{\cal Y}}{\cal Z}}.

Set
Z_{n}:=Z\times_{\cal Z}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})
and

Y_{n}:=Y\times_{\cal Y}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})
.
Let
\iota_{{\cal Z},n}

and
\iota_{{\cal Y},n}

be the natural closed immersions

Z_{n}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})

and

Y_{n}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})
, respectively.
Let
\iota_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z},n}\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})
be also the natural closed immersion.
Let

\overset{\to}{\iota}{}_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z}*}\colon\{{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}\text{-modules}\}\longrightarrow\{{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}\text{-modules}\}

be the natural direct image.

Lemma 3.8

(
[
NS1
, (4.3)]
).

Let

\iota^{\rm loc}_{\rm conv}\colon(Z/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})}\longrightarrow(Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})}

be the natural morphism of topoi.
Then the following diagram

(3.8.1)

\begin{CD}\{\text{isocrystals of }{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}\text{-modules}\}@>{\varphi_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}^{*}}>{}>\\
@V{\overset{\to}{\iota}{}_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z}*}}V{}V\\
\{\text{isocrystals of }{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}\text{-modules}\}@>{\varphi_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}^{*}}>{}>\end{CD}

\begin{CD}\{\text{isocrystals of }{\cal K}_{Z/S}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}\text{-modules}\}\\
@V{}V{\iota^{\rm loc}_{{\rm conv}*}}V\\
\{\text{isocrystals of }{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}\text{-modules}\}\end{CD}

is commutative.

Lemma 3.9

(
[
NS1
, (4.4)]
).

The following diagram of topoi

(3.9.1)

\begin{CD}(Z/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}@>{j_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}}>{}>(Z/S)_{\rm conv}\\
@V{\iota_{{\rm conv}}^{\rm loc}}V{}V@V{}V{\iota_{\rm conv}}V\\
(Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}@>{j_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}}>{}>(Y/S)_{\rm conv}\\
\end{CD}

is commutative.

Corollary 3.10

(
cf.
[
NS2
, (4.5)]
).

There exists a canonical
isomorphism of functors

(3.10.1)

L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}\circ\overset{\to}{\iota}_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z}*}\longrightarrow\iota_{{\rm conv}*}\circ L^{\rm UE}_{Z/S}

for the coherent
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}
-modules.

4
Vanishing cycle sheaves in log convergent topoi

In this section we generalize results in
[
NS2
, §5]
,
which can be applicable for SNCL schemes
(see also
[
NS1
, (2.3)]
for the analogues of
[
NS2
, §5]
).
Though one may think that the proofs in this section
are imitations of those in
[
NS2
, §5]
,
we give the complete proofs because some proofs in this section are rather different
from those in [loc. cit.].
Because the formulation in this section is better than that of [loc. cit.],
some of the proofs in this section become simpler than those of [loc. cit.].

Let
Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}
,
Y/S
,
g\colon Y^{\prime}\longrightarrow Y
and
S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S
be as in §
2
.
In this section we assume that
\overset{\circ}{Y}{}^{\prime}=\overset{\circ}{Y}
.
Let
M
and
N
be the log structures of
Y^{\prime}
and
Y
, respecitvely.
We assume that
N\subset M
and that the morphism
g\colon Y^{\prime}\longrightarrow Y

is induced by the inclusion
N\subset M
and
{\rm id}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}}
.
Denote the morphism
g\colon Y^{\prime}\longrightarrow Y
over
S^{\prime}_{1}\longrightarrow S_{1}
by

(4.0.1)

\epsilon_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}_{1}/S_{1}}\colon Y^{\prime}\longrightarrow Y

in this section.
The morphism
\epsilon_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}_{1}/S_{1}}

induces the following morphism of log convergent topoi:

(4.0.2)

\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S}\colon(Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv}\longrightarrow(Y/S)_{\rm conv}.

Let
f^{\prime}\colon Y^{\prime}\longrightarrow S^{\prime}
and
f\colon Y\longrightarrow S
be the structural morphisms.
We call the morphism
\epsilon_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}_{1}/S_{1}}

a
morphism of log schemes

forgetting the structure

M\setminus N
over
S^{\prime}_{1}/S_{1}

and we call the morphism

\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S}

the
morphism of log convergent topoi forgetting the structure

M\setminus N
over
S^{\prime}/S
.
When the log structure of
S
is trivial and
N
is trivial,
we call
\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S}
the

morphism forgetting the log structure
of
Y^{\prime}
over
S^{\prime}/S
.
For simplicity of notations, we denote

\epsilon_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}_{1}/S_{1}}
and
\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S}

simply by
\epsilon
and
\epsilon_{\rm conv}
before (
4.6
).
The morphism
\epsilon_{\rm conv}
also induces the following morphism

(4.0.3)

\epsilon_{\rm conv}\colon((Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})\longrightarrow((Y/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S})

of ringed topoi.
Let

u^{\rm conv}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}\colon((Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})\longrightarrow(Y^{\prime}_{\rm zar},f^{\prime}{}^{-1}({\cal K}_{S^{\prime}}))=(Y_{\rm zar},f^{\prime}{}^{-1}({\cal K}_{S^{\prime}}))

and

u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}\colon((Y/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S})\longrightarrow(Y_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))

be the projections in (
2.0.3
).
Let

v\colon(Y^{\prime}_{\rm zar},f^{\prime}{}^{-1}({\cal K}_{S^{\prime}}))\longrightarrow(Y_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))

be the natural morphism.
Then we have the following equality:

(4.0.4)

u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}\circ\epsilon_{\rm conv}=v\circ u^{\rm conv}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}.

Next we localize
\epsilon_{\rm conv}
’s in (
4.0.2
) and
(
4.0.3
).
Let

T^{\prime}=(U^{\prime},T^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},u^{\prime})=((\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime},M_{U^{\prime}}),(\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime},M_{T^{\prime}}),\iota^{\prime},u^{\prime})

and

T=(U,T,\iota,u)=((\overset{\circ}{U},N_{U}),(\overset{\circ}{T},N_{T}),\iota,u)

be an exact prewidening of
Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}
and an exact prewidening of
Y/S
, respectively.
Let
T^{\prime}\longrightarrow T
be a morphism of prewidenings over

\epsilon_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}_{1}/S_{1}}
.
Then we have a morphism

(4.0.5)

\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}T}\colon(Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}}\longrightarrow(Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T}

of topoi and a morphism

(4.0.6)

\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}T}\colon((Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}},{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}|_{T^{\prime}})\longrightarrow((Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T},{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{T})

of ringed topoi.
In
[
NS1
, (2.3.0.3)]
and
[
NS2
]
we have assumed that, locally on
\overset{\circ}{Y}
,
there exists a finitely generated commutative monoid
P

with unit element such that
P^{\rm gp}
has no
p
-torsion
and that there exists a chart
P\longrightarrow N
.
However we do not assume this in this paper
because we are given
N_{T}
already as a setting.
Henceforth we assume that the morphism
T^{\prime}\longrightarrow T
is integral and
that the underlying morphism
\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}
is an immersion.

The following is a generalization of
[
NS2
, (5.2)]
:

Lemma 4.1

(
cf.
[
NS2
, (5.2)]
).

Let the notations be as above.
Then the functor

\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}T*}
is exact.

Proof.

Let
\phi_{N}\colon T^{\prime\prime}:=(U^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime\prime},\iota^{\prime\prime},u^{\prime\prime})\longrightarrow T=(U,T,\iota,u)

(
U^{\prime\prime}=(\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime\prime},N_{U^{\prime\prime}})
,
T^{\prime\prime}=(\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime\prime},N_{T^{\prime\prime}})
)
be a morphism of widenings of
Y/S
(
N_{U^{\prime\prime}}
and
N_{T^{\prime\prime}}
are, by definition,
the log structures of
U^{\prime\prime}

and
T^{\prime\prime}
, respectively).
Consider the following quadruple

(U^{\prime\prime\prime},T^{\prime\prime\prime},\iota^{\prime\prime\prime},u^{\prime\prime\prime}):=(U^{\prime}\times_{U}U^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime}\times_{T}T^{\prime\prime},\iota^{\prime}\times_{U}\iota^{\prime\prime},u^{\prime}\times_{U}u^{\prime\prime}).

Here the fiber products are considered in the category of fine log schemes.
Because the morphism
T^{\prime}\longrightarrow T
is integral,
T^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
U^{\prime\prime\prime}
are integral.
Let

\phi_{U}\colon(U^{\prime\prime\prime})^{\circ}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime}

and

\phi\colon(T^{\prime\prime\prime})^{\circ}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}

be the underlying morphisms of
(formal) schemes obtained by
\phi_{N}
.
Set
M_{U^{\prime\prime}}:=\phi_{U}^{*}(M_{U^{\prime}})
,
M_{T^{\prime\prime}}:=\phi^{*}(M_{T^{\prime}})
,
U^{\prime}_{M}:=((U^{\prime\prime\prime})^{\circ},M_{U^{\prime\prime}})
and

T^{\prime}_{M}:=((T^{\prime\prime\prime})^{\circ},M_{T^{\prime\prime}})
.
Let
\iota^{\prime}_{M}\colon{U}^{\prime}_{M}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T^{\prime}_{M}

and
u^{\prime}_{M}\colon U^{\prime}_{M}\longrightarrow Y^{\prime}

be the natural morphisms.
Let

\phi_{M}\colon(U^{\prime}_{M},T^{\prime}_{M},\iota^{\prime}_{M},u^{\prime}_{M})\longrightarrow(U^{\prime},T^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},u^{\prime})

be the natural morphism.
Then
(U^{\prime}_{M},T^{\prime}_{M},\iota^{\prime}_{M},u^{\prime}_{M};\phi^{\prime}_{M})
is an object of
(Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}}
.
By the same proof as that of
[
NS1
, (2.3.3)]
, we see that

(4.1.1)

T^{\prime\prime\prime}=T^{\prime}_{M}.

We also see that

(4.1.2)

U^{\prime\prime\prime}=U^{\prime}_{M}.

By the formula (
4.1.1
) and
(
4.1.2
),
(\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}T})^{*}(U^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime\prime},\iota^{\prime\prime},u^{\prime\prime};\phi_{N})

is represented by

(U^{\prime}_{M},T^{\prime}_{M},\iota^{\prime}_{M},u^{\prime}_{M};\phi_{M})
.
Therefore,
for an object
E^{\prime}
in
(Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}}
, we obtain the following equalities

(4.1.3)

\displaystyle\Gamma((U^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime\prime},\iota^{\prime\prime},u^{\prime\prime};\phi_{N}),(\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}T})_{*}(E^{\prime}))

\displaystyle={\rm Hom}_{(Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}}}((\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}T})^{*}(U^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime\prime},\iota^{\prime\prime},u^{\prime\prime};\phi_{N}),E^{\prime})

\displaystyle=E^{\prime}(U^{\prime}_{M},T^{\prime}_{M},\iota^{\prime},u^{\prime}_{M};\phi_{M}).

Because
\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}
is an immersion,
the projection
(T^{\prime}_{M})^{\circ}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T^{\prime\prime}}
is an immersion.
Hence, by the formula (
4.1.3
),
we see that the functor
\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}T*}
is exact.
∎

Lemma 4.2

(
cf.
[
NS2
, (5.3)]
).

Let the notations be as above. Then the following diagram of
topoi is commutative
:

(4.2.1)

\begin{CD}(Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}}@>{j_{T^{\prime}}}>{}>(Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv}\\
@V{\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}T}}V{}V@V{}V{\epsilon_{\rm conv}}V\\
(Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T}@>{j_{T}}>{}>(Y/S)_{\rm conv}.\\
\end{CD}

The obvious analogue of
(
4.2.1
)
for ringed topoi

((Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})
and
((Y/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S})

also holds.

Proof.

Let
G
be an object of
(Y/S)_{\rm conv}
.
By the proof of (
4.1
),

(\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}T})^{*}\allowbreak(T)\allowbreak=T^{\prime}
.
Hence
(\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}T})^{*}j_{T}^{*}(G)=(\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}T})^{*}(G\times T)=\epsilon^{*}_{\rm conv}(G)\times T^{\prime}=j_{T^{\prime}}^{*}\epsilon^{*}_{\rm conv}(G)
.
Hence the former statement follows.
The latter statement immediately follows from this.
∎

Lemma 4.3

(
[
NS2
, (5.4)]
).

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{T}
is affine.
Let
E^{\prime}
be an admissible sheaf of

{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}|_{T^{\prime}}
-modules in
(Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv}|_{T^{\prime}}
.
Then the canonical morphism

(4.3.1)

\epsilon_{{\rm conv}*}j_{T^{\prime}*}(E^{\prime})\longrightarrow R\epsilon_{{\rm conv}*}j_{T^{\prime}*}(E^{\prime})

is an isomorphism in the derived category

D^{+}({\cal K}_{Y/S})
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of

[
NS2
, (5.4.1)]
by using
(
4.1
) and
(
4.2
).
∎

Corollary 4.4

(
cf.
[
NS2
, (5.5)]
).

Let the notations be as above.
Let
{\cal Y}^{\prime}:=(\overset{\circ}{\cal Y},{\cal M})
be a fine log
S^{\prime}
-formal scheme.
Let
\iota\colon Y^{\prime}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}^{\prime}
be an immersion over
S^{\prime}
.
Let

\iota\colon Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}
be an immersion over
S

fitting into the following commutative diagram
of log formal schemes over
S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S:

(4.4.1)

\begin{CD}Y^{\prime}@>{\iota^{\prime}}>{}>{\cal Y}^{\prime}\\
@V{\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S}}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
Y@>{\iota}>{}>{\cal Y}.\\
\end{CD}

Set
{\mathfrak{T}}:=(Y,{\cal Y},\iota,{\rm id}_{Y})
and

{\mathfrak{T}}^{\prime}:=(Y^{\prime},{\cal Y}^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},{\rm id}_{Y^{\prime}})
.
Let
\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
and
\{{\mathfrak{T}}^{\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be
the systems of the universal enlargements
of
\iota
and
\iota^{\prime}
, respectively,
with the following natural commutative diagram
for
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}:

(4.4.2)

\begin{CD}{\mathfrak{T}}^{\prime}_{n}@>{g^{\prime}_{n}}>{}>{\cal Y}^{\prime}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}@>{g_{n}}>{}>{\cal Y},\\
\end{CD}

where
g^{\prime}_{n}
and
g_{n}
are the natural morphisms.
Assume that the underlying morphism

\overset{\circ}{\mathfrak{T}}{}^{\prime}_{n}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}

of the left vertical morphism
{\mathfrak{T}}^{\prime}_{n}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}

in
(
4.4.2
)
is an immersion.

(
