Then this morphism becomes a closed immersion.
)

Let
L^{\rm UE}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}

be the log convergent linearization functor for isocrystals of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}{}^{\prime}}
-modules with respect to
\iota^{\prime}
.
Let
{\cal E}^{\prime}
be an isocrystal of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}{}^{\prime}}
-modules.
Then the canonical morphism

(4.4.3)

\epsilon_{{\rm conv}*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}({\cal E}^{\prime})\longrightarrow R\epsilon_{{\rm conv}*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}({\cal E}^{\prime})

is an isomorphism in
D^{+}({\cal K}_{Y/S})
.

Proof.

(
4.4
) immediately follows from (
4.3
).
∎

Lemma 4.5

(
cf.
[
NS2
, (5.6)]
).

Let the notations and the assumption be as in
(
4.4
)
.
Let
L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}
be the log convergent linearization functor for isocrystals of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}}
-modules.
Then there exist natural isomorphisms

(4.5.1)

L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\epsilon_{\rm conv*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}

of functors for isocrystals of
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}{}^{\prime}}
-modules.
Here we consider isocrystals of
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}{}^{\prime}}
-modules
as isocrystals of
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}}
-modules by using the closed immersions

{\mathfrak{T}}^{\prime}_{n}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}
’s.

Proof.

Let
\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}^{\prime}_{n}{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}}\colon(Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}^{\prime}_{n}}\longrightarrow(Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}}

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})

be the localized morphism of
\epsilon_{\rm conv}
at

{\mathfrak{T}}^{\prime}_{n}
and
{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}
.
Using the formula (
4.1.3
),
we can immediately check that

(\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}^{\prime}_{n}{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}})_{*}\varphi_{{\mathfrak{T}}^{\prime}_{n}}^{*}=\varphi_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}}^{*}
holds for
isocrystals of
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}{}^{\prime}}
-modules

{\cal E}^{\prime}=\{{\cal E}^{\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
for sufficiently large
n

for an object of
(T^{\prime\prime},\phi)
of
(Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}}

as follows:

\displaystyle\Gamma(T^{\prime\prime},(\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}^{\prime}_{n}{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}})_{*}\varphi_{{\mathfrak{T}}^{\prime}_{n}}^{*}({\cal E}^{\prime}_{n}))

\displaystyle=\Gamma(T^{\prime}_{M},\varphi_{{\mathfrak{T}}^{\prime}_{n}}^{*}({\cal E}^{\prime}_{n}))=\Gamma(T^{\prime}_{M},{\cal K}_{T^{\prime}_{M}}\otimes_{{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}^{\prime}_{n}}}{\cal E}^{\prime}_{n})

\displaystyle=\Gamma(T^{\prime}_{M},({\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}^{\prime}_{n}}\otimes_{{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}}}{\cal K}_{T^{\prime\prime}})\otimes_{{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}^{\prime}_{n}}}{\cal E}^{\prime}_{n})

\displaystyle=\Gamma(T^{\prime\prime},{\cal K}_{T^{\prime\prime}}\otimes_{{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}}}{\cal E}^{\prime}_{n}).

Here we have assumed that the morphism
T^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}
factors through

T^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}
for some
n
and we have used
the assumption in (
4.5
).
Furthermore, by (
4.2
)
we obtain the following commutative diagram:

(4.5.2)

\begin{CD}\{\text{isocrystals of }{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}{}^{\prime}}\text{-modules}\}@>{\varphi^{*}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}{}^{\prime}}}>{}>\{\text{isocrystals of }{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}^{\prime}}\text{-modules}\}\\
@V{\bigcap}V{}V@V{\{(\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}^{\prime}{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}})_{*}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}V{}V\\
\{\text{isocrystals of }{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}}\text{-modules}\}@>{{\varphi^{*}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}}}}>{}>\{\text{isocrystals of }{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{\mathfrak{T}}\text{-modules}\}\end{CD}

\begin{CD}@>{j_{{\mathfrak{T}}{}^{\prime}_{*}}}>{}>\{\text{crystals of }{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}\text{-modules}\}\\
@V{\epsilon_{\rm conv*}}V{}V\\
@>{j_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{*}}}>{}>\{\text{crystals of }{\cal K}_{Y/S}\text{-modules}\}.\end{CD}

Hence we obtain the isomorphism (
4.5.1
).

∎

Definition 4.6
.

Let
E^{\prime}
be an isocrystal of
{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-modules.
We call
R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S*}(E^{\prime})

the
vanishing cycle sheaf
of
E^{\prime}

along

M\setminus N
over
S^{\prime}/S
.
We call
R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S*}({\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})
the

vanishing cycle sheaf
of
Y/S^{\prime}/S

along

M\setminus N
.
If
N
is trivial, we omit the word
“along
M\setminus N
”.

Now assume that
{\cal Y}^{\prime}
is formally log smooth over
S^{\prime}
.
Then we have the following integrable connection for
a coherent crystal

{\cal E}^{\prime}:=\{{\cal E}^{\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}{}^{\prime}}
-modules by (
3.4
):

\displaystyle\nabla^{i}\colon L^{\rm UE}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}({\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S})\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}({\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}).

Hence we can define the following integrable connection

(4.6.1)

\displaystyle\nabla^{i}\colon L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})

fitting into the following commutative
:
diagram

(4.6.2)

\begin{CD}L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})@>{\nabla^{i}}>{}>L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{}V{\simeq}V\\
\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}({\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})@>{\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S*}(\nabla^{i})}>{}>\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}({\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}).\\
\end{CD}

The following theorem is a main result in this section:

Theorem 4.7

(
Poincaré lemma of a vanishing cycle sheaf (cf.
[
NS2
, (5.8)]
)).

Let the notations and the assumption
be as in
(
4.4
)
.
Assume that
{\cal Y}^{\prime}
is formally log smooth over
S^{\prime}
.
Let
E^{\prime}
be an isocrystal of
{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-modules.
Let
({\cal E}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})
be the
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}{}^{\prime}}
-module
with integrable connection corresponding to
E:

\nabla\colon{\cal E}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
.
Assume that we are given the commutative diagram
(
4.4.1
)
.
Then there exists a canonical isomorphism

(4.7.1)

R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S*}(E^{\prime})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})

in
D^{+}({\cal K}_{Y/S})
.

Proof.

By (
3.5.1
) we obtain the following isomorphism

(4.7.2)

\displaystyle E^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}L^{\rm UE}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}({\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}).

Applying
R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S*}
to
both hand sides of (
4.7.2
) and
using (
4.4
), (
4.5
) and the definition of
the differential (
4.6.1
),
we obtain the following:

\displaystyle R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S*}(E^{\prime})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}({\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longleftarrow}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}({\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}).

∎

Remark 4.8
.

In
[
NS2
, (5.8)]
we have assumed that
the open log analogue of
{\cal Y}/S
is log smooth,
while we do not assume that

{\cal Y}/S
is log smooth in this paper. This is a different point from the theory in [loc. cit.].
In ???

Proposition 4.9
.

Assume that
{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}
and
{\cal Y}/S

are formally log smooth over
S^{\prime}
and
S
, respectively.
Let
{\cal E}:=\{{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be
a coherent crystal of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}}
-modules.
Set
{\cal E}^{\prime}:={\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}}}{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}{}^{\prime}}
.
Let

(4.9.1)

\displaystyle\nabla\colon L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E})\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Y}/S})

be the integrable connection obtained in
(
3.4
)
.
Let

\displaystyle\nabla^{i}\colon L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Y}/S})\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal Y}/S})\quad(i\in{\mathbb{N}})

be the induced differential by
(
4.9.1
)
.
Then the following diagram is commutative
:

(4.9.2)

\begin{CD}L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})@>{\nabla^{i}}>{}>L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Y}/S})@>{\nabla^{i}}>{}>L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal Y}/S}).\end{CD}

Proof.

Though the proof is quite long, the proof is the same as that of
[
NS1
, (5.6)]

by writing down the connection in (
3.4
).
∎

5
Log convergent linearization functors over the underlying formal scheme
of a
p
-adic formal family of log points

In this section we consider the (log) convergent linearization functor
in the case where
S^{\prime}
and
S
in the previous section are
the underlying formal scheme of a

p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-family of log points.

Let
S
be a formal family of log points, that is,

S
is an fs(=fine and saturated) log formal scheme which is locally isomorphic to

{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal O}_{S}^{*}
with structural morphism

{\mathbb{N}}\owns n\longmapsto 0^{n}\in{\cal O}_{S}
.
In the following we assume that
\overset{\circ}{S}
is
a flat
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme in the sense of
[
O1
]
.
We call this
S
a
p
-adic
{\cal V}
-formal family of log points.
Let us recall the formal scheme
\overline{S}
defined in
[
Nakk4
]
as follows.

Recall that, for the log structure
M_{S}
of
S
, we denote

M_{S}/{\cal O}_{S}^{*}
by
\overline{M}_{S}
.
Let
S=\bigcup_{i\in I}S_{i}
be an open covering of
S
such that

\overline{M}_{S_{i}}\simeq{\mathbb{N}}
. Take local sections

t_{i}
’s in
\Gamma(S_{i},M_{S})
’s such that
the images of
t_{i}
’s in
\Gamma(S_{i},\overline{M}_{S})
’s are generators.
Set
S_{ij}:=S_{i}\cap S_{j}
.
Then there exist unique sections
u_{ji}\in\Gamma(S_{ij},{\cal O}^{*}_{S})
such that

(5.0.1)

t_{j}|_{S_{ij}}=u_{ji}t_{i}|_{S_{ij}}

in
\Gamma(S_{i}\cap S_{j},M_{S})
for all
i
,
j
’s.
Consider the
{\cal V}
-formal scheme

\widehat{\mathbb{A}}{}^{1}_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{i}}=\underline{\rm Spf}_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{i}}({\cal O}_{S_{i}}\{\tau_{i}\})

and the log
{\cal V}
-formal scheme

(\widehat{\mathbb{A}}{}^{1}_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{i}},({\mathbb{N}}\owns n\longmapsto\tau_{i}^{n}\in{\cal O}_{S_{i}}\{\tau_{i}\})^{a})
.
Denote this log
p
-adic
{\cal V}
-formal scheme by
\overline{S}_{i}
.
Then, by patching
\overline{S}_{i}
and
\overline{S}_{j}
along

\overline{S}_{ij}:=\overline{S}_{i}\cap\overline{S}_{j}
by the following equation

(5.0.2)

\tau_{j}|_{\overline{S}_{ij}}=u_{ji}\tau_{i}|_{\overline{S}_{ij}},

we have the log
p
-adic
{\cal V}
-formal scheme
\overline{S}=\bigcup_{i\in I}\overline{S}_{i}
.
The isomorphism class of the log formal scheme
\overline{S}

is independent of the choice of the system of
generators
t_{i}
’s.
By using this easy fact and by considering
the refinement of two open coverings of
S
,
we see that the isomorphism class
of the log formal scheme
\overline{S}
is independent of
the choice of the open covering
S=\bigcup_{i\in I}S_{i}
.
Because

\overset{\circ}{\overline{S}}_{i}=\overset{\circ}{S}_{i}\times_{{\rm Spf}({\cal V})}{\rm Spf}({\cal V}\{\tau_{i}\})
,
the natural morphism
\overline{S}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
is formally log smooth
(cf.
[
Ka
, (3.5)]
).
In the following we denote
\tau_{i}
by
t_{i}
by convenience of notation.

By killing “
t_{i}
”’s in
{\cal O}_{\overline{S}}
,
we have the following natural exact closed immersion

(5.0.3)

S\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{S}

over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
The local section
d\log t_{i}\in\Omega^{1}_{\overline{S}_{i}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{i}}
defines a global section of

\Omega^{1}_{\overline{S}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
by the equation (
5.0.2
) since

d\log u_{ij}=0
in
\Omega^{1}_{\overline{S}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
.
We denote this global section by
d\log t
. We also denote by
d\log t
the image of

d\log t
by the surjective morphism
\Omega^{1}_{\overline{S}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{S/\overset{\circ}{S}}
.

Set
S_{1}:=\underline{\rm Spec}^{\log}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}/\pi{\cal O}_{S})
.
Let
Y/S_{1}
be a log smooth scheme.
Let
Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}
be an immersion into a log smooth
log
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme over
\overline{S}
.
Let
\overline{T}
be the prewidening over
\overline{S}
obtained by the
immersion
Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}
.
Set
{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}:=\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}\times_{\overline{S}}S
.
Let
T
be the prewidening over
S
obtained by the immersion

Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
.
Let
g\colon{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}\longrightarrow S
be the structural morphism.
Let
\{(\overline{V}_{n},\overline{T}_{n})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be the system of
the universal enlargements of the immersion

Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}
over
\overline{S}
.
Set
(V_{n},T_{n}):=(\overline{V}_{n},\overline{T}_{n})\times_{\overline{S}}S
.
Let
\beta_{n}\colon V_{n}\longrightarrow Y

be the natural morphism.
Let
F
be a flat isocrystal of
{\cal K}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}
-modules.
Let
(\overline{\cal F}_{n},\overline{\nabla}_{n})
be the flat coherent
{\cal K}_{\overline{T}_{n}}
-modules
with integrable connection obtained by
F
:

\overline{\nabla}_{n}\colon\overline{\cal F}_{n}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal F}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{\overline{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
.
Set
{\cal F}_{n}:=\overline{\cal F}_{n}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{\overline{S}}}{\cal O}_{S}
.
Since
dt=td\log t
for a local section
t
such that

{\cal O}_{\overline{S}}={\cal O}_{S}\{t\}
locally on
\overline{S}
,

\overline{\nabla}_{n}

induces the following integrable connection

\displaystyle{\nabla}_{n}\colon{\cal F}_{n}\longrightarrow{\cal F}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

and we obtain the projective system
\{{\nabla}_{n}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
and
the following integrable connection

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{n}{\nabla}_{n}\colon\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n*}{\cal F}_{n}\longrightarrow(\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n*}{\cal F}_{n})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}.

For simplicity of notation, set

\displaystyle{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}:=\{{\cal F}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}.

Let
L^{\rm UE}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}
and
L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

be the (log) convergent linearization functors
for coherent
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\overline{T}}}
-modules
with respect to the immersions

Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}

and
\overset{\circ}{Y}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}({\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}})^{\circ}

over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Then the morphism

\displaystyle(\overline{T}_{n})^{\circ}=({\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}(\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}))^{\circ}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{\overset{\circ}{Y},n}((\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex})^{\circ})

is an isomorphism and the assumption in (
4.4
) is satisfied.
Let

\epsilon_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}\colon((Y/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}})\longrightarrow((\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

be the morphism forgetting the log structure of
Y
over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Consider the complex

\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\overline{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}).

By (
4.5.1
) and the log Poincaré lemma of a vanishing cycle sheaf
((
4.7
)),
we have the following equality:

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\overline{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})=L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\overline{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})(\simeq R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\overline{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})).

However this complex is
not
our desired complex.
Instead we consider the following sheaves for various
i
’s:

(5.0.4)

\displaystyle L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

\displaystyle=L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(L^{\rm UE}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))

\displaystyle(\simeq R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(L^{\rm UE}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))).

Here note that

\Omega^{i}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\not=\Omega^{i}_{(\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex})^{\circ}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

and that
{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}
is not formally log smooth over

\overset{\circ}{S}
in general.

Lemma 5.1
.

The differential

(5.1.1)

\displaystyle\nabla^{i}\colon L^{\rm UE}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\overline{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\overline{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

induces the following differential
:

(5.1.2)

\displaystyle\nabla^{i}\colon\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\longrightarrow\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}).

Proof.

Let
(\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime},\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime})
be an object of
(\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}
.
Since
(\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex})^{\circ}
is formally smooth over
\overset{\circ}{S}
,
there exists a morphism
\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}\longrightarrow(\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex})^{\circ}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}

which lifts the composite morphism

\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{Y}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex})^{\circ}
.
This morphism induces a morphism

\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}
of prewideings of
\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Let

\displaystyle\overline{\varphi}{}^{*}:\{{\rm isocrystals~of~}{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\overline{T}}}{\rm-modules}\}\longrightarrow\{{\rm isocrystals~of~}{\cal K}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}|_{\overline{T}}{\rm-modules}\}

be the functor (
3.0.1
).
Consider the following diagram

(5.1.3)

\begin{CD}\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime},n}(\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overline{T})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}@>{\{\overline{p}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}>{}>\{\overline{T}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\\
@V{\{p^{\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}V{}V\\
\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}.\end{CD}

By (
4.1
) and (
4.2
) we have the following:

(5.1.4)

\displaystyle(\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})))_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=(\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}j_{\overline{T}*}\overline{\varphi}{}^{*}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=(j_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}*}(\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}})_{\overline{T}\,\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}{}*}\overline{\varphi}{}^{*}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=((\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*})_{\overline{T}\,\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}{}*}\overline{\varphi}{}^{*}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}}

\displaystyle=(\overline{\varphi}{}^{*}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}{\overline{T}}}

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}p^{\prime}_{n*}\overline{p}_{n}^{*}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}).

On the other hand, we have the following similarly:

(5.1.5)

\displaystyle(\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\overline{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=(\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}j_{\overline{T}*}\overline{\varphi}^{*}(\overline{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}p^{\prime}_{n*}\overline{p}_{n}^{*}(\overline{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}).

Because

dt=td\log t=0
in
{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Q}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
,
we have the set
\{\nabla^{i}\}_{i=0}^{\infty}
of
differentials in (
5.1.2
).
∎

Remark 5.2
.

The differential
\nabla^{i}
in (
5.1.2
) is not a special case of
the differential (
4.6.1
) in the case
{\cal Y}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}={\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}

since
{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
is not necessarily formally log smooth over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.

Corollary 5.3
.

The differential
(
5.1.2
)

induces the following differential
:

\displaystyle\nabla^{i}\colon L^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\longrightarrow L^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}).

Proof.

This follows from (
4.5
) and (
5.1
).
∎

By (
5.3
) we can consider the following complex

\displaystyle L^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\simeq\epsilon_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),

which is isomorphic to

R\epsilon_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm conv}_{Y_{/}\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})
((
4.4
)).
In §
8
below we consider
the cosimplicial version of this complex.

Lemma 5.4
.

Consider
T
as an object
of
(Y/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}

by using the morphism
S\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Let

\displaystyle\varphi^{*}_{/\overset{\circ}{S}}\colon

\displaystyle\{\text{the category of isocrystals of }{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}\text{-modules}\}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\{\text{the category of isocrystals of }{\cal K}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}|_{T}\text{-modules}\}

be a natural functor defined by

\varphi^{*}_{/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\{{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty})(T^{\prime})=\Gamma(T^{\prime},\psi_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n}))
for
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, where

\psi_{n}\colon T^{\prime}\longrightarrow T_{n}
is a morphism such that the composite morphism

T^{\prime}\longrightarrow T_{n}\longrightarrow T
is
the given morphism
for an object
T^{\prime}
of
{\rm Conv}(Y/\overset{\circ}{S})|_{T}
.
Let

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{\varphi}{}^{*}\colon

\displaystyle\{\text{the category of isocrystals of }{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}\text{-modules}\}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\{\text{the category of isocrystals of }{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}}|_{\overset{\circ}{T}}\text{-modules}\}

be a natural functor defined by

\overset{\circ}{\varphi}{}^{*}(\{{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty})(T^{\prime})=\Gamma(T^{\prime},\psi_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n}))
for
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, where

\psi_{n}\colon T^{\prime}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}_{n}
is a morphism
such that the composite morphism

T^{\prime}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}_{n}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}
is
the given morphism
for an object
T^{\prime}
of
{\rm Conv}(\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S})|_{\overset{\circ}{T}}
.

(
Note that, because the immersion
Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
is exact,

\{\overset{\circ}{T}_{n}:=(T_{n})^{\circ}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
is the system of
the universal enlargements of the immersion

\overset{\circ}{Y}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}({\cal Q}^{\rm ex})^{\circ}.)

Then

(5.4.1)

\displaystyle L_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}^{\rm UE}({\cal E})=j_{T*}\varphi^{*}_{/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\{{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty})\in(Y/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv},

(5.4.2)

\displaystyle L_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}}^{\rm UE}({\cal E})=j_{\overset{\circ}{T}*}\overset{\circ}{\varphi}{}^{*}(\{{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty})\in(\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}.

Proof.

For an object
(U^{\prime},T^{\prime})
of
(Y/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}
,
the morphism
U^{\prime}\longrightarrow Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{T}

factors through a morphism
U^{\prime}\longrightarrow Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T
.
Hence
U^{\prime}=U^{\prime}\times_{(T^{\prime}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overline{T})}(T^{\prime}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}T)
.
Consider the following diagram:

(5.4.3)

\begin{CD}\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U^{\prime},n}(T^{\prime}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overline{T})\times_{\overline{T}_{n}}T_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}@>{\{p_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}>{}>\{T_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}@>{}>{}>T\\
@V{\{p^{\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U^{\prime},n}(T^{\prime}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overline{T})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}@>{\{\overline{p}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}>{}>\{\overline{T}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}@>{}>{}>\overline{T}\\
@V{\{\overline{p}^{\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}V{}V\\
T^{\prime}.\end{CD}

The right square in (
5.4.3
) is cartesian.
By (
2.3
),

(5.4.4)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{T}}_{U^{\prime},n}(T^{\prime}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}T)=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U^{\prime},n}(\overline{T}{}^{\prime}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overline{T})}\times_{\overline{T}}T.

Hence we have the following:

(5.4.5)

\displaystyle L_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}^{\rm UE}({\cal E})_{T^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}\overline{p}{}^{\prime}_{n*}\overline{p}_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n})=\varprojlim_{n}p^{\prime}_{n*}p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n}).

The last projective limit in (
5.4.5
) is equal to

j_{T*}\varphi^{*}_{/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\{{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty})\in(Y/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}
.
We obtain the formula (
5.4.2
) similarly.
∎

The lemma (
5.4
)
implies that the linearizations
L_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}
and
L_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

are equal to the linearizations with respect to the immersions

Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}
and

\overset{\circ}{Y}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}
, respectively, for
coherent
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}
-moduless.
Note that
{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}
and

({\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex})^{\circ}

are not necessarily log smooth over
\overset{\circ}{S}

and not smooth over
\overset{\circ}{S}
, respectively.

Example 5.5
.

In §
8
below we consider the following two cases for
a
{\cal V}
-formal family
S
of log points and an SNCL scheme
Y
over
S_{1}
:

(1)

\epsilon_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S}:=\epsilon_{(\overset{\circ}{X},M_{X},{\cal O}_{X}^{*})/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}=:\epsilon_{X/S}.

(2)

\epsilon_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S}:=\epsilon_{(\overset{\circ}{X},M_{X},{\cal O}_{X}^{*})/\overset{\circ}{S}/\overset{\circ}{S}}=:\epsilon_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}.

(The latter case is quite less familiar than the former case.)

6
Log convergent linearization functors of SNCL schemes

In this section we show several properties of
log convergent linearization functors of SNCL schemes.
These results are SNCL versions of results in
[
NS2
, §6]

in which several properties of the log convergent linearization functor of
a smooth scheme with a relative SNCD have been shown.

For a log smooth log formal scheme
{\cal Q}
over a log formal scheme
T

and for a nonnegative integer
i
and an integer
k
,
set

(6.0.1)

P_{k}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}/T}=\begin{cases}0&(k<0),\\
{\rm Im}(\Omega^{k}_{{\cal Q}/T}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{\cal Q}}\Omega^{i-k}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}/T}\longrightarrow\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}/T})&(0\leq k\leq i),\\
\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}/T}&(k>i).\end{cases}

Then we have a filtration

P:=\{P_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

on
\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}/T}
.

In this section we assume that there exists an immersion

X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}

into a formally log smooth log
{\cal V}
-formal scheme over
\overline{S}
.
Let
X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}

be the exactification of this immersion.

Set
{\cal P}:=\overline{\cal P}\times_{\overline{S}}S
and
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}:=\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}\times_{\overline{S}}S
.
Then
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
is the exactification of
the immersion
X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
.

For a finitely generated monoid
Q

and a set
\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\}

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
of generators of
Q
,
we say that
\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\}
is
minimal
if
there does not exit a generator
\{q^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,q^{\prime}_{r}\}
of
Q
such that
r<n
.
Let
Y=(\overset{\circ}{Y},M_{Y})
be
an fs(=fine and saturated) log (formal) scheme.
Let
y
be a point of
\overset{\circ}{Y}
.
Let
m_{1,y},\ldots,m_{r,y}
be local sections of
M_{Y}
around
y

whose images in
M_{Y,y}/{\cal O}_{Y,y}^{*}

form a minimal set of generators of
M_{Y,y}/{\cal O}_{Y,y}^{*}
.
Let
D(M_{Y})_{i}

(1\leq i\leq r)

be the local closed subscheme of

\overset{\circ}{Y}
defined by the ideal sheaf generated by
the image of
m_{i,y}
in
{\cal O}_{Y}
.
For a nonnegative integer
k
,
let
D^{(k+1)}(M_{Y})
be the disjoint union of
the
(k+1)
-fold intersections of different
D(M_{Y})_{i}
’s.
Assume that

(6.0.2)

M_{Y,y}/{\cal O}^{*}_{Y,y}\simeq{\mathbb{N}}^{r}

for any point
y
of
\overset{\circ}{Y}

and for some
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
depending on
y
.
It is easy to show that the scheme
D^{(k+1)}(M_{Y})
is
independent of the choice of
m_{1,y},\ldots,m_{r,y}

and it is globalized (cf.
[
Nakk3
, §4]
).
We denote this globalized scheme
by the same symbol
D^{(k+1)}(M_{Y})
.
Set
D^{(0)}(M_{Y}):=\overset{\circ}{Y}
.
Let
c^{(k+1)}\colon D^{(k+1)}(M_{Y})\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{Y}

be the natural morphism.

As in
[
D1
, (3.1.4)]
and
[
NS1
, (2.2.18)]
,
we have an orientation sheaf
\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(k+1)}(D(M_{Y}))

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})
associated to the set of
D(M_{Y})_{i}
’s.
We have the following equality

(6.0.3)

c^{(k+1)}_{*}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(k+1)}(D(M_{Y}))=\bigwedge^{k+1}(M_{Y}^{\rm gp}/{\cal O}_{Y}^{*})

as sheaves of abelian groups on
\overset{\circ}{Y}
.

The following has been proved in
[
Nakk3
]
:

Proposition 6.1

(
[
Nakk3
, (4.3)]
).

Let
g\colon Y\longrightarrow Y^{\prime}
be a morphism of fs log schemes
satisfying the condition
(
6.0.2
)
.
Assume that, for each point
y\in\overset{\circ}{Y}

and for each member
m
of the minimal generators
of
M_{Y,y}/{\cal O}^{*}_{Y,y}
, there exists
a unique member
m^{\prime}
of the minimal generators of

M_{Y^{\prime},\overset{\circ}{g}(y)}/{\cal O}^{*}_{Y^{\prime},\overset{\circ}{g}(y)}

such that
g^{*}(m^{\prime})\in m^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}}
.
Then there exists a canonical morphism

g^{(k+1)}\colon D^{(k+1)}(M_{Y})\longrightarrow D^{(k+1)}(M_{Y^{\prime}})
fitting
into the following commutative diagram of schemes
:

(6.1.1)

\begin{CD}D^{(k+1)}(M_{Y})@>{g^{(k+1)}}>{}>D^{(k+1)}(M_{Y^{\prime}})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\overset{\circ}{Y}@>{\overset{\circ}{g}}>{}>\overset{\circ}{Y}{}^{\prime}.\end{CD}

When
Y=X
,
we denote
D^{(k+1)}(M_{Y})
by
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}
and

\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(k+1)}(D(M_{Y}))
by

\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{X}/S_{1})
.
The sheaf
\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{X}/S_{1})
is
extended to an abelian sheaf

\varpi^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/S)

in the convergent topos
(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}
.
We call

\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{X}/S_{1})
and
\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/S)

the
zariskian orientation sheaf
of

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
and the
convergent orientation sheaf

of
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}
, respectively.

Because
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
is a formal SNCL scheme over
S
(
[
Nakk4
, (1.14.1)]
),
we can define
(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex})^{(k)}
as usual and we see that

D^{(k+1)}(M_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}})=D^{(k+1)}(M_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}})=\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}.

Let

a^{(k)}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}
and

b^{(k)}:\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}

be the morphisms induced by the natural closed immersions.

Let the middle objects in the following table
be the prewidenings obtained by
the left immersions over
S
and
\overset{\circ}{S}
and
let the right objects be the systems
of the universal enlargements of
these prewidenings:

X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\rm ex}

T

\overset{\to}{T}:=\{T_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

over
S

X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}

\overline{T}

\overset{\to}{\overline{T}}:=\{\overline{T}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

over
\overset{\circ}{S}

\overset{\circ}{X}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}

\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}

\overset{\to}{\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}}:=\{\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

over
\overset{\circ}{S}

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}

\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{(k)}

\overset{\to}{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{(k)}}:=\{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{(k)}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

over
\overset{\circ}{S}

Let
g_{n}\colon T_{n}\longrightarrow{\cal P}^{\rm ex}

and

\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{(k)}_{n}\colon\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{(k)}_{n}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})

be the natural morphisms.
Let
c^{(k)}_{n}\colon\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{(k)}_{n}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}_{n}

be the morphism induced by

b^{(k)}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}
.

Lemma 6.2

(
cf.
[
NS1
, (2.2.16) (1)]
)
.

The natural morphism

\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{(k)}_{n}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}_{n}\times_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex})^{(k)}=\overset{\circ}{{T}}_{n}\times_{\overset{\circ}{{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex})^{(k)}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Since the defining ideal of the immersion
\overset{\circ}{X}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}
has no relation with
the ideal of the image of the log structure of

\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}
, the proof is similar to that of
[
NS2
, (6.6)]
.
∎

Set

X_{n}:=X\times_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}T_{n}

and

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}_{n}:=\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}\times_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}}\overset{\circ}{T}{}_{n}=\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}\times_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}\overset{\circ}{T}{}_{n}

(n\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
As usual, we denote the left representable objects
in the following table
by the right ones for simplicity of notation:

(X_{n}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T_{n})\in(X/S)_{\rm conv}

T_{n}

(X_{n}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{T}_{n})\in(X/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}

\overline{T}_{n}

(\overset{\circ}{X}_{n}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}_{n})\in(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}

\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}_{n}

(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}_{n}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{(k)}_{n})\in(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}

\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{(k)}_{n}

Let

j_{T}:(X/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T}\longrightarrow(X/S)_{\rm conv}

j_{\overline{T}}:(X/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}|_{\overline{T}}\longrightarrow(X/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}

j_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}}:(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}|_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}}\longrightarrow(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}

j_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{(k)}}:(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}|_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{(k)}}\longrightarrow(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}

be localization functors and let

\varphi^{*}:\{{\rm isocrystals~of~}{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}{\rm-modules}\}\longrightarrow

\{{\rm isocrystals~of~}{\cal K}_{X/S}|_{T}{\rm-modules}\}

\overline{\varphi}{}^{*}:\{{\rm isocrystals~of~}{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\overline{T}}}{\rm-modules}\}\longrightarrow

\{{\rm isocrystals~of~}{\cal K}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}|_{\overline{T}}{\rm-modules}\}

\overset{\circ}{\overline{\varphi}}{}^{*}:\{{\rm isocrystals~of~}{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{{\overline{T}}}}{\rm-modules}\}\longrightarrow

\{{\rm isocrystals~of~}{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}|_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}}{\rm-modules}\}

\overset{\circ}{\varphi}{}^{(k)*}:\{{\rm isocrystals~of~}{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{(k)}}}{\rm-modules}\}\longrightarrow

\{{\rm isocrystals~of~}{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}|_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{(k)}}{\rm-modules}\}

be the morphisms of pull-backs
defined in (
3.0.1
).
Note that

(6.2.1)

\displaystyle\{{\rm isocrystals}~{\rm of}~{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}{\rm-modules}\}\subset\{{\rm isocrystals}~{\rm of}~{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{{\overline{T}}}}{\rm-modules}\}.

Note also that isocrystals of

{\cal K}_{X/S}|_{T}
-modules e.t.c.
are assumed to be coherent.

Let
{\cal E}:=\{{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be
a coherent
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}
-module.
Let
{\cal F}:=\{{\cal F}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be
a coherent
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\overline{T}}}
-module.
Let
{\cal G}:=\{{\cal G}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

be a coherent
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{(k)}}}
-module.
Set

L_{X/S}^{\rm UE}({\cal E}):=j_{T*}\varphi^{*}(\{{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty})\in(X/S)_{\rm conv},

L_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}^{\rm UE}({\cal F}):=j_{\overline{T}*}\overline{\varphi}^{*}(\{{\cal F}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty})\in(X/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv},

L_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}^{\rm UE}({\cal F}):=j_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}*}\overset{\circ}{\overline{\varphi}}{}^{*}(\{{\cal F}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty})\in(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}

and

L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal G}):=j_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{(k)}*}\overset{\circ}{\varphi}{}^{(k)*}(\{{\cal G}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty})\in(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}.

Let
E
be a flat isocrystal of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
-modules.
Let
\overline{\cal E}:=\{\overline{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be
a flat coherent
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}}}
-module
corresponding to
E
.
Since
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}}}={\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{{\overline{T}}}}
,

\overline{\cal E}:=\{\overline{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
is also
a flat coherent
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\overset{\circ}{\overline{T}}}}
-module
corresponding to
\epsilon_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}^{*}(E)
.
Let
{\cal E}:=\{{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be
a flat coherent
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}
-module
corresponding to
\epsilon_{X/S}^{*}(E)
.
Then
{\cal E}_{n}=\overline{\cal E}_{n}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{\overline{S}}}{\cal O}_{S}
.
Let
{\cal E}^{(k)}:=\{{\cal E}^{(k)}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be a flat coherent

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{(k)}}}
-module corresponding to
a^{(k)*}_{\rm crys}(E)
.
By (
3.4
) we have a complex

L^{\rm UE}_{X/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S})
of

{\cal K}_{X/S}
-modules.
By the log convergent Poincaré lemma ((
3.5
)),
we have a natural quasi-isomorphism

(6.2.2)

\epsilon_{X/S}^{*}(E)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}L^{\rm UE}_{X/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}).

Similarly we have the following quasi-isomorphism:

(6.2.3)

a^{(k)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{(k)}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(k)}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex})^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}).

Let us recall the morphism

\epsilon_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}\colon((X/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}})\longrightarrow((\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

forgetting the log structure of
X
over
\overset{\circ}{S}
((
5.5
)).
By (
5.1
)
the differential

(6.2.4)

\displaystyle\nabla^{i}\colon L^{\rm UE}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\overline{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\overline{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

induces the following differential
:

(6.2.5)

\displaystyle\nabla^{i}\colon\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\longrightarrow\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}).

Hence we have the following complex by (
5.3
):

(6.2.6)

\displaystyle L^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}).

By (
5.4
) the linearization in (
6.2.6
) can be calculated
not only with respect to the immersion

\overset{\circ}{X}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex})^{\circ}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}

but also
with respect to the immersion

\overset{\circ}{X}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}({\cal P}^{\rm ex})^{\circ}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
In the following we calculate the linearization in (
6.2.6
)
with respect to the latter immersion.

The family

\{P_{k}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\}_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}

gives a subcomplex

P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

of
\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
.
Since the following diagram

\begin{CD}X@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}\\
@V{\epsilon_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\overset{\circ}{X}@>{\subset}>{}>(\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex})^{\circ}\end{CD}

is commutative, the following diagram is commutative by (
4.9
):

\begin{CD}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\overline{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})@>{\nabla^{i}}>{}>\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\overline{\cal E}\otimes_{\overline{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\overline{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{(\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex})^{\circ}/\overset{\circ}{S}})@>{\nabla^{i}}>{}>L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\overline{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{(\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex})^{\circ}/\overset{\circ}{S}}).\end{CD}

Hence we have the following commutative diagram by (
5.3
):

\begin{CD}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})@>{\nabla^{i}}>{}>\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\\
\Big\|\Big\|\\
L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})@>{\nabla^{i}}>{}>L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{({\cal P}{}^{\rm ex})^{\circ}/\overset{\circ}{S}})@>{\nabla^{i}}>{}>L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{({\cal P}{}^{\rm ex})^{\circ}/\overset{\circ}{S}}).\end{CD}

Since the connection
\nabla^{0}\colon L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\overline{\cal E})\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\overline{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{(\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex})^{\circ}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

has no log poles, so is

\nabla^{0}\colon L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E})\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{({\cal P}{}^{\rm ex})^{\circ}/\overset{\circ}{S}})
.
Consequently we indeed have a complex

P_{k}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}):=L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}(P_{k}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))\quad(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

Here note that we cannot obtain the complex
“
L^{\rm UE}_{X/S}(P_{k}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))
”.

As in
[
NS2
, (6.7)]
, the following theorem (2) is
one of key theorems in this paper.

Theorem 6.3
.

(1)

The natural morphism

(6.3.1)

{\cal E}_{n}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\longrightarrow{\cal E}_{n}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

is injective.

(2)
The natural morphism

(6.3.2)

P_{k}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

is injective.

Proof.

(1): (1) immediately follows from (
3.2
).

(2):
This is a local problem.
Let
(\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime},\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime},\overset{\circ}{\iota},\overset{\circ}{u})
be an object of

{\rm Conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})
.
We may assume that
\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}
is affine.
Since
\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}

is formally smooth over
\overset{\circ}{S}
,
we have a morphism
\overline{e}\colon\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}

such that the composite morphism

\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}

is equal to the composite morphism

\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}
.
Let
r\colon\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}

be the second projection.
Let
T^{\prime}
be a log formal scheme
whose underlying formal scheme is
\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}
and
whose log structure is the inverse image of that of
S
.
Set
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}:=\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}

(by abuse of notation),

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}:={\cal P}^{\rm ex}\times_{S}T^{\prime}
and

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}:={\cal P}^{\rm ex}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}
.
Then
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}
is
a formal SNCL scheme over
T^{\prime}
and

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}:=\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}
.
We have the following diagram:

(6.3.3)

\begin{CD}\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}\\
@A{\{p^{\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}A{}A\\
\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime},n}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}@>{\{p_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}>{}>\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{\overset{\circ}{X},n}(\overset{\circ}{{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}=\{\overset{\circ}{T}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\\
@V{\{q^{\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}V{}V@V{}V{\{q_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}V\\
\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}@>{r}>{}>\overset{\circ}{{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}.\\
\end{CD}

By the remark after (
6.2.6
) and (
3.0.5
),

\displaystyle(P_{k}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=(P_{k}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}p^{\prime}_{n*}p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T_{n}}}q_{n}^{*}(P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}p^{\prime}_{n*}\{p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n})\otimes_{p_{n}^{*}({\cal O}_{T_{n}})}p_{n}^{*}q_{n}^{*}(P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\}

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}p^{\prime}_{n*}\{p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n})\otimes_{q_{n}^{\prime}\!{}^{*}r^{*}({\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}})}q_{n}^{\prime}\!{}^{*}r^{*}(P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\}.

Because
\varprojlim_{n}
and
p^{\prime}_{n*}
are left exact
and because
q_{n}^{\prime}\!{}^{*}
is left exact by (
3.2
),
we have only to prove that the morphism

r^{*}(P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\longrightarrow r^{*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})
is injective.
This injectivity is clear because

\displaystyle r^{*}(P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})=P_{k}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}/{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}}.

∎

By (
6.3
) (2), the family

\{P_{k}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

of
{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
-modules
defines a filtration on the complex

L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})
.
Hence we obtain an object

(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),\{P_{k}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}})

in
{\rm C}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{X/S})
.
For simplicity of notation, we denote it by

(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),P).

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
k
be a positive integer.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}
is affine over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Then there exist the following quasi-isomorphisms

(6.4.1)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}

\displaystyle L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\overset{\sim}{\longleftarrow}a^{(k-1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(k-1)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-1)}_{{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))[-k]

in
D^{+}({\cal K}_{X/S})
.

Proof.

Let

b^{(k)}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{(k)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal P}

be the natural morphism.
Then, as in the proof of
[
Nakk4
, (1.3.14)]
,
we have the following isomorphism

(6.4.2)

{\rm Res}\colon{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}b^{(k-1)}_{*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k-1)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-1)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}))[-k]\quad(k\geq 1)

by using the immersion
X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}
over
\overline{S}
.
Hence, by (
6.3
) (2) and (
3.3
) and by the SNCL version of
the proof of
[
NS2
, (6.9)]
,
we have the isomorphism (
6.4.1
).
∎

For the case
k=0
, we obtain the following:

Proposition 6.5
.

There exists the following isomorphism
:

(6.5.1)

\displaystyle P_{0}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle(a^{(0)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(0)*}_{{\rm conv}*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\longrightarrow a^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(1)*}_{{\rm conv}*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\longrightarrow\cdots)

\displaystyle={\rm Ker}(a^{(0)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(0)*}_{{\rm conv}*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\longrightarrow a^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(1)*}_{{\rm conv}*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))).

Proof.

The following proof is a slightly simpler log convergent version of
[
Nakk4
, (1.3.21)]
.

Let the notations be as in the proof of (
6.3
).
Set

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}_{T^{\prime}}:=\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
Let

b^{(k)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}_{T^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{T^{\prime}}

and

c^{(k)}_{n\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime}{}^{(k)},n}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}_{T^{\prime}})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime},n}(\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex})

be the natural morphisms.
Consider the following commutative diagram:

(6.5.2)

\begin{CD}\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime}{}^{(k)},n}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}_{T^{\prime}})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}@>{\{q^{\prime}{}^{(k)}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}>{}>\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}_{T^{\prime}}\\
@V{\{c^{(k)}_{n\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}V{}V@V{}V{b^{(k)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}}V\\
\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime},n}(\overset{\circ}{{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}@>{\{q^{\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}>{}>\overset{\circ}{{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}\\
@V{\{p_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}V{}V\\
\{T_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}.\end{CD}

and the diagram (
6.3.3
).
By (
6.2
) and (
2.3
) we see that the square in (
6.5.2
)
is a cartesian diagram.
Then
c^{(0)}_{n\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}
induces the following morphism of complexes:

(6.5.3)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}P_{0}(p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}/T^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}\{c^{(0)}_{n\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}*}(c^{(0)*}_{n\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}(p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n}))\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(0)}_{T^{\prime}}}}}q^{\prime}{}^{(0)*}_{n}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(0)}_{T^{\prime}}/\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}/\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}{c}{}^{(1)}_{n\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}*}(c^{(1)*}_{n\overset{\circ}{T^{\prime}}}(p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n}))\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(1)}_{T^{\prime}}}}q^{\prime}{}^{(1)*}_{n}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(1)}_{T^{\prime}}/\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}/\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime})))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow\cdots\}.

We claim that the whole complex (
6.5.3
) is exact.
Indeed, by
[
DI
, (4.2.2) (a), (c)]

we have the following exact sequence

(6.5.4)

0\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}/\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}/{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}}/\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}})\longrightarrow b^{(0)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{(0),{\rm ex}}_{T^{\prime}}/\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}/\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}))\longrightarrow\cdots.

By (
3.2
) we have the following exact sequence

(6.5.5)

0\longrightarrow q^{\prime*}_{n}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}/{T^{\prime}}}/{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}/{T^{\prime}}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}))\longrightarrow q^{\prime}{}^{*}_{n}(b^{(0)}_{T^{\prime\prime}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{(0),{\rm ex}}_{T^{\prime}}/T^{\prime}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}/\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}))\longrightarrow\cdots.

Here note that

\Omega^{i}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}/T^{\prime}}/\Omega^{i}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}/T^{\prime}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}})

(i\in{\mathbb{N}})
is an
{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}}
-module.
By
[
Nakk4
, (1.3.21) (4), (1.3.12.1)]
we have
the following isomorphism:

(6.5.6)

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}/T^{\prime}}/\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}/T^{\prime}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}})\simeq P_{0}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}/T^{\prime}}/\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}/T^{\prime}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}})=P_{0}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}/\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}.

Because
E
is a flat
{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
-module,

\overline{\cal E}_{n}
is a flat
{\cal K}_{\overline{T}_{n}}
-module.
Hence
{\cal E}_{n}
is a flat
{\cal K}_{T_{n}}
-module and
we have the following exact sequence of
{\cal K}_{T_{n}}
-modules:

(6.5.7)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\{p_{n}({\cal E}_{n})\otimes_{q^{\prime}{}^{*}_{n}({\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}})}q^{\prime}{}^{*}_{n}(P_{0}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}/{T^{\prime}}})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\{p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n})\otimes_{q^{\prime}{}^{*}_{n}({\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}})}q^{\prime}{}^{*}_{n}(b^{(0)}_{T^{\prime}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{(0)}_{T^{\prime}}/{T^{\prime}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}_{T^{\prime}}/\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime})))\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\longrightarrow\cdots.

Because
b^{(k)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}}
is an affine morphism,
the affine base change theorem tells us that
the target of the morphism (
6.5.7
) is equal to

\displaystyle\{p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n})\otimes_{q^{\prime}{}^{*}_{n}({\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}})}c^{(0)}_{n\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}*}q^{\prime}{}^{(0)*}_{n}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{(0)}_{T^{\prime}}/{T^{\prime}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}_{T^{\prime}}/\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime})))\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\longrightarrow\cdots.

Because the exactness in question is a local problem, we may neglect the orientation sheaves

\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}_{T^{\prime}}/\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime})
’s.
By considering this
neglectance into account, the target is isomorphic to

\displaystyle\{p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n})\otimes_{q^{\prime}{}^{*}_{n}({\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}})}c^{(0)}_{n\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}*}q^{\prime}{}^{(0)*}_{n}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{(0)}_{T^{\prime}}/{T^{\prime}}})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\longrightarrow\cdots.

For a coherent crystal
\{H_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
of

\{{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime},n}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}})}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
-modules,

R^{1}\varprojlim_{n}H_{n}=0
by the log version of
[
O2
, (3.8)]

which follows from the proof of [loc. cit.].
Hence the sequence (
6.5.3
) is exact.

Because the right hand side of (
6.5.3
) with the abbreviation of the orientation sheaves
is equal to

\{a^{(0)}_{{\rm conv}*}(L_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(0)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}(c^{(0)*}({\cal E})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{(0),{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{(0),{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))\}_{T}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow,

the right hand side is equal to

a^{(0)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(0)*}_{{\rm conv}}(E))\longrightarrow a^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(1)*}_{{\rm conv}}(E))\longrightarrow\cdots

by the convergent Poincaré lemma (
[
O2
, (5.6)]
,
[
NS2
, (3.7)]
).

The equality in (
6.5.1
) follows from the argument above.
∎

For simplicity of notation, set

({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}},P):=(\{{\cal E}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\}_{n=1}^{\infty},\{\{{\cal E}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}).

Proposition 6.6
.

Let
\beta_{n}\colon X_{n}\longrightarrow X
be a natural morphism
and
identify
(T_{n})_{{\rm zar}}
with
(X_{n})_{\rm zar}
.
Then

(6.6.1)

Ru^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(L^{\rm UE}_{X/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),P)=\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n*}(({\cal E}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}},P))

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of
[
NS2
, (3.9)]
.
∎

The following is an SNCL analogue of
[
Nakk3
, (5.13)]
,
whose proof is the same as that of [loc. cit.]:

Proposition 6.7
.

Let
{\cal V}^{\prime}
,
\pi^{\prime}
,
\kappa^{\prime}
and
S^{\prime}

be similar objects to
{\cal V}
,
\pi

\kappa
and
S

as in
§
2
, respectively.
Let
X^{\prime}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}

be a similar closed immersion over
\overline{S}{}^{\prime}
to
X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}
over
\overline{S}
.
Let
a^{\prime}{}^{(k)}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\prime(k)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\prime}

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})

be the natural morphism of log schemes over
S^{\prime}_{1}
.
Assume that there exists the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}X@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal P}\\
@V{g}V{}V@V{}V{{\mathfrak{g}}}V\\
X^{\prime}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}\end{CD}

over
\overline{S}\longrightarrow\overline{S}{}^{\prime}
.
Let
E^{\prime}
be a similar isocrystal to
E
in

((\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}})
.
Let
\Phi\colon\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}_{\rm conv}(E^{\prime})\longrightarrow E
be a morphism of isocrystals of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}}
-modules.
Let
{\cal M}^{\rm ex}
and
{\cal M}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}
be
the log structures of
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
and
{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}
,
respectively.
Assume that, for each point
x\in\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}

and for each member
m
of the minimal generators
of
{\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{x}/{\cal O}^{*}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex},x}
,
there exists a unique member
m^{\prime}
of the minimal generators of

{\cal M}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}_{\overset{\circ}{g}(x)}/{\cal O}^{*}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex},\overset{\circ}{g}(x)}

such that
g^{*}(m^{\prime})=m
and such that the image of
the other minimal generators of

{\cal M}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}_{\overset{\circ}{g}(x)}/{\cal O}^{*}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex},\overset{\circ}{g}(x)}
by
g^{*}

are the trivial element of

{\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{x}/{\cal O}^{*}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex},x}
.
Then the following diagram is commutative
:

(6.7.1)

\begin{CD}{\rm Res}:{\mathfrak{g}}_{{\rm conv}*}{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})@>{\sim}>{}>\\
@A{}A{}A\\
{\rm Res}:{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}}({\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}})@>{\sim}>{}>\end{CD}

\begin{CD}{\mathfrak{g}}_{{\rm conv}*}a^{(k-1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(L_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k-1)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}{}^{(k-1)}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))[-k]\\
@A{}A{}A\\
a^{\prime}{}^{(k-1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(L_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}{}^{(k-1)}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}}({\cal E}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\prime\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{({\cal P}{}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex})^{\circ}{}^{(k-1)}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}))[-k].\end{CD}

Proof.

We leave the proof of this proposition to the reader because the proof is only
the SNCL analogue of that of

[
NS2
, (6.13)]
.
∎

7
Simplicial log convergent topoi

In this section we give the notations of simplicial log convergent topoi and
simplicial Zariski topoi for later sections.

Let the notations be as in the beginning of
§
2
.
Let
Y=\underset{i\in I}{\bigcup}Y_{i}

be an open covering of
Y
, where

I
is a (not necessarily finite) set.
Set
Y_{0}:=\coprod_{i\in I}Y_{i}
and

Y_{n}:={\rm cosk}_{0}^{Y}(Y_{0})_{n}:=\underset{n~{\rm pieces}}{\underbrace{Y_{0}\times_{Y}\cdots\times_{Y}Y_{0}}}
.
Then we have the simplicial log scheme
Y_{\bullet}=Y_{\bullet\in{\mathbb{N}}}
over
Y
.
We also have the ringed topos

(({Y_{\bullet}/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}/S})
.
Let

\pi^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}\colon({Y_{\bullet}/S})_{\rm conv}\longrightarrow({Y/S})_{\rm conv}

be the natural morphism of topoi.
The morphism

\pi^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}
induces the following morphism

(7.0.1)

\pi^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}\colon(({Y_{\bullet}/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}/S})\longrightarrow(({Y/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S})

of ringed topoi and a morphism of filtered derived categories:

(7.0.2)

R\pi^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}\colon{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}/S})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y/S}).

By using the composite morphisms
f_{i}\colon\overset{\circ}{Y}_{i}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{Y}\overset{\overset{\circ}{f}}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{S}

(i\in I)
, we
also have an analogous simplicial ringed topos

({\overset{\circ}{Y}}_{\bullet{\rm zar}},f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal K}_{S}))
and
an analogous morphism

(7.0.3)

\pi^{\rm zar}_{Y/S*}\colon({\overset{\circ}{Y}}_{\bullet{\rm zar}},f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal K}_{S}))\longrightarrow({\overset{\circ}{Y}}_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))

of ringed topoi.
We have the following morphism of filtered derived categories:

(7.0.4)

R\pi^{\rm zar}_{Y/S*}\colon{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal K}_{S}))\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S})).

Let
u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}\colon((Y/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S})\longrightarrow({\overset{\circ}{Y}}_{{\rm zar}},f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))

be the projection in (
2.0.3
).
We also have the following natural projection

(7.0.5)

u^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\bullet}/S}\colon(({Y_{\bullet}/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}/S})\longrightarrow({\overset{\circ}{Y}}_{\bullet{\rm zar}},f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal K}_{S})).

We have the following commutative diagram:

(7.0.6)

\begin{CD}{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}/S})@>{R\pi^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}}>{}>{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y/S})\\
@V{Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\bullet}/S*}}V{}V@V{}V{Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}}V\\
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal K}_{S}))@>{R\pi^{\rm zar}_{Y/S*}}>{}>{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S})).\end{CD}

Assume that we are given the commutative diagram
(
2.0.4
) and open coverings

\{Y_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
Y
and

\{Y^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
Y^{\prime}
, respectively,
such that
g
induces a morphism

g_{i}\colon Y^{\prime}_{i}\longrightarrow Y_{i}
of log schemes.
Then the family
\{g_{i}\}_{i\in I}

induces the natural morphism

g_{\bullet}\colon Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow Y_{\bullet}

of simplicial log schemes over the morphism
Y^{\prime}\longrightarrow Y
.
Hence we obtain the following commutative diagram:

(7.0.7)

\begin{CD}{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}/S^{\prime}})@>{Rg_{\bullet{\rm conv}*}}>{}>{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}/S})\\
@V{R\pi^{\rm conv}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}*}}V{}V@V{}V{R\pi^{\rm zar}_{Y/S*}}V\\
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})@>{Rg_{{\rm conv}*}}>{}>{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y/S}).\end{CD}

We also have the following commutative diagram

(7.0.8)

\begin{CD}{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{\prime}{}^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal K}_{S^{\prime}}))@>{Rg_{\bullet{\rm zar}*}}>{}>{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal K}_{S}))\\
@V{R\pi^{\rm zar}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}*}}V{}V@V{}V{R\pi^{\rm zar}_{Y/S*}}V\\
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{\prime}{}^{-1}({\cal K}_{S^{\prime}}))@>{Rg_{{\rm zar}*}}>{}>{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S})).\end{CD}

Let
M
be the log structure of
Y
.
Let
N
be a sub log structure of
M
.
Let
M_{i}
(resp.
N_{i}
) be the pull-back of

M
(resp.
N
) to
Y_{i}

(i\in I)
.
Let

\epsilon_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S}\colon Y=(\overset{\circ}{Y},M)\longrightarrow(\overset{\circ}{Y},N)

be the morphism forgetting the structure
M\setminus N

over a morphism
S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S
in §sec:logcd.
For simplicity of notation, denote
(\overset{\circ}{Y},M)
and

(\overset{\circ}{Y},N)
by
Y^{\prime}
and
Y
, respectively.
The morphism
\epsilon_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S}

induces the following morphisms of ringed topoi:

(7.0.9)

\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\bullet},M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})/S^{\prime}/S}\colon((Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}/S^{\prime}})\longrightarrow((Y_{\bullet}/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}/S}).

We have the following commutative diagram

(7.0.10)

\begin{CD}((Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}/S^{\prime}})@>{\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\bullet},M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})/S^{\prime}/S}}>{}>((Y_{\bullet}/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}/S})\\
@V{\pi^{\rm conv}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}}V{}V@V{}V{\pi^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}}V\\
((Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})@>{\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S^{\prime}/S}}>{}>((Y/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S}).\end{CD}

8
Modified
P
-filtered convergent complexes and
p
-adic semi-purity

In this section we define the modified
P
-filtered convergent complex of an isocrystal
on the underlying scheme of an SNCL scheme in characteristic
p>0
.
This filtered complex is the first fundamenetal complex in this paper.
Especially it plays a central role in the construction of
the convergent Steenbrink filtered complex, which will be proved in later sections.

Let
S
be a
{\cal V}
-formal family of log points.
Let
Y/S_{1}
be a log smooth scheme.
Let
g\colon Y\longrightarrow S
be the structural morphism.
Let
Y=\bigcup_{i\in I}Y_{i}
be an affine open covering of
Y
.
Let
Y_{\bullet}
be the simplicial log scheme obtained by this affine open covering of
Y
.

Let
\star
be nothing or
\bullet
.
Let

(8.0.1)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{Y_{\star}/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}\colon((Y_{\star}/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{\star}/S})\longrightarrow((Y_{\star}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{\star}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),

(8.0.2)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{Y_{\star}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\colon((Y_{\star}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{\star}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\longrightarrow((\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\star}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\star}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

and

(8.0.3)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{Y_{\star}/S}\colon((Y_{\star}/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{\star}/S})\longrightarrow((\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\star}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\star}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

be the morphisms forgetting the log structures.
Then
\epsilon_{Y_{\star}/S}=\epsilon_{Y_{\star}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\circ\epsilon_{Y_{\star}/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}
.

Let
Y_{i}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}_{i}
be an immersion into a log smooth
log affine
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme over
\overline{S}

such that the image of
\overline{\cal Q}_{i}
in
\overline{S}
is
a log affine
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme.
If
Y_{i}
is sufficiently small, this immersion indeed exists by
[
Nakk4
, (1.1.6)]

and the criterion of the log smoothness in
[
Ka
]
.
Set
\overline{\cal Q}_{0}:=\coprod_{i}\overline{\cal Q}_{i}
and

\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}:={\rm cosk}_{0}^{\overline{S}}(\overline{\cal Q}_{0})
.
The underlying formal scheme of
\overline{\cal Q}_{n}
is the disjoint union of
affine
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-schemes.
We have a simplicial immersion

Y_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}

into a log smooth log
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme over
\overline{S}
.
Set
{\cal Q}_{\bullet}:=\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{S}}S
.
Let
g_{\bullet}\colon{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow S
be the structural morphism.
Let
\{(\overline{V}_{\bullet n},\overline{T}_{\bullet n})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be the system of
the universal enlargements of the simplicial immersion

Y_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S}
.
Set
(V_{\bullet n},T_{\bullet n}):=(\overline{V}_{\bullet n},\overline{T}_{\bullet n})\times_{\overline{S}}S
.
Let
\beta_{\bullet n}\colon V_{\bullet n}\longrightarrow Y_{\bullet}

be the natural morphism.
Let
F
be a flat isocrystal of
{\cal K}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}
-modules.
Let
(\overline{\cal F}{}^{\bullet}_{n},\overline{\nabla}_{n})
be the flat coherent
{\cal K}_{\overline{T}_{\bullet n}}
-modules
with integrable connection obtained by
F
:

\overline{\nabla}_{n}\colon\overline{\cal F}{}^{\bullet}_{n}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal F}{}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{\overline{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
.
Set
{\cal F}_{n}:=\overline{\cal F}{}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{S}}}{\cal O}_{S}
.
Since
dt=td\log t
for a local section
t
such that

{\cal O}_{\overline{S}}={\cal O}_{S}\{t\}
locally on
\overline{S}
,

\overline{\nabla}_{n}

induces the following integrable connection

\displaystyle{\nabla}_{n}\colon{\cal F}^{\bullet}_{n}\longrightarrow{\cal F}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

and we obtain the projective system
\{{\nabla}_{n}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
and
