the following integrable connection

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{n}{\nabla}_{n}\colon\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{\bullet n*}{\cal F}_{n}\longrightarrow(\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{\bullet n*}{\cal F}_{n})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}.

For simplicity of notation, set

\displaystyle{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}:=\{{\cal F}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}.

Let
L^{\rm UE}_{Y_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
and
L^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

be the (log) convergent linearization functors with respect to the immersions

Y_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}

and
\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Consider the following sheaf

(8.0.4)

\displaystyle L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\simeq\epsilon_{Y_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

for
i\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
which is isomorphic to

R\epsilon_{Y_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})
((
4.4
)).
By (
5.3
)
we obtain the complex

L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}).

Let
L_{Y_{\bullet}/S}
be the log convergent linearization functor for the immersion

Y_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}_{\bullet}
over
S
.
Set

{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}=\{{\cal F}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
.

Theorem 8.1
.

Let
\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

be the morphism of ringed topoi for
\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}
in

(
7.0.1
)
.
Then the complex

R\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

is independent of the choice of an affine open covering of
Y
and
an immersion
Y_{i}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}_{i}

(i\in I)
into a log smooth
log affine
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme over
\overline{S}
. .

Proof.

Since the natural morphism

{\cal Q}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}
is an immersion over
S\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
,
the induced morphism

{\mathfrak{T}}_{X,n}({\cal Q})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{X,n}(\overline{\cal Q})
is an immersion.
Hence we obtain the following equality by the log Poincaré lemma (
3.5.1
)
and (
4.7
):

(8.1.1)

\displaystyle R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\bullet}/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y_{\bullet}/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\pi^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(F)))

\displaystyle=R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\bullet}/S*}(L^{\rm UE}_{Y_{\bullet}/S}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}))

\displaystyle=L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}).

Hence the following formula holds by the cohomological descent:

(8.1.2)

\displaystyle R\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}))

\displaystyle=R\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\bullet}/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y_{\bullet}/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\pi^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(F)))

\displaystyle=R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}R\pi^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\pi^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F)))

\displaystyle=R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F)).

It is immediate to see that
the following sequence

(8.1.3)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\cal F}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}[-1]\overset{\theta_{\bullet}\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal F}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\longrightarrow{\cal F}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}\longrightarrow 0

is exact.
Here
\theta_{\bullet}:=g^{*}_{\bullet}(d\log t)\in\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
,
where
t
is a local section of
M_{S}
which gives the local generator
of
\overline{M}_{S}
. (Note again that
g^{*}_{\bullet}(d\log t)
is independent of the choice of
t
.)
In fact, the following sequence

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\cal F}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i-1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}\overset{\theta_{\bullet}\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal F}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\longrightarrow{\cal F}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}\longrightarrow 0

is locally split for each
i\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Hence we obtain the following exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S})[-1]\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S})\longrightarrow 0.

Applying
R\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}
to
this exact sequence and using (
8.1.2
),
we have the following triangle:

(8.1.4)

\displaystyle R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F))[-1]\overset{\theta\wedge}{\longrightarrow}R\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))\longrightarrow R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F))\overset{+1}{\longrightarrow}.

Let
Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
be another analogous simplicial immersion.
Then, by considering a refinement of two affine open coverings of
Y

and the fiber product,
we may assume that there exists the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}Y_{\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}.\end{CD}

Now it is a routine work to show that

R\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(L^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))

is independent of the choice of an affine open covering of
Y

and the immersion
Y_{i}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}_{i}
over

\overline{S}
.
∎

Definition 8.2
.

We call

R\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(L^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))

the
modified convergent complex
of
F
and we denote
it by

\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(F)
.
When
F={\cal K}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}
, we call
\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(F)

the
modfifed convergent complex
of
Y/\overset{\circ}{S}
and
denote it by
\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal K}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}})
.
We call
Ru^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\widetilde{R}\epsilon_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(F))

the
modified isozariskian complex
of
F
and
we denote it by
\widetilde{R}u^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(F)
.
When
F={\cal K}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}
, we call
\widetilde{R}u^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(F)

the
modfifed convergent-isozariskian complex
of
Y/\overset{\circ}{S}
and
denote it by
\widetilde{R}u^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal K}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}})
.

By (
8.1.4
) we obtain the following triangles:

(8.2.1)

\displaystyle R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F))[-1]\overset{\theta\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(F)\longrightarrow R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F))\overset{+1}{\longrightarrow},

(8.2.2)

\displaystyle Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F))[-1]\overset{\theta\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\widetilde{R}u^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(F)\longrightarrow Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F))\overset{+1}{\longrightarrow}.

Proposition 8.3
.

Let the notations be as in
(
2.0.4
)
.
Assume that
Y^{\prime}
and
Y
are log smooth over
S^{\prime}_{1}
and
S_{1}
, respectively.
For a morphism
g^{*}\colon g^{*}_{\rm conv}(F)\longrightarrow F^{\prime}

of
{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}}
-modules,
there exists a morphism

(8.3.1)

\displaystyle g^{*}\colon\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(F)\longrightarrow R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}*}(F)

such that

{\rm id}_{Y}^{*}={\rm id}_{\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(F)}
and

(g\circ h)^{*}=R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}(h^{*})\circ g^{*}
for an analogous morphism

h\colon Y^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow Y^{\prime}
over
S^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow S^{\prime}
to
g

and an analogus morphism
h^{*}\colon h^{*}_{\rm conv}(F^{\prime})\longrightarrow F^{\prime\prime}
of
{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime\prime}}
-modules
to
g^{*}\colon g^{*}_{\rm conv}(F)\longrightarrow F^{\prime}
of
{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}}
-modules.

Proof.

(The proof is not so standard.)
Let
Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow Y_{\bullet}
be a morphism of simplicial log schemes obtained by
affine open coverings of
Y^{\prime}
and
Y
in the beginning of this section.
Let
Y_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}
and

Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
be immersions into
formally log smooth log formal schemes
over
\overline{S}
and
\overline{S}{}^{\prime}
, respectively, which are constructed in the beginning of this section.
Set
\overline{\cal R}_{\bullet}:=\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\times_{S^{\prime}}(\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{S}}\overline{S}{}^{\prime})
.
By using the immersion
Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
,
the composite morphism

Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow Y_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}
and
the projection
\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{S}}\overline{S}{}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}
,
we have the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal R}_{\bullet}@>{}>{}>\overline{S}{}^{\prime}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
Y_{\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}@>{}>{}>\overline{S}.\end{CD}

Let

\displaystyle{\cal F}{}^{\prime\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal R}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal R}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}}:=\{{\cal F}{}^{\prime\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal R}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal R}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}.

be an analogous object to

{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
.
Then the commutative diagram above and
the morphism
g^{*}\colon g^{*}_{\rm conv}(F)\longrightarrow(F^{\prime})

induces the following morphism

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}_{\rm conv}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}}({\cal F}{}^{\prime\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal R}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal R}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}}).

This morphism induces the morphism (
8.3.1
).

It is a routine work to show that
the morphism (
8.3.1
) is well-defined
and that the relations in (
8.3
) hold.
∎

Let
X
be an SNCL scheme over
S_{1}
. Let
f\colon X\longrightarrow S_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S
be
the structural morphism. Let
X_{\bullet}
be the simplicial log scheme
obtained by an affine covering of
X
.
Let
X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
be an immersion into
a log smooth simplicial log
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme over
\overline{S}
.
Since
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}
is a simplcial strict semistable formal scheme over
\overset{\circ}{S}
,
the underlying formal scheme
\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}
is formally smooth
over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Set
{\cal P}_{\bullet}:=\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{S}}S
.
Let
E
be a flat isocrystal of
{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
-modules.
Let
\{(\overline{U}_{\bullet n},\overline{T}_{\bullet n})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be the system of
the universal enlargements of the immersion

X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S}
.
Set
(U_{\bullet n},T_{\bullet n}):=(\overline{U}_{\bullet n},\overline{T}_{\bullet n})\times_{\overline{S}}S
.
Let
(\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}_{n},\nabla)
be the corresponding integrable connection of
the
{\cal K}_{\overline{T}_{\bullet n}}
-module to
\pi^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}\epsilon^{*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)
:

\overline{\nabla}_{n}\colon\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}_{n}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{\cal P}\Omega^{1}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
.
Let
\beta_{\bullet n}\colon U_{\bullet n}\longrightarrow X_{\bullet}

be the natural morphism.
Set
{\cal E}^{\bullet}_{n}:=\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{S}}}{\cal O}_{S}
.
Let

\displaystyle{\nabla}_{n}\colon{\cal E}^{\bullet}_{n}\longrightarrow{\cal E}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

be the induced integrable connection by
\overline{\nabla}_{n}
obtained by using the relation
“
dt=t\log t
” in
\Omega^{1}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
as before.
We obtain the projective system
\{{\nabla}_{n}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
and
the following integrable connection

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{n}{\nabla}_{n}\colon\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{\bullet n*}{\cal E}^{\bullet}_{n}\longrightarrow(\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{\bullet n*}{\cal E}^{\bullet}_{n})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}/\overset{\circ}{S}}.

Here we have identified

({T_{\bullet n}})_{\rm zar}
with
({U_{\bullet n}})_{\rm zar}
.
For simplicity of notation, set

\displaystyle({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}},P):=(\{{\cal E}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\}_{n=0}^{\infty},\{\{{\cal E}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}).

This is indeed a filtered complex by (
6.3
) (1).
We also have the following filtered complex by (
6.3
) (2):

\displaystyle(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),P):=

\displaystyle(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\{{\cal E}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}),

\displaystyle\{L^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}(\{{\cal E}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\}_{n=0}^{\infty})\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}).

In order to prove (
8.5
) below, we need the following:

Lemma 8.4

(
cf.
[
Nakk3
, (4.14)]
)
.

Let
k
be a nonnegative integer. Then the following hold
:

(1)
For the morphism

g\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{n}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{n^{\prime}}

corresponding to a morphism
[n^{\prime}]\longrightarrow[n]
in
\Delta
,
the assumption in
(
6.1
)
is satisfied for
Y={\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{n}

and
Y^{\prime}={\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{n^{\prime}}
.
Consequently there exists a morphism

g^{(k)}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}_{n}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}_{n^{\prime}}

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})
fitting into
the commutative diagram
(
6.1.1
)

for
Y={\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{n}
and
Y^{\prime}={\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{n^{\prime}}
.

(2)

The family

\{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}_{n}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}

gives a simplicial scheme
\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}_{\bullet}

with natural morphism

b^{(k)}_{\bullet}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

of simplicial schemes.

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of
[
Nakk3
, (4.14)]
.
∎

Theorem 8.5
.

Let
\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

be the morphism of ringed topoi for
\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}
in

(
7.0.1
)
.
Then the filtered complex

R\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}((L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),P))\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

is independent of the choice of the open covering of
X
and the simplicial immersion

X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S}
.

Proof.

By (
8.1
),

R\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))=:\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))

is independent of the choice of the open covering of
X
.

Let
a_{\bullet}^{(k-1)}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k-1)}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}

be the natural morphism.
By (
6.5
) and the cohomological descent we obtain the following:

(8.5.1)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{0}^{P}R\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))=P_{0}R\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))

\displaystyle=R\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(a^{(0)}_{\bullet{\rm conv}*}(a^{(0)*}_{\bullet{\rm conv}}(E^{\bullet})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(0)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\longrightarrow a^{(1)}_{\bullet{\rm conv}*}(a^{(1)*}_{\bullet}{\rm conv}(E^{\bullet}))\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(1)}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\longrightarrow\cdots)

\displaystyle=(a^{(0)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(0)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(0)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\longrightarrow a^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(1)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(1)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\longrightarrow\cdots)

\displaystyle={\rm Ker}(a^{(0)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(0)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})\longrightarrow a^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(1)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))).

Let
k
be a positive integer.
Because the filtered direct image and the gr-functor commutes
(
[
NS1
, (1.3.4)]
), we have the following equalities by (
8.4
),
(
6.4.1
) and the convergent Poincaré lemma:

(8.5.2)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}R\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))

\displaystyle=R\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\rm gr}_{k}^{P}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))

\displaystyle=R\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(a^{(k-1)}_{\bullet{\rm conv}*}(a^{(k-1)*}_{\bullet{\rm conv}}(E)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(k-1)}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S})))[-k]

\displaystyle=a^{(k-1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(k-1)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(k-1)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))[-k].

Because the rest of the proof is a routine work, we leave the rest of the proof to the reader.
∎

Definition 8.6
.

We call

R\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}((L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),P))

the
modified
P
-filtered convergent complex
of
E
and we denote
it by

(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),P).

When
E={\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
, we call

(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),P)

the
modfifed
P
-filtered convergent complex
of
X/\overset{\circ}{S}
and
denote it by
(\widetilde{R}\epsilon_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal K}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}),P)
.
We call
Ru^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}((\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),P))

the
modified isozariskian
P
-filtered complex
of
E
and
we denote it by
(\widetilde{R}u^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),P)
.
When
E={\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
,
we call

Ru^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}((\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),P))

the
modfifed
P
-filtered log isozariskian complex
of
Y/\overset{\circ}{S}
and
denote it by
(\widetilde{R}u^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal K}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}}),P)
.

Set

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}^{k}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)):={\cal H}^{k}(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)))\quad(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

The following is a main result in this section:

Theorem 8.7

(
p
-adic semi-purity).

Let
k
be a nonnegative integer. Then

(8.7.1)

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}^{k}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a^{(k-1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(k-1)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))&(k>0),\\
{\rm Ker}(a^{(0)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(0)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\longrightarrow a^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(1)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})))&(k=0).\end{cases}

Proof.

The “increasing filtration”

\{P_{k}\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

on
\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))
gives
the following spectral sequence

(8.7.2)

E_{1}^{-k,q+k}={\cal H}^{q}({\rm gr}_{k}^{P}\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)))\Longrightarrow\widetilde{R}^{q}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(E).

By (
8.5.2
) and (
8.5.1
) we have the following equality:

(8.7.3)

\displaystyle{\cal H}^{q}({\rm gr}_{k}^{P}\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)))

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}{\cal H}^{q-k}(a^{(k-1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(k-1)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))&(k>0),\\
{\cal H}^{q}({\rm Ker}(a^{(0)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(0)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\longrightarrow a^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(1)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})))))&(k=0).\end{cases}

Hence
E_{1}^{-k,q+k}=0
for
q\not=k
.
Now we obtain (
8.7.1
) by the following equalities:

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}^{k}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))={\cal H}^{k}(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)))=E_{1}^{-k,2k}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a^{(k-1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(k-1)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))&(k>0),\\
{\rm Ker}(a^{(0)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(0)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\longrightarrow a^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(1)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})))&(k=0).\end{cases}

.

∎

Theorem 8.8

(
Comparison theorem).

Let
\tau
be the canonical filtration on a complex.
There exists a canonical isomorphism

(8.8.1)

(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),\tau)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),P).

Proof.

There exists the following natural morphism
of filtered complexes of
{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
-modules:

(8.8.2)

(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),\tau)\longrightarrow(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),P).

By
[
NS1
, (2.7.2)]
there exists a canonical morphism

(8.8.3)

(R{\pi}^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),\tau)\longrightarrow R{\pi}^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}((L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),\tau)).

Hence we obtain the following morphism

(8.8.4)

(R{\pi}^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),\tau)\longrightarrow(R{\pi}^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),P).

which is nothing but a morphism

(8.8.5)

(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),\tau)\longrightarrow(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),P).

To prove that this morphism is a
filtered isomorphism,
it suffices to prove that the induced morphism

(8.8.6)

{\rm gr}_{k}^{\tau}\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))\longrightarrow{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))\quad(k\in{\mathbb{Z}})

is an isomorphism.
By (
8.7.1
) we have the following equalities:

(8.8.7)

\displaystyle{\cal H}^{q}({\rm gr}_{k}^{\tau}\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)))

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a^{(k-1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(k-1)*}_{{\rm conv}*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))&(q=k>0),\\
{\rm Ker}(a^{(0)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(0)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\longrightarrow a^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(1)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})))&(q=k=0),\\
0&({\rm otherwise}).\end{cases}

By (
8.7.1
),

{\cal H}^{q}({\rm gr}_{k}^{P}\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)))

is also equal to the last formulas in (
8.8.7
).
Hence the morphism (
8.8.1
) is an isomorphism.

The rest we have to show is that
the morphism (
8.8.1
) is independent of
the choice of an affine open covering of
X
and
the simplicial immersion
X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
.
Because it is a routine work,
we leave the rest of the proof to the reader.
∎

Corollary 8.9

(
Contravariant functoriality of the modified
P
-filttered complex
)
.

Let the notations be as in
(
8.3
)
.
Let
X^{\prime}
be an SNCL scheme over
S^{\prime}_{1}
.
Let

(8.9.1)

\begin{CD}X^{\prime}@>{g}>{}>X\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S^{\prime}_{1}@>{u_{1}}>{}>S_{1}\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram of log schemes.
Let
E^{\prime}
be an isocrystal on

(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime})_{\rm conv}

and let

(8.9.2)

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}\colon\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}_{\rm crys}(E^{\prime})\longrightarrow E

be a morphism of isocrystals on
(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime})_{\rm conv}
.
Then there exists a morphism

(8.9.3)

\displaystyle g^{*}\colon(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}}(E^{\prime})),P)\longrightarrow R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}((\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)),P))

such that

{\rm id}_{X}^{*}={\rm id}_{\widetilde{R}\epsilon_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))}
and

(g\circ h)^{*}=R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}(h^{*})\circ g^{*}
for analogous morphisms

h\colon X^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow X^{\prime}
and
h^{*}\colon\overset{\circ}{h}{}^{*}_{\rm conv}(E^{\prime})\longrightarrow E^{\prime\prime}
of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}}
-modules.

Proof.

This follows from (
8.3
) and (
8.8
).
∎

Let
\pi^{\prime}
be another non-zero element of the maximal ideal of
{\cal V}
.
Assume that
\pi{\cal V}\subset\pi^{\prime}{\cal V}
.
Set
S^{\prime}_{1}:=\underline{\rm Spec}^{\log}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}/\pi^{\prime})
.
Then we have a natural closed immersion

S^{\prime}_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S_{1}
. Set
X^{\prime}:=X\times_{S_{1}}S^{\prime}_{1}
.
Let
i\colon X^{\prime}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}X
be the natural closed immersion.

Because
i_{{\rm conv}*}
is exact ((
2.1
) (1)),
we have the following functor:

(8.9.4)

i_{{\rm conv}*}\colon{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{X^{\prime}/S})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{X/S}).

Proposition 8.10
.

(8.10.1)

i_{{\rm conv}*}((\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal K}_{X^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),\tau))=(\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal K}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}),\tau).

Proof.

By (
2.1
) (3)
we have the following equalities:

\displaystyle i_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime(k)}}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

\displaystyle={\cal K}_{{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\quad(k\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Hence (
8.10
) follows from (
8.5.1
) and (
8.5.2
).
∎

9
Convergent Steenbrink filtered complexes

Let
{\cal V}
,
\pi
,
S
and
S_{1}
be as in the Introduction.
Let the notations be as in the previous section.
In this section we construct a filtered complex

(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}})
and
we calculate
{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)
((
1.7
)).

Let

(9.0.1)

\displaystyle\theta\colon\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))\longrightarrow\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))[1]

be a morphism of complexes induced
by
f^{*}(d\log t)\wedge
, where
t
is a local section of
M_{S}

whose image in
\overline{M}_{S}
is a local generator.
Note that
\theta
is indeed a morphism of complexes since
d\log t
is a one-form.
Set
H:=\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))
.
Strictly speaking, we have to take a flasque resolution
I^{\bullet\bullet}
of

L^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

and we have to set
H:=\pi^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(I^{\bullet\bullet})
.
Instead of considering (
9.0.1
), we consider the following morphism

(9.0.2)

\displaystyle\theta\colon H\longrightarrow H\{1\}.

Here
\{1\}
means the
1
-shift on the left without the change of the complex.
Note that
\theta
in (
9.0.2
) is not a morphism of complexes:
“
d\theta=-\theta d
” since
d\log t
is a one-form.
Consider the double complex

(9.0.3)

\begin{CD}\cdots\\
@A{\theta}A{}A\\
H/\tau_{j}[1]\{j\}\\
@A{\theta}A{}A\\
\cdots\\
@A{\theta}A{}A\\
H/\tau_{1}[1]\{1\}\\
@A{\theta}A{}A\\
H/\tau_{0}[1].\end{CD}

Here we mean the differential morphism of the double complex
by the morphism “
d+\partial
”, where the relation between the horizontal differential morphism
“
d
” and the vertical differential morphism “
\partial
”
is “
d\partial+\partial d=0
”.
Set

\displaystyle(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P):=(s(H/\tau_{0}[1]\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}H/\tau_{1}[1]\{1\}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}H/\tau_{j}[1]\{j\}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots),\{P_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}),

where

\displaystyle P_{k}A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E):=s(\cdots\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\tau_{\max\{2j+k+1,j\}}H/\tau_{j}[1]\{j\}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots)\quad(k\in{\mathbb{Z}},j\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Here we denote the double complex (
9.0.3
) as

H/\tau_{0}[1]\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}H/\tau_{1}[1]\{1\}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}H/\tau_{j}[1]\{j\}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots

for saving the space.
Usually,

P_{k}A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)
is defined to be

\displaystyle(\cdots\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\tau_{2j+k+1}H/\tau_{j}[j+1]\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots).

However this is mistaken since the inequality
2j+k+1\geq j
does not necessarily hold.

The following is a key lemma for
(
9.2
) below:

Lemma 9.1
.

(9.1.1)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}

\displaystyle a^{(2j+k)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(2j+k)*}_{{\rm conv}}(E)

\displaystyle\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))[-2j-k].

Proof.

We have the following equalities by (
8.7.1
):

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)

\displaystyle=(\cdots\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}^{P}_{2j+k+1}H[1]\{j\}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots)\quad(j\geq\max\{-k,0\})

\displaystyle=(\cdots\overset{0}{\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}^{P}_{2j+k+1}H[1]\{j\}\overset{0}{\longrightarrow}\cdots)\quad(j\geq\max\{-k,0\})

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}{\rm gr}^{P}_{2j+k+1}H[1]\{j\}\{-j\}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}a^{(2j+k)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(2j+k)*}_{{\rm conv}*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))[-2j-k].

∎

Theorem 9.2
.

There exists the following canonical isomorphism

(9.2.1)

\displaystyle\theta\colon R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E).

Proof.

We have the following equalities by (
8.8.1
):

\displaystyle A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)

\displaystyle=s(H/P_{0}[1]\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}H/P_{1}[1]\{1\}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}H/P_{j}[1]\{j\}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots)

\displaystyle=R{\pi}^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\cdots\overset{\theta_{\bullet}}{\longrightarrow}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})/P_{j}[1]\{j\}\overset{\theta_{\bullet}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots)

\displaystyle=R{\pi}^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\cdots\overset{\theta_{\bullet}}{\longrightarrow}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})/P_{j}[1]\{j\}\overset{\theta_{\bullet}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots).

It suffices to prove that the morphism

(9.2.2)

\displaystyle\theta_{\bullet}\colon L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S})\longrightarrow

\displaystyle(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})/P_{0}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}[1]\overset{\theta_{\bullet}}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})/P_{1}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}[1]\overset{\theta_{\bullet}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots)

is an isomorphism.
Here one must note that
the following diagram

\begin{CD}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S})@>{\theta_{\bullet}}>{}>(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})/P_{0}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\\
@V{\nabla^{i}}V{}V@V{}V{-\nabla^{i+1}}V\\
L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S})@>{\theta_{\bullet}}>{}>(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i+2}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})/P_{0}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\end{CD}

is commutative in order to confirm that the morphism
\theta_{\bullet}
in (
9.2.2
)
is a morphism of complexes.
Because
\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}_{0}
is affine,
the morphism
\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\overline{S}}
is affine.
Hence the morphism
\overset{\circ}{\cal P}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
is also affine.
By (
3.3
) and (
6.3
) (2),

L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})/P_{j}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

is isomorphic to

L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}/P_{j})
.

Now we may assume that there exists an exact immersion

X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}
into an affine log scheme
over
S_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{S}
.
Let the notations be as in the proofs of
(
6.3
) and (
6.5
).
Then we have only to prove that

\displaystyle\theta\colon p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n})\otimes_{q^{\prime}{}^{*}_{\!n}({\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T}})}q^{\prime}{}^{*}_{\!n}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T}/T^{\prime}})\longrightarrow

\displaystyle[(p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n})\otimes_{q^{\prime}{}^{*}_{\!n}({\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T}})}q^{\prime}{}^{*}_{\!n}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T}/T})/P_{0}[1]\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle(p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n})\otimes_{q^{\prime}{}^{*}_{\!n}({\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T}})}q^{\prime}{}^{*}_{\!n}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T}/T})/P_{1}[1]\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots].

is exact.
Since
{\cal E}^{\bullet}_{n}
is a flat
{\cal K}_{T_{n}}
-module and
q^{\prime}_{n}
is exact ((
3.2
)),
it suffices to prove that the morphism

\displaystyle\theta\colon\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T^{\prime}}/T^{\prime}}\longrightarrow[\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T}/T}/P_{0}[1]\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T}/T}/P_{1}[1]\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots]

is a quasi-isomorphism.
This is well-known.
(See
[
Mo
, 1.3.5]

and
[
Nakk4
, (1.3.21), (1.4.3)]
for the proof of this fact.)
∎

Corollary 9.3
.

There exists the following spectral sequences
:

(9.3.1)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}R^{q-2j-k}\overset{\circ}{f}{}^{(2j+k)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(2j+k)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow R^{q}\overset{\circ}{f}_{{\rm conv}*}(R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E))),

(9.3.2)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}R^{q-2j-k}\overset{\circ}{f}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(a^{(2j+k)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})))

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow R^{q}f_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E)).

Definition 9.4
.

Set
(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,E),P):=Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}((A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P))
.
We call
(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
and
(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,E),P)

the
weight-filtered convergent complex
of
E
and
the
weight-filtered isozariskian complex
of
E
, respectively.
When
E={\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
and
\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}
is proper,
we call
(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
and
(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,E),P)

the
weight-filtered convergent complex
of
X/S
and
the
weight-filtered convergent isozariskian complex
of
X/S
, respectively.
When
E={\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
,
we call the spectral sequences (
9.3.1
) and
(
9.3.2
)
the
convergent weight spectral sequence
of
X/S
and
the
weight spectral sequence
of
X/S
, respectively.

By (
9.2.1
) we can denote the filtered complexes

(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
and
(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,E),P)

by

(9.4.1)

\displaystyle(R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E)),P)

and

(9.4.2)

\displaystyle(Ru^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E)),P),

respectively.
We can also denote the filtered complexes

R\overset{\circ}{f}_{{\rm conv}*}((A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P))

and
Rf_{X/S*}((A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,E),P))

by

(9.4.3)

\displaystyle(Rf_{{\rm conv}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E)),P)

and

(9.4.4)

\displaystyle(Rf_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E)),P),

respectively.

Remark 9.5
.

Let
l
be a prime to
p
. Let
X_{\rm et}
and
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\rm et}

be the Kummer log étale topos of
X
and the étale topos of
\overset{\circ}{X}
,
respectively. Let
\epsilon_{X/S_{1}}\colon X\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}
be the morphism
forgetting the log structure of
X
.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
is the spectrum of a field
\kappa
of
characteristic
p\geq 0
.
In this case

R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}({\cal K}_{X/S})
is a
p
-adic analogue of

R\epsilon_{X/S_{1},{\rm et}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l})\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
in
[
Nak
, p. 173]
.

10
Convergent Rapoport-Zink-Steenbrink-Zucker filtered complexes

The results in this section are complements of those in the previous section.
In this section we construct another weight-filtered convergent complex
in a manner which is analogous to
[
RZ
]
and
[
SZ
]
,
which we call a convergent Rapoport-Zink-Steenbrink-Zucker filtered complex.
See also
[
Nak
]
and
[
FN
]
.

First let us recall the Hirsch extension (cf.
[
Nakk5
]
).

Let the notations be as in the previous section.
Let
\widetilde{t}
be a local section of
M_{S}

whose image
t
in
\overline{M}_{S}:=M_{S}/{\cal O}_{S}^{*}
is the local generator.
Let
U_{S}
be a free
{\cal O}_{S}
-module of rank
1
with a basis
u
:

U_{S}={\cal O}_{S}u
.
Though we do not ask what
u
is, we consider
u
as
“
\log t
” (not “
\log\widetilde{t}
”) in our mind.
For a local section
\widetilde{t}{}^{\prime}=a\widetilde{t}

(a\in{\cal O}_{S}^{*})
of
M_{S}
,
we do not change
U_{S}
.
Let
\varphi\colon U_{S}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
be the following composite morphism

\displaystyle f^{-1}(U_{S})\longrightarrow f^{-1}(\Omega^{1}_{S/\overset{\circ}{S}})\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{2}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

of
f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})
-modules, where
the first morphism
is defined by
u\longmapsto d\log t:=d\log\widetilde{t}
.
Let
\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S}}(U_{S})
be a locally free PD-algebra over
{\cal O}_{S}
generated by
U_{S}
.
Consider the Hirsch extension

\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S}}(U_{S})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{S}}{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

of the complex

{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
by
\varphi
;
the differential is defined by the following:

d(u^{[n]}\omega)=u^{[n-1]}d\log t\wedge\nabla(\omega)+u^{[n]}\nabla(\omega)\quad(\omega\in{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}},n\in{\mathbb{N}}).

We denote this complex by

{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}[U_{S}]
.
(We do not need to mind what
U_{S}
is in this paper. If you want to know the definition of
U_{S}
,
see
[
Nakk5
]
for the definition of
U_{S}
.)
Set

\displaystyle N_{\bullet}:=d/du\colon{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}[U_{S}]\longrightarrow{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}[U_{S}]\quad(d/du(u^{[n]}):=u^{[n-1]}).

Since
N_{\bullet}
is surjective, we have the following exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\longrightarrow{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}[U_{S}]\overset{N_{\bullet}}{\longrightarrow}{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}[U_{S}]\longrightarrow 0\quad(i\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Because this sequence is locally split,
the following sequence is exact:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}[U_{S}])

\displaystyle\overset{N_{\bullet}}{\longrightarrow}L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}[U_{S}])\longrightarrow 0.

Hence we have the following canonical isomorphism

(10.0.1)

\displaystyle\iota\colon L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm MF}(N_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}[U_{S}])\oplus L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}[U_{S}])[-1].

Consider the following morphism

(10.0.2)

\displaystyle\psi\colon{\rm MF}(N_{\bullet})\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

defined by the following

(\sum_{n=0}u^{n}\omega_{n},\sum_{n=0}u^{n}\eta_{n})\longmapsto\omega_{0}+d\log t\wedge\eta_{0}.

As in
[
Nakk2
, (3.1)]
we can check that this morphism is a morphism of complexes.

Proposition 10.1
.

The morphism
\psi
is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

Since
\iota
is a quasi-isomorphism and

\psi\circ\iota
is the identity,
\psi
is a quasi-isomorphism.
∎

Corollary 10.2
.

The morphism
\psi
induces the following quasi-isomorphism

(10.2.1)

\displaystyle\psi\colon({\rm MF}(N_{\bullet}),\tau)\longrightarrow(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),\tau),

which we denote by
\psi
again.

Now consider the morphism

\displaystyle\theta\colon{\rm MF}(N_{\bullet})\longrightarrow{\rm MF}(N_{\bullet})[1]

defined by
“
(x,y)\longmapsto(0,x)
”.
Set

\displaystyle{\rm MF}(N):=R\pi^{\rm coinv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\rm MF}(N_{\bullet}))

and

\displaystyle A^{N}_{\rm conv}(X/S,E):=s({\rm MF}(N)/\tau_{0}[1]\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}{\rm MF}(N)/\tau_{1}[1]\{1\}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots)

and

\displaystyle(A^{N}_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P):=(A^{N}_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),\{(\cdots\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\tau_{\max\{2j+k+1,j\}}{\rm MF}(N)/\tau_{j}[1]\{j\}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}).

Proposition 10.3
.

The morphism
\psi
induces the following canonical filtered isomorphism

(10.3.1)

\displaystyle(A^{N}_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P),

which we denote by
\psi
again.

Proof.

By the definition of
\psi
, It is obvious that
the following diagram

\begin{CD}{\rm MF}(N_{\bullet})@>{\psi}>{}>L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\\
@A{\theta}A{}A@A{}A{\theta}A\\
{\rm MF}(N_{\bullet})@>{\psi}>{}>L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\end{CD}

is commutative.
Because
\psi
is an isomorphism, the morphism (
10.3.1
) is an isomorphism.
The rest we have to prove is that the morphism is independent of the choices of
the affine open covering of
X_{\bullet}
and
the embedding system
X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S}
.
Because this is a routine work, we leave the detail of the proof to the reader.
∎

Definition 10.4
.

We call
(A^{N}_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
the

convergent Rapoport-Zink-Steenbrink-Zucker filtered complex

of
E
. When
E={\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
,
we call
(A^{N}_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
the

convergent Rapoport-Zink-Steenbrink-Zucker filtered complex
of
X/S
.

Remark 10.5
.

(1) Let the notations be as in (
9.5
).
Set
X_{l^{-n}}:=X\times_{{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[{\mathbb{N}}])}{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[l^{-n}{\mathbb{N}}])
, where
the log structure of
{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[l^{-n}{\mathbb{N}}])

is associated to the inclusion

l^{-n}{\mathbb{N}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{Z}}[l^{-n}{\mathbb{N}}]
.
As in
[
Nak
, p. 723]
, set
X_{{\rm t}l}:=\varprojlim_{n}X_{l^{-n}}
.
Let
\pi\colon X_{{\rm t}l}:\longrightarrow X_{\rm et}
be the natural morphism.
Then

R\pi^{\rm coinv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(L^{\rm UE}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}[U_{S}]))
is
a
p
-adic analogue of

R(\epsilon_{X/S_{1},{\rm et}}\pi)_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
.
Consequently

(A^{N}_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
is a
p
-adic analogue of the

(s(A^{\bullet\bullet}),W)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\rm et}})
,
though the filtered derived category

{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\rm et}})

does not appear in [loc. cit.] nor
[
RZ
]
.
Here
A^{\bullet\bullet}
is the double complex in [loc. cit] with the shift of the complex
as in §
9
.
(It is easy to prove that
(s(A^{\bullet\bullet}),W)
is indeed independent of the choice of
the injective resolution
({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\rm et}}\longrightarrow I^{\bullet}

(n\in{\mathbb{N}})
by using the
l
-adic analogue of
(
9.1
) and (
9.2
).)

11
The functoriality of weight-filtered convergent and isozariskian complexes

Let the notations be as in (
8.9
).
In this section we prove the contravariant functoriality of

(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)
,
(A^{N}_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)

and
(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}(X/S,E),P)
by using (
8.8
).
This theme has not been discussed in a lot of references except

[
Nakk1
]
,
[
Nakk4
]
and
[
Nakk5
]
(cf.
[
T
]
,
[
Ma
]
);
even the pull-back of the abrelative
and the absolute Frobenius on
p
-adic Steenbrink complexes
has not been discussed except the references above;
the action (
11.1.3
) below dividing the “degree mapping”
has not been considered in a lot of references.
Consequently the yoga of arithmetic weights cannot be used except them.

Theorem 11.1

(
Contravariant Functoriality).

Let the notations be as in
(
8.9
)
.
Then there exists a canonical morphism

(11.1.1)

g^{*}_{{\rm conv}}\colon(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)\longrightarrow R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}((A_{\rm conv}(X^{\prime}/S^{\prime},E^{\prime}),P))

fitting into the following commutative diagram

(11.1.2)

\begin{CD}A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)@>{g^{*}_{{\rm conv}}}>{}>R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}(A_{\rm conv}(X^{\prime}/S^{\prime},E^{\prime}))\\
@A{\theta}A{\simeq}A@A{\simeq}A{R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}(\theta^{\prime})}A\\
R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E))@>{g^{*}_{{\rm conv}}}>{}>R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E^{\prime})).\end{CD}

The morphisms
g^{*}_{{\rm conv}}
’s satisfy the transitive law and

(({\rm id}_{X/S})_{\rm conv})^{*}={\rm id}_{(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)}
.

Proof.

Set
H:=\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))

and
H^{\prime}:=\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}}(E^{\prime}))
.
Let
\theta^{\prime}\colon H^{\prime}\longrightarrow H^{\prime}[1]
be an analogous morphism to
\theta
.
By (
8.9
) and
[
NS1
, (2.7.2)]
there exists the following composite morphism

\displaystyle g^{*}\colon(H,\tau)\longrightarrow(R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}(H^{\prime}),\tau)\longrightarrow R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}((H^{\prime},\tau)).

Recall the morphism
u\colon S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S
in (
2.0.4
).
Because
u^{*}\circ\theta=(\deg u)\theta^{\prime}
by the definition of
{\rm deg}(u)

(
[
Nakk4
, (1.1.42)]
),
we have the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}H[1]\{j\}/\tau_{j+1}@>{\deg(u)^{-1}g^{*}}>{}>R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}(H^{\prime}[1]\{j\}/\tau_{j+1})\\
@A{\theta}A{}A@A{}A{R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}(\theta^{\prime})}A\\
H[1]\{j-1\}/\tau_{j}@>{g^{*}}>{}>R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}(H^{\prime}[1]\{j-1\}/\tau_{j})\end{CD}

for
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Hence the morphisms

(11.1.3)

\displaystyle g_{j}:=(\deg u)^{-(j+1)}g^{*}\colon H[1]\{j-1\}/\tau_{j}\longrightarrow R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}(H^{\prime}[1]\{j-1\}/\tau_{j})

for
j
’s induce
the following morphism

\displaystyle g^{*}_{{\rm conv}}\colon A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)\longrightarrow R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}(A_{\rm conv}(X^{\prime}/S^{\prime},E^{\prime})).

In fact, this morphism induces the morphism (
11.1.1
).
The commutativity of the diagram (
11.1.2
)
follows from that of the following commutative diagram

(11.1.4)

\begin{CD}H/\tau_{0}[1]@>{g^{*}_{{\rm conv}}}>{}>R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}(H/\tau_{0}[1])\\
@A{\theta^{\prime}}A{\simeq}A@A{\simeq}A{R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}(\theta)}A\\
R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E))@>{g^{*}_{{\rm conv}}}>{}>R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}(E)),\end{CD}

which can be shown by the local calculation as usual.
∎

Corollary 11.2
.

Let
E^{\rm conv}_{\rm ss}(X/S,E)
and
E_{\rm ss}(X/S,E)

be the spectral sequences
(
9.3.1
)

and
(
9.3.2
)
for
X/S
and
E
, respectively.
Let
E^{\rm conv}_{\rm ss}(X^{\prime}/S^{\prime},E^{\prime})
and
E_{\rm ss}(X^{\prime}/S^{\prime},E^{\prime})

be the spectral sequences
(
9.3.1
)

and
(
9.3.2
)
for
X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}
and
E^{\prime}
.
Then the morphism
g^{*}_{\rm conv}

induces a morphism

(11.2.1)

g^{*}_{\rm conv}\colon u^{-1}E^{\rm conv}_{\rm ss}(X^{\prime}/S^{\prime},E^{\prime})\longrightarrow E^{\rm conv}_{\rm ss}(X/S,E)

and

(11.2.2)

g^{*}_{\rm conv}\colon u^{-1}E_{\rm ss}(X^{\prime}/S^{\prime},E^{\prime})\longrightarrow E_{\rm ss}(X/S,E)

of spectral sequences.

Proof.

The proof is straightforward.
∎

Let

a^{\prime}{}^{(k)}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}{}^{(k)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}

be the natural morphism.
By (
11.1
) the morphism

g^{*}_{\rm conv}

induces the following morphism

(11.2.3)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}(g^{*}_{\rm conv})\colon a^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{(k)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}/\overset{\circ}{S}))[-k]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R\overset{\circ}{g}_{{\rm conv}*}a^{\prime}{}^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{\prime}{}^{(k)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))[-k].

Assume that
g
induces a morphism

g_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}{}^{(k)}
for any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
In this case we make the morphism

{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}(g^{*}_{\rm conv})
in
(
11.2.3
) explicit under the following assumptions.
Assume that the following two conditions hold:

(11.2.4): there exists the same cardinality of
smooth components of
\overset{\circ}{X}
and
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}
over

S_{1}
and
S^{\prime}_{1}
, respectively:

\overset{\circ}{X}=\bigcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}
,

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}=\bigcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
,
where
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}
and
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
are
smooth components of

\overset{\circ}{X}/S_{1}
and
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}_{1}
, respectively.

(11.2.5):
there exist positive integers
e_{\lambda}
and local generators

x_{\lambda}
and
x^{\prime}_{\lambda}
of
M_{X}
and
M_{X^{\prime}}

defining the equations of
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}
and
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}_{\lambda}

(\lambda\in\Lambda)
such that
g^{*}(x_{\lambda})=(x^{\prime}_{\lambda})^{e_{\lambda}}
in
\overline{M}_{X}=M_{X}/{\cal O}_{X}^{*}
.

Set
\underline{\lambda}:=\{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{k}\}

(\lambda_{j}\in\Lambda,~(\lambda_{i}\not=\lambda_{j}~(i\not=j)))

and

\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}:=\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{k}}
,

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}_{\underline{\lambda}}:=\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}_{\lambda_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}_{\lambda_{k}}
.
Let
a_{\underline{\lambda}}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}
and

a^{\prime}_{\underline{\lambda}}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}_{\underline{\lambda}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}

be the natural exact closed immersions.
Consider the following
direct factor of the morphism (
11.2.3
):

(11.2.6)

\displaystyle g^{*}_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}\colon a_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}*}(a^{*}_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/{\overset{\circ}{S}})[-k]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow Rg_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}*}a^{\prime}{}_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}*}(a^{\prime}{}^{*}_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(E^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}))[-k],

where
\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}/{\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}})
and

\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})
are
the convergent orientation sheaves
associated to the sets
\{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}_{\lambda_{j}}\}_{j=1}^{k}

and
\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{j}}\}_{j=1}^{k}
, respectively,
defined as in
[
NS1
, p. 81]
.

Proposition 11.3
.

Let the notations
and the assumptions be as above.
Let

\overset{\circ}{g}_{\underline{\lambda}}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}_{\underline{\lambda}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}

be the induced morphism by
g
and
let

(11.3.1)

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}_{\underline{\lambda}}\colon\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}_{\underline{\lambda},{\rm crys}}(a^{*}_{\underline{\lambda}}(E))\longrightarrow a^{\prime}{}^{*}_{\underline{\lambda}}(E^{\prime})

be the induced morphism of isocrystals on

(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}_{\lambda}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime})_{\rm conv}
by

(
11.3.1
)
.
Then the morphism

g^{*}_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}
in

(
11.2.6
)
is equal to
(\prod_{j=1}^{k}e_{\lambda_{j}})(a^{\prime}_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}*}\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}_{\underline{\lambda},{\rm conv}})
for

k\geq 0
. Here we define

\prod_{j=1}^{k}e_{\lambda_{j}}
as
1
for
k=0
.

Proof.

One has only to imitate the proof of

[
NS1
, (2.9.3)]
and make the convergent linearization of it.
∎

In
[
Nakk4
, (1.5.7)]
we have proved the following:

Proposition 11.4
.

Let the assumptions and the notations be as above.
Set
\Lambda(x):=\{\lambda\in\Lambda~|~x\in\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}
.
Then
\deg(u)_{x}=e_{\lambda}
for
\lambda\in\Lambda(x)
.
In particular,

e({\lambda})
’s are independent of
the choice of an element of
\Lambda(x)
.

Definition 11.5
.

(1) We call

\{e_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{>0})^{\Lambda}

the
multi-degree
of
g

with respect to the decompositions of

\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda}

and
\{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda}
of
\overset{\circ}{X}
and
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}
, respectively.
We denote it by

{\rm deg}_{\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{X}^{\prime}}(g)\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{>0})^{\Lambda}
.
If
e_{\lambda}
’s for all

\lambda
’s
are equal,
we also denote

e_{\lambda}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

by

{\rm deg}_{\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}}(g)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
.

(2) Assume that

e_{\lambda}
’s for all
\lambda
’s are equal.
Let
v\colon{\cal E}\longrightarrow{\cal F}

be a morphism of

{\cal K}_{S}
-modules.
Let
k
be a nonnegative integer.
The
D
-
twist
by
k

v(-k)\colon{\cal E}(-k;u)\longrightarrow{\cal F}(-k;u)

of
v
with respect to
u

is, by definition, the morphism

{\rm deg}(u)^{k}v\colon{\cal E}\longrightarrow{\cal F}
.

Corollary 11.6
.

Set
f^{\rm conv}_{X/S}:=f\circ u^{\rm conv}_{X/S}
.
Assume that
e_{\lambda}
’s for
all
\lambda
’s are equal.
Let
E_{\rm ss}(X/S,E)
be
the following spectral sequence

(11.6.1)

E_{1}^{-k,q+k}=\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}R^{q-2j-k}f^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(a^{(2j+k)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k)}_{{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))(-j-k,u)

\Longrightarrow R^{q}f^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E))

and let
E_{\rm ss}(X^{\prime}/S^{\prime},E^{\prime})
be the obvious
analogue of the above for
X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}
.
Then there exists a morphism

(11.6.2)

g^{*}_{\rm conv}\colon u^{-1}E_{\rm ss}(X^{\prime}/S^{\prime},E^{\prime})\longrightarrow E_{\rm ss}(X/S,E)

of spectral sequences.

Proof.

(
11.6
) immediately follows
from (
11.3
).
∎

Let
F_{S_{1}}\colon S_{1}\longrightarrow S_{1}
be
the absolute Frobenius endomorphism of
S_{1}
.
Set

X^{[p]}:=X\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{1},F_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}}}\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
.
The abrelative Frobenius morphism

F\colon X\longrightarrow X^{[p]}
over
F_{S_{1}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}}\colon S_{1}\longrightarrow S_{1}^{[p]}:=S_{1}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{1},F_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}}}\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
induces
the usual relative Frobenius morphism

\overset{\circ}{F}{}^{(k)}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}\longrightarrow(X^{[p]})^{\circ,(k)}
.
It is easy to see that

(X^{[p]})^{\circ,(k)}=\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{1},F_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}}}\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}=:\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)^{\prime}}
.
We also set
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}:=\overset{\circ}{X}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{1},F_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}}}\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
.
Let
a^{(k)}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}

and

a^{(k)^{\prime}}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}

be the natural morphisms.
We define the
relative Frobenius action

\Phi_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\colon a^{(k)^{\prime}}_{{\rm conv}*}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S})\longrightarrow F_{{\rm conv}*}a^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}\varpi^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})

as the identity
under the natural identification

\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}F^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}\varpi^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}).

Let
S_{1}^{[p]}(S)
be a fine log formal scheme
whose underlying formal scheme is
\overset{\circ}{S}
and the log structure of

S_{1}^{[p]}(S)
is the sub-log structure of
S

such that the isomorphism

M_{S}/{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}M_{S_{1}}/{\cal O}_{S_{1}}^{*}
induces
the following isomorphism

\displaystyle M_{S_{1}^{[p]}(S)}/{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm Im}(F_{S_{1}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}}^{*}\colon F_{S_{1}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}}^{*}(M_{S_{1}^{[p]}})\longrightarrow M_{S_{1}})/{\cal O}_{S_{1}}^{*}.

Then we have an obvious morphism
S\longrightarrow S_{1}^{[p]}(S)
of log formal schemes.
When
g
in (
8.9.1
)
is equal to the relative Frobenius
F\colon X\longrightarrow X^{[p]}
over
S\longrightarrow S_{1}^{[p]}(S)
,
we denote (
9.3.2
)+(the compatibility with Frobenius)
by the following:

(11.6.3)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}(X/S)=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}R^{q-2j-k}f^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(a^{(2j+k)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k)}_{{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})))(-j-k)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow{}

\displaystyle R^{q}f^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E)).

Definition 11.7
.

In the case where
E
is the trivial coefficient and
\overset{\circ}{X}
is proper over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
,
we call the sequence (
11.6.3
)
the
weight spectral sequence of

X/S
(more precisely over
(S_{1},S)
).

12
Edge morphisms

In this section we define the cycle class
in a convergent cohomology sheaf for a smooth divisor on a smooth scheme
(cf.
[
Fa
, §2]
,
[
NS1
, (2.8)]
).
Then, as in
[
NS1
, (10.1)]
, we give a
description of the edge morphism between
the
E_{1}
-terms of the spectral sequence
(
11.6.3
). Because the proof is the same as that of

[
Nakk1
, (10.1)]
,
we omit the proofs in this section.

Let
S
and
S_{1}
be as in §
6
.
Let
g\colon Z\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
be a smooth scheme over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
.
By abuse of notation,
we also denote by
g
the composite
morphism
Z\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Let
D
be a smooth divisor on
Z
.
Let
F
be an isocrystal in
(Z/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm conv}
.
Let

a\colon D\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}Z
be
the natural closed immersion over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
.
Let

a_{\rm zar}\colon D_{\rm zar}\longrightarrow Z_{\rm zar}

be the induced morphism of Zariski topoi.
Let
a_{\rm conv}\colon(({D/\overset{\circ}{S}})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{D/\overset{\circ}{S}})\longrightarrow(({Z/\overset{\circ}{S}})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Z/\overset{\circ}{S}})

be also the induced morphism of convergent ringed topoi.

Let
Z_{\bullet}
be the smooth simplicial scheme over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
obtained by
an affine covering of
Z
.
Let
(Z_{\bullet},D_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}_{\bullet}

be a simplicial immersion into a log smooth simplicial scheme over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Let
{\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}
be the log structure of
{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}
.
Let
b_{\bullet}\colon D^{(1)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}){\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

be the natural simplicial morphism.
Let
({\cal F}^{\bullet},\nabla):=\{({\cal F}^{\bullet}_{n},\nabla)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

be an integrable connection obtained by
F
.
By using the Poincaré residue isomorphism
with respect to
D^{(1)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})
,
we have the following exact sequence:

(12.0.1)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega_{\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}^{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}^{\bullet}

\displaystyle\overset{\rm Res}{\longrightarrow}b_{{\bullet}*}(b^{*}_{{\bullet}}({\cal F}^{\bullet})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{D^{(1)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})}}\Omega_{D^{(1)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})/\overset{\circ}{S}}^{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{\rm zar}(D^{(1)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})/\overset{\circ}{S}))[-1]\longrightarrow 0.

Let
L^{\rm UE}_{Z_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

(resp.
L^{\rm UE}_{D^{(1)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
)
be the convergent linearization functor
with respect to the simplicial immersion

Z_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

(resp.
D_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}D^{(1)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}
)).
By (
3.10
),

L^{\rm UE}_{Z_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}b_{\bullet{\rm zar}*}=a_{{\rm conv}\bullet*}L^{\rm UE}_{D^{(1)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
.
Hence we have the following exact
sequence by (
6.4
) and (
6.3
) (2):

(12.0.2)

0\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{Z_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega_{\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{Z_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}^{\bullet})

\longrightarrow a_{{\rm conv}\bullet*}(L^{\rm UE}_{D_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}(b^{*}_{{\bullet}}({\cal F}^{\bullet})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{D^{(1)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})}}\Omega_{D^{(1)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})/\overset{\circ}{S}}^{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}}(D_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}))[-1])\longrightarrow 0.

Recall the morphisms

\pi^{\rm conv}_{Z/\overset{\circ}{S}}
and
\pi^{\rm conv}_{D/\overset{\circ}{S}}

of ringed topoi in §
7
.
By applying
R{\pi}^{\rm conv}_{Z/\overset{\circ}{S}*}
to (
12.0.2
),
we obtain the following triangle:

(12.0.3)

\longrightarrow R{\pi}^{\rm conv}_{Z/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{Z_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega_{\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow R{\pi}^{\rm conv}_{Z/\overset{\circ}{S}*}L^{\rm UE}_{Z_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow

a_{{\rm conv}*}R{\pi}_{D/\overset{\circ}{S}{\rm conv}*}(L^{\rm UE}_{D^{(1)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}(b^{*}_{{\bullet}}({\cal F}^{\bullet})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{D^{(1)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{D^{(1)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})/\overset{\circ}{S}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}}(D_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}))[-1]\overset{+1}{\longrightarrow}\cdots.

By (
3.5
), (
4.7
) and
by the cohomological descent,
we have the following triangle:

(12.0.4)

\longrightarrow F\longrightarrow R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(Z,D)/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{(Z,D)/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F))\longrightarrow a_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{*}_{\rm conv}(F)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S}))[-1]\overset{+1}{\longrightarrow}\cdots.

Hence we have the boundary morphism

(12.0.5)

d\colon a_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{*}_{\rm conv}(F)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S}))[-1]\longrightarrow F[1]

in
D^{+}({\cal K}_{Z/S})
and the following morphism

(12.0.6)

d\colon a_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{*}_{\rm conv}(F)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\longrightarrow F[2]

in
D^{+}({\cal K}_{Z/\overset{\circ}{S}})
.
Set

(12.0.7)

G^{\rm conv}_{D/Z,F}:=-d.

and call
G^{\rm conv}_{D/Z}
the

log convergent Gysin morphism
of
D
.
Then we have the following cohomology class

(12.0.8)

\displaystyle c^{\rm conv}_{Z/\overset{\circ}{S}}(D,F):=G^{\rm conv}_{D/Z,F}\in{\cal E}{\it xt}^{0}_{{\cal K}_{Z/\overset{\circ}{S}}}(

\displaystyle a_{{\rm conv}*}(a^{*}_{\rm conv}(F)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S})),F[2]).

It is a routine work to prove that
G^{\rm conv}_{D/Z,F}

(and
c^{\rm conv}_{Z/\overset{\circ}{S},F}(D,F)
) depends only
on
Z/\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Since
\varpi^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}}(D/\overset{\circ}{S})

is canonically isomorphic to
{\mathbb{Z}}
and since
there exists a natural morphism

F\longrightarrow a_{{\rm conv}*}a^{*}_{\rm conv}(F)
,
we have a cohomology class

\displaystyle c^{\rm conv}_{Z/\overset{\circ}{S}}(D,F)

\displaystyle\in{\cal E}{\it xt}^{0}_{{\cal K}_{Z/\overset{\circ}{S}}}({\cal K}_{Z/\overset{\circ}{S}},{\cal K}_{Z/\overset{\circ}{S}}[2])=:\underline{\cal H}_{{\log}{\textrm{-}}{\rm conv}}^{2}(Z/\overset{\circ}{S}).

Next we give the expression of the edge morphism
d_{1}^{\bullet\bullet}
of
(
11.6.3
) by the Čech-Gysin morphism.

First, fix a decomposition

\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
of
\overset{\circ}{X}

by smooth components of
\overset{\circ}{X}
over
S_{1}
and fix a total order on
\Lambda
.
For
\underline{\lambda}:=\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{k-1}\}

(\lambda_{0}<\cdots<\lambda_{k-1})
,

\underline{\lambda}_{j}:=\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\widehat{\lambda}_{j},\ldots,\lambda_{k-1}\}
, set
