\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}:=\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{0}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{k-1}}

and
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}:=\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{0}}\cap\cdots\widehat{\overset{\circ}{X}_{{\lambda}_{j}}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{k-1}}
for
k\geq 2
.
Here
~\widehat{}~
means the elimination.
Then
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}

is a smooth divisor on
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}
over
S_{1}
.
Let

\iota^{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}_{\underline{\lambda}}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}

be the closed immersion.
As in
[
NS1
, p. 81]
,
we have the following orientation sheaves

\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}):=\varpi_{\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{k-1}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})

and

\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}):=\varpi_{\lambda_{0}\cdots\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\cdots\lambda_{k-1}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})

associated to the sets
\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{k}

and
\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{k}\setminus\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{j}}\}
, respectively.
We denote by
(\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{k-1})

the local section giving a basis of

\varpi_{\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{k-1}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})
;
we also denote by
(\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{k-1})

the local section giving a basis of

\varpi_{\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{k-1}{\rm zar}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/S)
.
Let
a_{\underline{\lambda}}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}
be the natural closed immersion.
By (
12.0.7
) we have a morphism

(12.0.9)

\displaystyle G^{{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}{\rm conv}}_{\underline{\lambda}}:=G^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}},a_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}^{*}(E)}\colon

\displaystyle\iota^{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}*}(a_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}^{*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\lambda_{j}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\longrightarrow a_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}^{*}(E)[2].

We fix an isomorphism

(12.0.10)

\varpi_{\lambda_{j}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})

by the following morphism

(\lambda_{j})\otimes(\lambda_{0}\cdots\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\cdots\lambda_{k-1})\longmapsto(-1)^{j}(\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{k-1}).

We identify

\varpi_{\lambda_{j}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})
with

\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})

by this isomorphism.
We also have the following composite morphism

(12.0.11)

(-1)^{j}G^{{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}{\rm conv}}_{\underline{\lambda}}\colon\iota^{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}*}(a_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}^{*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\iota^{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}*}(a_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}^{*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\lambda_{j}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\overset{G^{{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}{\rm conv}}_{\underline{\lambda}}\otimes 1}{\longrightarrow}

a_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\rm conv}}^{*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})[2]

defined by

(12.0.12)

{\rm``}x\otimes(\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{k-1})\longmapsto(-1)^{j}G^{{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}{\rm conv}}_{\underline{\lambda}}(x)\otimes(\lambda_{0}\cdots\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\cdots\lambda_{k-1}){\rm"}.

The morphism (
12.0.12
) induces
the following morphism
of log convergent cohomologies:

(12.0.13)

(-1)^{j}G^{{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}{\rm conv}}_{\underline{\lambda}}\colon R^{q-k}f^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(a_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}^{*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\longrightarrow

R^{q-k+2}f^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(a_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\rm conv}}^{*}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})).

Set

(12.0.14)

\displaystyle G:=\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}\sum_{\{\underline{\lambda}:=\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{2j+k}\}~|~\lambda_{\gamma}<\lambda_{\alpha}\;(\gamma<\alpha)\}}\sum_{\beta=0}^{2j+k}(-1)^{\beta}G_{\underline{\lambda}}^{\underline{\lambda}_{{\beta}}}\colon

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}R^{q-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(a^{(2j+k)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))

\displaystyle(-j-k,u)\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k+1,0\}}R^{q-2j-k+2}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k-1)}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(a^{(2j+k-1)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k-1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))

\displaystyle(-j-k+1,u).

Let
\iota_{\underline{\lambda}}^{\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}
be
the natural immersion.
The morphism
\iota_{\underline{\lambda}}^{\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}

induces the morphism

(12.0.15)

(-1)^{\beta}\iota_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm crys}}^{\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}*}\colon\iota_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm crys}}^{\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}*}(a^{*}_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\rm conv}}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}{\rm crys}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{T}))\longrightarrow a^{*}_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(E)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}).

Set

(12.0.16)

\displaystyle\rho:=\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}\sum_{\{\underline{\lambda}:=\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,<\lambda_{2j+k}\}~|~\lambda_{\gamma}<\lambda_{\alpha}\;(\gamma<\alpha)\}}\sum_{\beta=0}^{2j+k}(-1)^{\beta}\iota_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}^{\underline{\lambda}_{\beta*}}\colon

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}R^{q-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(E^{m}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))

\displaystyle(-j-k,u)\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}R^{q-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k+1)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E^{m}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k+1)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k+1)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))

\displaystyle(-j-k,u).

Proposition 12.1
.

The edge morphism between the
E_{1}
-terms of
the spectral sequence
(
11.6.3
)

is given by the following diagram
:

(12.1.1)

\begin{split}{}&\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}R^{q-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(a^{(2j+k)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\\
{}&\phantom{R^{q-2m-k}f_{(\overset{\circ}{X}^{(k)},Z|_{\overset{\circ}{X}^{(k+m)}})/S*}({\cal O}\quad\quad}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))(-j-k,u).\end{split}

\text{\scriptsize{${G+\rho}$}}~\downarrow\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad

\begin{split}{}&\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k+1,0\}}R^{q-2j-k+2}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k-1)}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(a^{(2j+k-1)*}_{\rm conv}(E)\\
{}&\phantom{R^{q-k}f_{(\overset{\circ}{X}^{(k)},Z|_{\overset{\circ}{X}^{(k)}})/S*}({\cal O}\quad\quad}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k-1)}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))(-j-k+1,u).\end{split}

Proof.

This proposition follows from
[
Nakk3
, (2.2.12), (2.2.13)]

and the proof of
[
Nakk1
, (10.1)]
.
∎

13
Monodromy operators

In this section we define the
p
-adic monodromy operator on the log convergent cohomologcal complex
of a log smooth scheme. We also define the
p
-adic quasi-monodromy operator
on the log convergent Steenbrink complex
(A_{\rm conv},P)
of an SNCL scheme.
We prove that they are equal.

Let the notations be as in §
8
and §
9
.
Let us recall the following triangle (
8.2.1
):

(13.0.1)

\displaystyle R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F))[-1]\overset{\theta\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\widetilde{R}\epsilon_{Y/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(F)\longrightarrow R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F))\overset{+1}{\longrightarrow}.

Hence we obtain the following boundary morphism

(13.0.2)

\displaystyle d\colon R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F))\longrightarrow R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F)).

Definition 13.1
.

We call the boundary morphism above
the
convergent monodromy operator
of
F

and we denote it by

(13.1.1)

\displaystyle N_{\rm conv}\colon R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F))\longrightarrow R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F)).

In the case where
F
is trivial, we call
N_{\rm conv}

the
convergent monodromy operator
of
Y/S
.

Remark 13.2
.

We can also construct the following morphism

(13.2.1)

\displaystyle\nabla\colon Rf^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F))\longrightarrow Rf^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F))\otimes_{{\cal O}_{S}}\Omega^{1}_{S/\overset{\circ}{S}}

by using the “convergent Gauss-Manin connection” for
the composite morphism
Y\longrightarrow S\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
if
we prove the base change formula for the log convergent cohomologies.
(The base change formula will be proved in later sections.)
By using the following residue morphism

(13.2.2)

\displaystyle{\rm Res}\colon\Omega^{1}_{S/\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{S},

we obtain the following composite morphism

(13.2.3)

\displaystyle N\colon Rf^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F))\overset{\nabla}{\longrightarrow}Rf^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F))\otimes_{{\cal O}_{S}}\Omega^{1}_{S/\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\rm Res}{\longrightarrow}R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S/\overset{\circ}{S}}(F)).

Though we can also prove that this
N
is equal to the induced morphism by

N_{\rm conv}
, we do not discuss this in this paper.

Next we define the
p
-adic convergent quasi-monodromy operator and
we formulate the variational
p
-adic monodromy-weight conjecture ((
13.5
))
and the variational filtered log
p
-adic hard Lefschetz conjecture ((
13.6
)).
The former is a generalization of the
p
-adic monodromy-weight conjecture in
[
Mo
]
as in
[
Nakk4
]
.

Set

\displaystyle A^{\bullet\bullet}:=H/\tau_{0}[1]\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}H/\tau_{1}[1]\{1\}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}H/\tau_{j}[1]\{j\}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots

and

\displaystyle P_{k}A^{\bullet\bullet}:=(\cdots\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\tau_{\max\{2j+k+1,j\}}H/\tau_{j}[1]\{j\}\overset{\theta}{\longrightarrow}\cdots)\quad(k\in{\mathbb{Z}},j\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Consider the following morphism

(13.2.4)

\displaystyle\nu^{ij}:={\rm proj}\colon{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i+j+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}/P_{j+1}\longrightarrow{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i+j+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}/P_{j+2}.

This morphism induces the morphism

\nu^{ij}:={\rm proj}.\colon A^{ij}\longrightarrow A^{i-1,j+1}.

Set

(13.2.5)

\displaystyle\nu:=s(\oplus_{i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\nu^{ij}).

Let

(13.2.6)

\nu\colon(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)\longrightarrow(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P\langle-2\rangle)

be a morphism of complexes induced by

\{\nu^{ij}\}_{i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}}
.
Here
\langle-2\rangle
means the (-2)-shift of the filtration:

P\langle-2\rangle_{k}A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E):=P_{k-2}A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)
.
We also have the morphism

\theta\colon A^{ij}\longrightarrow A^{i,j+1}.

Let
g\colon X\longrightarrow X
be a morphism fitting into the following commutative diagram

(13.2.7)

\begin{CD}X^{\prime}@>{g^{\prime}}>{}>X^{\prime\prime}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
X@>{g}>{}>X\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{1}@>{}>{}>S_{1}\\
@V{\bigcap}V{}V@V{}V{\bigcap}V\\
S@>{u}>{}>S,\end{CD}

where
X^{\prime\prime}

is another disjoint union
of the member of an affine open covering of
X
.
Since the following diagram

(13.2.8)

\begin{CD}A^{ij}@>{{\rm proj}.}>{}>A^{i-1,j+1}\\
@V{g^{*ij}}V{}V@V{}V{\deg(u)g^{*i-1,j+1}}V\\
g_{*}(A^{ij})@>{{\rm proj}.}>{}>g_{*}(A^{i-1,j+1})\end{CD}

is commutative, the morphism (
13.2.6
) is
the following morphism

(13.2.9)

\nu\colon(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P)\longrightarrow(A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E),P\langle-2\rangle)(-1,u).

Set

(13.2.10)

B^{ij}:=A^{i-1,j}(-1,u)\oplus A^{ij}\quad(i,j\in{\mathbb{N}})

and

(13.2.11)

B^{ij}:=A^{i-1,j}(-1,u)\oplus A^{ij}\quad(i,j\in{\mathbb{N}})

(cf.
[
St
, p. 246]
).
The horizontal differential morphism

d^{\prime}\colon B^{ij}\longrightarrow B^{i+1,j}

is, by definition, the induced morphism by a morphism

d^{\prime}\colon B^{ij}\longrightarrow B^{i+1,j}

defined by the following formula:

(13.2.12)

\displaystyle d^{\prime}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})=(\nabla\omega_{1},-\nabla\omega_{2})

and the vertical one

d^{\prime\prime}\colon B^{ij}\longrightarrow B^{i,j+1}
is the induced morphism
by a morphism
d^{\prime\prime}\colon B^{ij}\longrightarrow B^{i,j+1}

defined by the following formula:

(13.2.13)

\displaystyle d^{\prime\prime}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})=(-\theta\wedge\omega_{1}+\nu(\omega_{2}),\theta\wedge\omega_{2}).

It is easy to check that

B^{\bullet\bullet}
is actually a double complex.
Let
B
be the single complex of
B^{\bullet\bullet}
.

Let

(13.2.14)

\displaystyle\mu_{{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}}\colon\widetilde{R}u_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E))\longrightarrow B

be a morphism
of complexes induced by the following morphisms

\displaystyle\mu^{i}_{\bullet}\colon{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\longrightarrow

\displaystyle~{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}/P_{0}\oplus{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}/P_{0}\quad(i\in{\mathbb{N}})

defined by the following formula

\displaystyle\mu^{i}_{\bullet}(\omega):=(\omega~{\rm mod}~P_{0},\theta\wedge\omega~{\rm mod}~P_{0})\quad(\omega\in{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}).

Then we have the following
morphism of triangles:

(13.2.15)

\begin{CD}@>{}>{}>A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)[-1]@>{}>{}>B\\
@A{(\theta_{X/S}\wedge*)[-1]}A{}A@A{}A{\mu_{{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}}}A\\
@>{}>{}>R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E))[-1]@>{\theta\wedge}>{}>\widetilde{R}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E))\end{CD}

\begin{CD}@>{}>{}>A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)@>{+1}>{}>\\
@A{\theta_{X/S}\wedge}A{}A\\
@>{}>{}>R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E))@>{+1}>{}>.\\
\end{CD}

Hence we obtain the following as in
[
St
, p. 246]

and
[
Nakk1
, (11.10)]
:

Proposition 13.3
.

The zariskian monodromy operator

(13.3.1)

\displaystyle N\colon

\displaystyle R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X/S}(E))\longrightarrow R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X/S}(E))(-1,u)

is equal to

\nu\colon A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)\longrightarrow A_{\rm conv}(X/S,E)(-1,u)

via the isomorphism
(
9.2.1
)
.

Corollary 13.4
.

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}
is quasi-compact.
Then the zariskian monodromy operator

(13.4.1)

\displaystyle N\colon

\displaystyle R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X/S}(E))\longrightarrow R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X/S}(E))(-1,u)

is nilpotent.

Proof.

(
13.4
) immediately follows from
(
13.3
) since
\nu
is nilpotent.
∎

Next we give two conjectures as in
[
Nakk4
]
.

Conjecture 13.5

(
Variational convergent monodromy-weight conjecture).

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
is projective.
Let
k
and
q
be nonnegative integers.
Then we conjecture that the induced morphism

(13.5.1)

N^{k}\colon{\rm gr}^{P}_{q+k}R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal K}_{X/S})\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{P}_{q-k}R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal K}_{X/S})(-k,u)

by the monodromy operator is an isomorphism.

Let the assumption be as in (
13.5
).
Assume that the relative dimension of
\overset{\circ}{X}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
is of pure dimension
d
.
Let
L
be a relatively ample line bundle on

\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
.
Let
{\cal J}_{X/S}
be the “defining ideal sheaf” in
(X/S)_{\rm conv}
:

\Gamma((U,T),{\cal J}_{X/S}):={\rm Ker}({\cal K}_{T}\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{U})
for
an object
(U,T)\in{\rm Conv}(X/S)
.
Let
i\colon X_{\rm zar}\longrightarrow(X/S)_{\rm conv}

be the morphism of topoi defined by

i_{*}(F)((U,T)):=F(U)
for an object
(U,T)
in
{\rm Conv}(X/S)
.
Then we have the following exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow 1+{\cal J}_{X/S}\longrightarrow{\cal O}^{*}_{X/S}\longrightarrow i_{*}({\cal O}_{X}^{*})\longrightarrow 0.

Hence we have the following boundary morphism

\displaystyle R^{1}f_{*}({\cal O}^{*}_{X})\longrightarrow R^{2}f_{X/S*}(1+{\cal J}_{X/S})(1).

Composing the induced morphism

\displaystyle R^{2}f_{X/S*}(1+{\cal J}_{X/S})(1)\longrightarrow R^{2}f_{X/S*}({\cal K}_{X/S})(1)

by the following logarithm morphism

\displaystyle\log\colon 1+{\cal J}_{X/S}\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{X/S},

we obtain the following composite morphism (cf.
[
BO2
, §3]
):

\displaystyle c_{1,{\rm conv}}\colon R^{1}f_{*}({\cal O}^{*}_{X})\longrightarrow R^{2}f_{X/S*}(1+{\cal J}_{X/S})(1)\longrightarrow R^{2}f_{X/S*}({\cal K}_{X/S})(1).

Here note that

\log(1+a):=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}a^{n}/n
is
defined for a local section
a
of
{\cal J}_{X/S}
since the topology of

T
is
p
-adic for an enlargement
(U,T)
of
X/S
.

Set
\eta=c_{1,{\rm conv}}(L)\in R^{2}f_{X/S*}({\cal K}_{X/S})(1)
.
The variational filtered log
p
-adic hard Lefschetz conjecture
is the following:

Conjecture 13.6

(
Variational filtered
convergent log hard Lefschetz conjecture).

Let
L
be a relatively ample line bundle on
X/S_{1}
. Set
\eta:=c_{1,{\rm conv}}(L)
.
Then we conjecture the following
:

(1)
The following cup product

(13.6.1)

\eta^{i}\colon R^{d-i}f_{X/S*}({\cal K}_{X/S})\longrightarrow R^{d+i}f_{X/S*}({\cal K}_{X/S})(i)

is an isomorphism.

(2)

In fact,
\eta^{i}
is the following isomorphism of filtered sheaves:

(13.6.2)

\eta^{i}\colon(R^{d-i}f_{X/S*}({\cal K}_{X/S}),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(R^{d+i}f_{X/S*}({\cal K}_{X/S})(i),P).

Here
(i)
means that

P_{k}(R^{d+i}f_{X/S*}({\cal K}_{X/S})(i))=P_{k+2i}R^{d+i}f_{X/S*}({\cal K}_{X/S}).

In the next section we give results for these conjectures after giving a comparison theorem
on the relative log convergent cohomology and the relative log crystalline cohomology of
X/S
.

14
Comparison theorem

In this section we compare
(A_{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar},P)

with
(A_{\rm zar},P)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{L}{\mathbb{Q}}

in the case where
\pi{\cal V}
has a PD-strucutre
\gamma
.
(In the case of the trivial coefficient, it is not necessary to assume that

\pi{\cal V}
has a PD-strucutre
\gamma
.)

Let
{\cal V}
,
\pi
,
S
and
S_{1}

be as in §
2
.
Assume that
\pi{\cal V}
has a PD-strucutre
\gamma
.
Because we have assumed that
S
is flat over
{\cal V}
,

\gamma
extends to
S
.
By abuse of notation we denote this extended PD-structure by
\gamma
again.
Let
f\colon Y\longrightarrow S_{1}
be a fine log scheme over
S_{1}
.
Then we have the log crystalline topos

({Y/S})_{\rm crys}
of
Y/(S,\pi{\cal O}_{S},\gamma)
.
Let

u^{\rm crys}_{Y/S}\colon(({Y/S})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{Y/S})\longrightarrow(Y_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))

be the canonical projection.
To prove the comparison theorem, we recall the following,
which is a special case of
[
NS2
, (11.4)]
and a relative version of

[
O2
, Theorem 7.7]
and
[
Sh2
, Theorem 3.1.1]
:

Theorem 14.1

(
Comparison theorem).

Let
Y
be a log smooth log scheme over
S_{1}
.
Denote by

(14.1.1)

\Xi\colon{\rm Isoc}_{\rm conv}(Y/S)(:={\rm Isoc}_{\rm conv,zar}(Y/S))\longrightarrow{\rm Isoc}_{\rm crys}(Y/S)

the relative version of
the functor defined in
[
Sh1
, Theorem 5.3.1]

and denoted by
\Phi
in
[
loc. cit.
]
.
Let
E
be an object of

{\rm Isoc}_{\rm conv}(Y/S)
.
Then there exists a functorial isomorphism

(14.1.2)

Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(E)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}Ru^{\rm crys}_{Y/S*}(\Xi(E)).

Let
X
be an SNCL scheme over
S_{1}
.
Let
f\colon X\longrightarrow S
be the structural morphism.
To give the comparison theorem, we have to give a generalization of

(A_{\rm zar}(X/S,F),P)

for a more generalized sheaf than a quasi-coherent crystal
F
on the crystalline site

{\rm Crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})
defined in
[
Nakk4
]
.
Let
X_{\bullet}
be a simplicial log scheme obtained by an affine open covering of

X
and a simplicial immersion

X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
into a formally log smooth log formal scheme
over
\overline{S}
. Let
\overline{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}
be the log PD-envelope of
this immersion over
(\overset{\circ}{S},p{\cal O}_{S},[~])
.
Set
{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}:=\overline{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{S}}S
.
Assume that, we are given a
a flat quasi-coherent
{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}
-module
(
{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}:={\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
)
with integrable connection

({\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}),\nabla)
:

\nabla\colon{\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet})\longrightarrow{\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}.

We endow

{\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

with the tensor product
P
of the trivial filtration on

{\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet})
and
the (pre)weight filtration on
\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
.
Then, as in
[
Nakk4
]
, we have the filtered double complex

(A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S,{\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}))^{\bullet\bullet},P)
as follows:

(14.1.3)

\displaystyle A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S,{\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}))^{ij}

\displaystyle:={\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i+j+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}/P_{j}

\displaystyle:={\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i+j+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}/P_{j}({\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i+j+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})\quad(i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}).

We consider the following boundary morphisms of
double complexes:

(14.1.4)

\begin{CD}A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S,{\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}))^{i,j+1}\\
@A{\theta\wedge}A{}A\\
A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S,{\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}))^{ij}@>{-\nabla}>{}>A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S,{\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}))^{i+1,j}.\\
\end{CD}

The sheaf

A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S,{\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}))^{ij}

has a quotient filtration
P
obtained by the filtration
P
on

{\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i+j+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
:

(14.1.5)

P_{k}A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S,{\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}))^{ij}:=(P_{2j+k+1}+P_{j})({\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i+j+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}})/P_{j}.

Let
(A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S,{\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet})),P)
be the single filtered complex
associated to this double complex.
We assume that the following three conditions (A), (B), (C) hold:

(A) We assume that, for another simplicial immersion

X^{\prime}_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
and
for the following commutative diagram

(14.1.6)

\begin{CD}X_{\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{g_{\bullet}}V\\
X^{\prime}_{\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet},\end{CD}

there exists a morphism

(14.1.7)

\rho_{g_{\bullet}}\colon{\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}_{\bullet})\longrightarrow g_{\bullet*}({\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}))

of
{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}_{\bullet}}
-modules
or
{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}_{\bullet}}
-modules
satisfying the usual cocycle condition
for the following commutative diagram:

(14.1.8)

\begin{CD}X_{\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
X^{\prime}_{\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
X^{\prime\prime}_{\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}{}^{\prime\prime}_{\bullet}.\end{CD}

Here
{\cal P}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
is the analogue of

{\cal P}_{\bullet}
for the immersion

X^{\prime}_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}

and

{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
is the analogue of

{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}
for the immersion

X^{\prime}_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
.

(B) We assume that the following morphism

\displaystyle R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S,{\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}_{\bullet}))\longrightarrow R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S,{\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}))

induced by the morphisms

g_{\bullet}\colon{\cal P}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\cal P}^{\prime}_{\bullet}

and
\rho_{g_{\bullet}}
is an isomorphism
in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T}))
.

(C) We assume that, for a positive integer
k
,
for a nonnegative integer
n
,
the following morphism

(14.1.9)

{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}({\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}_{n})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}_{\!\!n}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}_{\!\!n}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}Rg_{n*}({\rm gr}^{P}_{k}({\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{n})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}/\overset{\circ}{S}}))

induced by the morphisms

g_{\bullet}\colon{\cal P}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\cal P}^{\prime}_{\bullet}

and
\rho_{g_{\bullet}}
is an isomorphism in
D^{+}(f^{-1}_{n}({\cal O}_{T}))
.

Then, by the same standard proof as that of the well-definedness of

(A_{\rm zar}(X/S,F),P)
in
[
Nakk4
]
,
we can prove that the filtered complex

R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}((A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S,{\cal E}({\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet})),P))

is a well-defined filtered complex.

Now let

E
be an object of
{\rm Isoc}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})
.
Let
(\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet},\overline{\nabla})=\{(\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}_{n},\overline{\nabla})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

(\overline{\nabla}\colon\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}_{n+1}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
)
be the integrable connection obtained by

\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}(E)
and the simplicial immersion

X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
.
By the universality of the log enlargement
\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{X_{\bullet},n}(\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
,
there exists an
n(\bullet)
(depending on
\bullet

such that the morphism
\overline{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}

factors through a morphism

\overline{\lambda}{}_{n(\bullet)}\colon\overline{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{X_{\bullet},n}(\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet})
.
Hence we obtain the following integrable connection:

\displaystyle\overline{\lambda}{}^{*}_{n(\bullet)+1}(\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}_{n(\bullet)+1})\longrightarrow\overline{\lambda}{}^{*}_{n}(\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}_{n(\bullet)})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}.

Since
\overline{\lambda}{}^{*}_{n}(\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}_{n(\bullet)})
is independent of for large
n(\bullet)
’s,
we set
\overline{\lambda}{}^{*}(\overline{\cal E}):=\overline{\lambda}{}^{*}_{n(\bullet)}(\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}_{n(\bullet)})

for a large
n(\bullet)
.
Then we have the following integrable connection:

\displaystyle\overline{\lambda}{}^{*}(\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow\overline{\lambda}{}^{*}(\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}.

The morphism
\overline{\lambda}_{n(\bullet)}\colon\overline{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}

induces a morphism

\lambda_{n(\bullet)}\colon{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{X_{\bullet},n(\bullet)}({\cal P}_{\bullet})
.
Set
{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}_{n(\bullet)}:=\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}_{n(\bullet)}\otimes_{{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{X_{\bullet},n(\bullet)}(\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet})}}{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{X_{\bullet},n(\bullet)}({\cal P}_{\bullet})}
and

{\lambda}{}^{*}({\cal E}):={\lambda}{}^{*}_{n(\bullet)}(\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}_{n(\bullet)})
for a large
n(\bullet)
.
Then we have the following integrable connection:

(14.1.10)

\displaystyle\nabla\colon\lambda^{*}({\cal E}{}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow\lambda^{*}({\cal E}{}^{\bullet})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}.

Lemma 14.2
.

The connection
(
14.1.10
)
satisfies the three conditions
(A), (B), (C)
.

Proof.

The condition (A) hold since it holds for the connection

\displaystyle\nabla\colon{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}.

By (
14.1.2
) and (
3.6.1
),
we obtain the following commutative diagram

(14.2.1)

\begin{CD}R\pi^{\rm zar}_{X/S*}(\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n*}({\cal E}^{\bullet}_{n}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}))@>{}>{}>R\pi^{\rm zar}_{X/S*}(\lambda^{*}({\cal E}^{\bullet}){\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S})\\
\Big\|\Big\|\\
Ru^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E))@>{\sim}>{}>Ru^{\rm crys}_{X/S*}(\Xi(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E))).\end{CD}

By the same proof as that of (
9.2.1
), we also have the following commutative diagram:

(14.2.2)

\begin{CD}A_{{\rm iso}{\textrm{-}}{\rm zar}}(X/S,E)@>{}>{}>R\pi^{\rm zar}_{X/S*}(A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S,\lambda^{*}({\cal E}^{\bullet})))\\
@A{\theta\wedge}A{}A@A{}A{\theta\wedge}A\\
R\pi^{\rm zar}_{X/S*}(\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n*}({\cal E}^{\bullet}_{n}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}))@>{\sim}>{}>R\pi^{\rm zar}_{X/S*}(\lambda^{*}({\cal E}^{\bullet}){\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}).\end{CD}

Hence the condition (B) holds.

We also have the following commutative diagram by using the Poincaré residue isomorphism:

(14.2.3)

\begin{CD}{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}A_{{\rm iso}{\textrm{-}}{\rm zar}}(X/S,E)=\\
@V{}V{}V\\
R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\rm gr}_{k}^{P}A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S,\lambda^{*}({\cal E}^{\bullet})))@>{\sim}>{}>\end{CD}

\begin{CD}\underset{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}{\bigoplus}R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n*}({\cal E}^{\bullet}_{n}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(2j+k)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(2j+k)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S})))[-2j-k])\\
@V{}V{}V\\
\underset{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}{\bigoplus}R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(\lambda^{*}({\cal E}^{\bullet}){\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(2j+k)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(2j+k)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{S}))[-2j-k]).\end{CD}

Because
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}
is smooth,
we can define the following functor by (
14.1.1
):

\Xi^{(k)}\colon{\rm Isoc}_{\rm conv}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S})\longrightarrow{\rm Isoc}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}).

Because the right hand sides of (
14.2.3
)
are isomorphic to

\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}a^{(2j+k)}_{*}(Ru^{\rm conv}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(a^{(2j+k)*}_{{\rm conv}}(E))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})))

and

\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}a^{(2j+k)}_{*}(Ru^{\rm crys}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\Xi^{(2j+k)}(a^{(2j+k)*}_{{\rm conv}}(E))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))),

respectively,
the right vertical morphism is an isomorphism
by the trivial log version of (
14.1
).
Hence the condition (C) holds.
∎

Definition 14.3
.

Let
L
be a crystal on
{\rm Crys}(X/S)

such that
K\otimes_{\cal V}L=\Xi(\epsilon^{*{\rm conv}}_{X/S}(E))
.
We set

\displaystyle(A_{{\rm iso{\textrm{-}}zar}}(X/S,\overset{\circ}{L}),P):=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}((A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S,\lambda^{*}({\cal E})),P)).

Here note that we do not define
\overset{\circ}{L}
.

Corollary 14.4
.

There exists the following spectral sequence

(14.4.1)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}R^{q-2j-k}f^{\rm crys}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}(\Xi^{(2j+k)}(a^{(2j+k)*}_{{\rm conv}}(E))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))(-j-k)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow R^{q}f^{\rm crys}_{X/S*}(\epsilon_{X/S}^{{\rm crys}*}(\Xi(E)))

Proof.

We have already proved this in the proof of (
14.2
).
∎

Corollary 14.5
.

The spectral sequences
(\ref{ali:wtfapwt})
and

(\ref{ali:sxa})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

are canonically isomorphic.

Theorem 14.6

(
Comparison theorem of
p
-adic isozariskian
and zariskian Steenbrink complexes).

(
1.9
)
holds.

Proof.

We have already proved (
14.6
)
in the proof of (
14.2
).
∎

Proposition 14.7

(
Base change theorem).

Let
{\cal V}^{\prime}
,
K^{\prime}
,
\pi^{\prime}
,
S^{\prime}
and
S_{1}^{\prime}

be as in
§
11
.
Let

(14.7.1)

\begin{CD}S^{\prime}@>{u}>{}>S\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\rm Spf}({\cal V}^{\prime})@>{}>{}>{\rm Spf}({\cal V})\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram of
p
-adic formal families of log points.
Let

\begin{CD}X^{\prime}@>{g}>{}>X\\
@V{f^{\prime}}V{}V@V{}V{f}V\\
S^{\prime}_{1}@>{u}>{}>S_{1}\end{CD}

be a cartesian diagram of SNCL schemes.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{f}
is quasi-compact and quasi-separated.
Then the base change morphism

(14.7.2)

{\cal K}_{S^{\prime}}\otimes^{L}_{{\cal K}_{S}}(Rf^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(g^{*}_{\rm conv}(E))),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(Rf^{\rm conv}_{X^{\prime}/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}(E)),P)

is an isomorphism in the filtered derived category

{\rm DF}({\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

This follows from (
1.9.3
) and the log crystalline base change theorem
and the classical crystalline base change theorem and
the proof of (
14.2
) and the descending induction on the number of the filtration
P
.
∎

Remark 14.8
.

By (
8.10
) we can replace
p
by
\pi
for the case where
E
is trivial.

Henceforth we consider the case of the trivial coefficient.
Using (
1.9.3
) for the trivial coefficient, we obtain the following:

Corollary 14.9
.

(1)
If the crystalline analogue of
(
13.5
)
holds,
then
(
13.5
)
holds.

(2)
If the crystalline analogue of
(
13.6
)
holds,
then
(
13.6
)
holds.

Remark 14.10
.

As is well-known, the crystalline analogues of (
13.5
) and (
13.6
)
and in some cases. See
[
Nakk4
]
and the references in [loc. cit.].

Theorem 14.11

(
E_{2}
-degeneration).

The spectral sequence
(\ref{ali:wtfapwt})
degenerates at
E_{2}
if
\overset{\circ}{X}
is proper over

\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
and if
E
is the trivial coefficient.

Proof.

In
[
Nakk3
]
, by the same proof as that of

[
NS1
, (2.17.2)]
,
we have proved the
E_{2}
-degeneration of

(\ref{ali:ixsxa})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

when
\overset{\circ}{X}
is proper over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
.
Hence (
14.11
) follows from (
1.9.3
) for the trivial coefficient.
∎

Let us recall the notation (
9.4.2
).
Set

\displaystyle(Rf_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E)),P):=Rf_{*}(Ru^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{X/S}(E)),P).

Theorem 14.12

(
Strict compatibility).

Let
f\colon X\longrightarrow S_{1}
and
f^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\longrightarrow S_{1}
be proper SNCL schemes over
S_{1}
.
Let
g\colon X^{\prime}\longrightarrow X
be a morphism of simplicial log
schemes over
S_{1}
.
Let
q
be an integer.
Then the induced morphism

(14.12.1)

g^{*}\colon R^{q}f^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}({\cal K}_{X/S})\longrightarrow R^{q}f^{\rm conv}_{X^{\prime}/S*}({\cal K}_{X^{\prime}/S})

is strictly compatible with the weight filtration.

Proof.

In
[
Nakk4
]
we have proved the strict compatibility of
g^{*}

with respect to the weight filtration in the case of
log crystalline cohomologies.
Now (
14.12
) follows from the comparison theorem
(
14.1
).
∎
